# STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md — Stage P-bis Design Freeze + Findings Log

**Stage:** P-bis — Open-Issue Close-Out + Testnet Go-Live + Frontend.
**Status:** Entry freeze. Locked 2026-07-11 (PB-D1—PB-D5; PB-D1/PB-D2 user-adjudicated). Branch `stage-p-bis` (cut from `main` @ `f72b309` = the CM-series tip) per PB-D3 (base amended same-day from `b5f4b8b`). Execution truth until superseded by explicit amend. No code lands until this freeze locks (§12 ambiguity-gate).
**Canonical spec:** stage scope + workstream roster + gate in `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` § "Stage P-bis" (the 2026-07-11 restructure record); testnet strategy `FINDINGS.md` OQ-15 / OQ-16; frontend OQ-18 (separate repo `aumm-app`); audit firm OQ-17 (deferred to Stage Q); constitution §xxix (`10_constitution.md` L203).
**Related:** `STAGE_P_NOTES.md` P-D1 (network-agnostic deploy scripts), P-D9 (hevm/Act), P-D12 (F-14 rails), P-D23 / P-D26 (the original P-bis bundle), P-D40 (F-20 `swapFeeManager`), P-D42 (coverage); `CLAUDE.md` §11 (the live carry-forward list); `docs/white_hat/AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md` (the F-13 / F-15 Accepted-risk rows).

**Framing.** Stage P-bis exists so the Stage-Q external audit receives a frozen, self-audited candidate running live on a public testnet with the `aumm-app` frontend connected — the audit becomes a verification event, not a discovery event. Three sequenced workstreams (PB-D4): **(A)** open-issue close-out (every live `CLAUDE.md` §11 carry-forward plus the ex-Stage-Q queued inputs), **(B)** Sepolia deployment (stubs + Router binds + the live broadcast), **(C)** the frontend. `stage-p-bis-complete` is the audit-candidate freeze.

---

## PB-D1 — Sepolia is the live-broadcast target (closes the P-D1 live-target deferral; user-adjudicated 2026-07-11)

The load-bearing criterion is 12-second block parity: every protocol constant is block-denominated (OQ-3 / OQ-4 / OQ-5 — `BLOCKS_PER_DAY = 7_200` assumes 12s slots), so any ~2s-block L2 testnet would distort every time-gated behavior (halving eras, epochs, veto windows, qualification cliffs) by ~6×, restricting the field to Ethereum L1 testnets. Sepolia: 12s slots, the standard application testnet (mature faucet / Etherscan / wallet tooling), canonical Permit2 deployed, Balancer V3 reference deployments present (orientation only — Aureum deploys its own Vault + Router per P-D26). Hoodi rejected (validator/staking-focused Holesky successor; thinner application tooling). Tenderly-only rejected (private — fails the public live-frontend goal; it remains the private mainnet-fork complement per the Stage-P/Q notes). Execution hedge: testnet infra states drift, so workstream B opens with a verify pass (faucet availability, Permit2 presence at the canonical address, explorer contract-verification flow) before any broadcast sub-step is drafted.

## PB-D2 — §xxix multisig dissolution closes via the wiring step — no constitutional amendment (user-adjudicated 2026-07-11)

The K-D9 retained slice splits into two classes. (i) Re-settable: emission-layer governance (`EmissionDistributor` / `BodenseeBootstrapChannel` / `TVLOracle` / `EfficiencyOracle`), `VaultClassRegistry` governance, `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` donate authority — all carry mechanically-ready, re-settable `setGovernanceContract` rotations (per the P9.5 authority-lifetime review). Deliverable: a governance-handoff rotation script firing these rotations at the K-handoff point (mirroring K's `setAuthorizer` migration; joins the deploy orchestration), plus a fork-test witness leg on the orchestrator-deployed stack (the P-D36 roster pattern). (ii) Immutable: the Miliarium `pauseManager` role accounts are write-once at `registerPool` and cannot rotate — but the role is NON-exclusive (`_ensureAuthenticatedByRole`, `VaultAdmin.sol` L787-793, falls through to the authorizer, per the F-20 record), so `AureumGovernance` holds pause via `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer` regardless; post-dissolution the role slots become inert pointers at the dead Safe — harmless by construction. The F-20 / P-D40 `swapFeeManager` sub-slice is already resolved (Fix A `address(0)` — authority follows the migrating authorizer). Net: §xxix stands unamended; dissolution = fire the rotations + dissolve the Safe after the OQ-10 window; no permanent operational slice.

## PB-D3 — Branch + close mechanics mirror P-D4

Fresh branch `stage-p-bis` cut from `main` @ `f72b309` — base amended same-day (user-adjudicated 2026-07-11) from `b5f4b8b` (the restructure commit): the CM-* CLAUDE.md revision series (nine docs commits, `2d91a9f`…`f72b309`) landed on `main` after the freeze locked, and cutting at the stale base would run the stage on a pre-revision CLAUDE.md and break the P-D4-style close-time ff-merge. Close mechanics mirror P-D4: two-commit PENDING close (`PENDING_PBIS` placeholder in the `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` master row + the Completion Log; `CLAUDE.md` §11 stays hash-free) → the `*-backfill` commit substitutes the real hash → tag `stage-p-bis-complete` at the backfill commit → ff-merge to `main`. `stage-p-bis-complete` is the frozen audit candidate handed to Stage Q.

## PB-D4 — Stage shape: three sequenced workstreams A → B → C

A (open-issue close-out) → B (Sepolia deployment) → C (`aumm-app` frontend). A before B so the one broadcast carries the fixed code; B before C so the frontend integrates against live addresses. Workstream-A exit rule: every roster item (`STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` § "Stage P-bis" holds the roster) exits Fixed, Built, or Explicitly-descoped-with-rationale — each descope is recorded as its own PB-D entry, nothing silently carried past the tag. The hevm/Act formal-methods bundle (P-D9) and the WK.18 thin-venue sim run LAST in A, after all fix items land, so their results bind the near-frozen tree. Workstream C is tracked from this repo as a milestone plus the deployed-addresses artifact contract (`aumm-app` is a separate repo per OQ-18); C's completion gate here is the artifact published + the frontend demonstrably reading the Sepolia deployment.

## PB-D5 — Workstream-A adjudication queue runs FIRST

The build-vs-descope adjudications are scheduled as the first sub-steps of workstream A, before any build work (§12 ambiguity-gate — decisions precede materialization): (1) OQ-20 / OQ-21 — controller-initiated `routeYieldFee` + `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` cadence throttle (E-D10); (2) OQ-22 — 2-hop fallback through ZCHF / sUSDS; (3) pools 04 ixViatica / 07 ixCambio restore-vs-descope (E-D17 literals bar) + the ixCasper waEthwstETH 16% leg composite-RP restore + the OQ-I10 recorder-binding remainder; (4) F-13 — the rate-derived `limitRaw` / `minBptAmountOut` bound vs accept-with-quantifying-sim; (5) F-15 — the F-06-style snapshot propagation vs accept. Each adjudication locks as its own PB-D entry before its build/descope sub-step is drafted.

## PB-D6 — OQ-20 / OQ-21 disposition: BUILD both (user-adjudicated 2026-07-11; PB2.1, builds insert as PB2.1b+)

The D4.6 controller entry point and the epoch throttle both build at P-bis. **Entry point (OQ-20 Option A, signature now pinned):** `function routeYieldFeeToHook(address pool, IERC20 token, uint256 amount) external authenticate returns (uint256 bptMinted)` on `AureumProtocolFeeController` — body: `token.forceApprove(FEE_ROUTING_HOOK, amount)` (SafeERC20 already `using` at L59), then `bptMinted = IAureumFeeRoutingHook(FEE_ROUTING_HOOK).routeYieldFee(pool, token, amount)` (new import `../fee_router/IAureumFeeRoutingHook.sol`); the hook pulls via `safeTransferFrom` (`AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` L611), preserving the leg asymmetry (B10 `safeTransfer` ≠ yield `safeTransferFrom`, OQ-20); pool/amount validation stays hook-side (L606–L609 caller-pin + ZeroAddress + InvalidPool(DER_BODENSEE) + ZeroAmount) — the controller adds no duplicate gates; no controller-side event — the hook's `YieldFeeRouted` (L621) is the observability surface; concrete-only, no interface extension (`IAureumProtocolFeeControllerHookExtension` stays scoped to the hook-invoked swap-leg surface per its NatSpec). **Throttle (OQ-21, granularity (a) per-pool):** `mapping(address pool => uint256 lastRouteBlock) private _lastRouteBlock;` + new error `RouteThrottled();` the gate `block.number < _lastRouteBlock[pool] + AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` reverts before the approve; the stamp writes only after the hook call returns (FINDINGS L1092 — a reverting route must not burn the pool's epoch); stamp-after-external-call is safe under the authenticate gate (a reentering token contract fails authentication, and the hook leg is Vault-unlock-guarded). `AureumTime` becomes a controller import (`../lib/AureumTime.sol`) — library-internal constant, compile-time inlined, no storage-layout impact. Verified free pre-build: `routeYieldFeeToHook` has zero hits across src/, test/, script/. Build roster: PB2.1b controller edit; PB2.1c unit tests (authenticate stranger-revert, throttle boundary at exactly +BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH, stamp-unchanged-on-hook-revert, exact allowance consumed to zero, bptMinted propagation); PB2.1d yield-leg fork witness (collect → route → der-Bodensee BPT growth, the Leg-B sibling); PB2.1e FINDINGS OQ-20/OQ-21 status closure + the plan Completion Log row. The §ix spec amendment stays user-side (PB2.10).

## PB-D7 — OQ-22 disposition: BUILD the 2-hop valuation fallback (user-adjudicated 2026-07-12; PB2.2, builds insert as PB2.2b+)

**Scope correction first:** the PB2.2 roster label "2-hop fee-routing fallback" (plan L70; CLAUDE.md §11) is wording drift — the fee-routing 2-hop (the ixEDEL bridge) was an F-14 alternative dropped at P-D12 and stays dropped; the live carry-forward is the **TVLOracle valuation 2-hop** (OQ-22 step 2, FINDINGS L1119 "a 2-hop path through ZCHF or sUSDS"; H-D9 shipped Phase 1 = direct venues only). Quantified Phase-1 gap on the deployed roster: ixLibertas (06) prices 42% (sUSDS 14 + ysyBOLD 14 + GHO 14) — scrvUSD 15 + USDC 14 + PYUSD 15 + USDT 14 = 58% resolve to `tvl_contribution = 0`; ixAetheron (02) prices ~0% (the F-14 venue wall; the ixEDEL-BPT 16% leg is held by no other pool); downstream `tvlEMA → CCB score → emission share` under-reads ixLibertas ~2.4×, and `EfficiencyOracle.recordFees` zeroes the same four fee tokens through `quoteSvZCHF`. **Mechanism (pinned, all inside `TVLOracle.sol` — `ITVLOracle` unchanged, consumers unchanged, constructor unchanged, zero call-site ripple):** (i) `_venueRatio` widens to `(address v, address base, address quote)` — the quote-side test `u == SVZCHF` becomes `u == quote`; direct callers pass `SVZCHF`. (ii) The existing two-leg venue walk (reverse-map leg + Miliarium dense leg, K-D8 dedup, `acc / count` mean) extracts byte-equivalent to `_directRatio(address base, address quote)`. (iii) `_constellationRatio(U)` = identity at `SVZCHF`; else `_directRatio(U, SVZCHF)`; on zero, `_twoHopRatio(U)` — direct venues always win, the fallback fires only on direct-miss. (iv) `_twoHopRatio(U)`: iterate the hop roster; skip `I == U` and `I == SVZCHF`; `h2 = _directRatio(I, SVZCHF)` first (skip on 0 — keeps a venueless intermediate such as raw ZCHF inert), then `h1 = _directRatio(U, I)` (skip on 0); candidate `h1 * h2 / 1e18`; return the arithmetic mean across contributing intermediates, else 0. No transitive hops — `_twoHopRatio` calls only `_directRatio`, depth exactly 2; a U reachable only through an intermediate that itself needs a hop stays 0 (pinned). (v) Hop roster: `address[] private _hopUnderlyings` + membership mapping + `addHopUnderlying(address)` `onlyGovernance` append-only (`ZeroAddress` / `AlreadyAdded` guards, event, no clear primitive — mirrors `setTokenUnderlying` stickiness), the H-D8 append-only-roster pattern behind the existing `governance` slot (no second authority per H-D9); inert while empty, so pre-seed behavior is byte-identical Phase 1 and the existing suites pass unchanged. Seed = ZCHF + USDS per L1119 (underlying-key space); production seeding joins the post-deploy governance wiring channel that already populates `tokenToUnderlying` (DeployStageF seeds empty by design — no deploy-script edit; testnet execution rides PB3). **Gas:** fallback cost only on direct-miss underlyings; the distributor user path (`recordEmissions → quoteSvZCHF(AuMM)`) is direct-hit at Bodensee and never falls back; `recordFees` is keeper-cadence; `updateEMA` daily per OQ-5a-bis; the F-19 `isPoolInitialized` skip carries into both hop legs via `_venueRatio`. **Residual (explicit disposition, not silent carry):** ixAetheron stays ~0 under any OQ-22-scoped option — the venue wall is a roster fact, not a code gap; remedy is product-level (Stage-O composition challenge or a future governance constellation venue). **Build roster:** PB2.2b `src/emission/TVLOracle.sol` (mechanism + every "Phase 1 / no 2-hop" NatSpec phrase updated to Phase-2 truth); PB2.2c unit tests (governance/duplicate/zero gates + event; fallback-only precedence under a live direct venue; exact product value on a USDS-hop direct-miss; mean across two contributing intermediates; `I == U` / `I == SVZCHF` skips; h2-miss inertness; no-transitive-hop zero; `tvl()` + `quoteSvZCHF` surface the fallback; empty-roster Phase-1 regression); PB2.2d fork witness (direct-miss leg recognized via the USDS hop with Bodensee as hop-2 on the mainnet-fork stack + EMASampler propagation); PB2.2e FINDINGS OQ-22 Phase-2 addendum + plan Completion Log rows + the roster-label reconciliation. Verified free pre-build: `addHopUnderlying` / `hopUnderlying` / `HopUnderlying` / `_twoHop` / `_directRatio` — zero hits across src/, test/, script/.

## PB-D8 — Pool-restoration disposition: ixCasper waEthwstETH BUILD via composite RP; 04 ixViatica / 07 ixCambio DESCOPE to the composition-challenge path; OQ-I10 closed (user-adjudicated 2026-07-12; PB2.3, builds insert as PB2.3b+)

**(i) ixCasper (03) — BUILD: restore waEthwstETH at the 16% theme leg, rated via a single Aureum `CompositeRateProvider`.** The M-D8-canonical Aave V3 stataToken wrapper waEthwstETH (`0x322AA5F5Be95644d6c36544B6c5061F072D16DF5`) returns at sorted index [1], replacing the M-D11 plain-wstETH stopgap; rate stack = `CompositeRateProvider(IERC4626(waEthwstETH), IRateProvider(0x72D07D7DcA67b8A406aD1Ec34ce969c90bFEE768))` — hop 1 `previewRedeem(1e18)` (wrapper share → wstETH), hop 2 the canonical Balancer wstETH RP (`stEthPerToken`, wstETH → ETH) — the N-D9 ysyBOLD stack shape and exactly the restoration M-D11 prescribed for re-evaluation. Fork-probed live 2026-07-12 (user-run `cast` per §8b): waEthwstETH `decimals()` = 18, `asset()` = wstETH `0x7f39C581F595B53c5cb19bD0b3f8dA6c935E2Ca0`, wstETH `decimals()` = 18 — both F-11 constructor gates pass; `previewRedeem(1e18)` = 1.0015e18, the hop-1 leg no prior run ever verified (M5.1b proved only the direct-RP revert); wstETH RP `getRate()` = 1.2392e18 (1.2377e18 at M5.1b block 25374166 — consistent stETH accrual); composed rate ≈ 1.2410e18. Evidentiary: the direct Aave stata RP `0xe1D97bF61901B075E9626c8A2340a7De385861Ef` still reverts (empty revert data) — the M-D11 anomaly class stands, and the composite stays the route regardless per N-D8's Aave-stata-oracle-avoidance rationale. Knock-ons: `IxCasperConfig` leaves the clean-`pure` set for the N-D7 param-injected form — `config(address waEthwstEthCompositeRp)` + `DeployIxCasper._config()` `pure`→`view` resolving env key `WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER`, the single-param mirror of `DeployIxMagnix`; in-place index-[1] literal swap, no re-sort (`0x322A…` sits between fWSTETH `0x2411…` and fWETH `0x9055…`, the slot wstETH held — M-D11 pre-verified); the leg stays `WITH_RATE` + `paysYieldFees = true`, the Aave supply-yield layer M-D11 called unrealizable now realizable; QG numerator 68% → 84% (fWSTETH 26 + fWETH 26 + waEthwstETH 16 + svZCHF 16), +32pp over `MIN_ERC4626_WEIGHT = 52e16`, re-aligning the code with the `03_ixCasper.md` profile's canonical 84% composition. Build roster: PB2.3b `script/pools/configs/03_ixCasper.s.sol` (param-injected `config(address)` + index-[1] literals + header/QG/M-D11-paragraph NatSpec rewrite) + `script/pools/DeployIxCasper.s.sol` (`_config()` `view` + env read — one commit, the pair compiles together); PB2.3c `test/fork/StageMIntegration.t.sol` + `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol` fixtures deploy the composite + `vm.setEnv` the key ahead of `DeployIxCasper().run()` (mirroring the ysyBOLD stack, StageNIntegration L91—L99 / StagePIntegration L242—L250; no QG literals pinned in either suite — the eligibility assertion is boolean, the split-form fork re-run arbitrates); PB2.3d `src/rate_provider/CompositeRateProvider.sol` NatSpec — the N-D9 "first real consumer" dev note gains the second consumer (comment-only, no code change); PB2.3e docs closure — `STAGE_I_PLAN.md` OQ-I10 closed-annotation + the plan's PB2.10 row gains the (iv) items + Completion Log rows. Verified free pre-build: `WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER` / `waEthwstEthCompositeRp` — zero hits across src/, test/, script/.

**(ii) 04 ixViatica / 07 ixCambio — DESCOPE to the Stage-O composition-challenge path.** Availability re-probed 2026-07-12, a month-plus after the M2 snapshot: fBRZ (04) — the rendered `docs.fluxfinance.com/addresses` page (browser-verified; the site is client-side-rendered, so the earlier bare-curl zero-output was not independently conclusive) lists exactly fUSDC / fDAI / fUSDT / fFRAX / fOUSG — no fBRZ, page "Last updated on June 13, 2024"; aEURS (07) — zero `EURS` occurrences across the bgd-labs address-book raw sources `AaveV3Ethereum.sol` / `AaveV3EthereumLido.sol` / `AaveV3EthereumEtherFi.sol` / `AaveV2Ethereum.sol` (plain-Solidity text, fetch-reliable), with the `AaveV3Polygon.sol` control returning 8 hits — the method detects EURS where it is listed, so the Ethereum-mainnet negative is real. Consequences unchanged from M-D7/M2: 07 is QG-critical-unbuildable (numerator 37% < the immutable 52% floor without aEURS's 18pp); 04's five-token composition cannot be assembled as specced (fBRZ resolves to no E-D17 literal under either protocol reading — Flux per the `07a` citation, or Fluid, which lists no BRZ either). Both slots exit the launch roster (the constellation launches 26 of 28) and re-enter, if ever, via the Stage-O composition-challenge path (`registerGaugeFromComposition`, governance-only, like-for-like) on a future aEURS Ethereum listing or BRZ vault. Explicitly rejected: a substitute EUR-4626 for 07 or an alternative BRL leg for 04 — spec amendments that belong to the challenge path, not a P-bis unilateral swap; the profiles stand unamended.

**(iii) OQ-I10 recorder-binding remainder — closed, zero remainder.** The remainder was 04/07 only (pilots bound at Stage I; M/N slots per M-D9); with both descoped under (ii), every launched pool carries its AuMT instance + recorder bind, and a future challenge-path replacement takes its binds at its own launch as a property of that flow, not a P-bis obligation. **(iv) User-side spec edits surfaced (join the PB2.10 batch):** the `03_ixCasper.md` profile prose mislabels Fluid as "Flux Finance" (fWSTETH / fWETH are Fluid per the `07a` rows, bound at the Fluid addresses `0x2411…` / `0x9055…`, M5 fork-verified — the code was always right); `04_ixViatica.md`'s "Flux Finance BRZ vault" wording shares the confusion; the `07a` slot-03 waEthwstETH row (flagged "RP unresolvable on mainnet" per M-D11) updates to the Aureum-composite restoration.

## PB-D9 — F-13 disposition: SPLIT — onAfterSwap ACCEPT with sandwich-sim evidence; routeYieldFee BUILD caller-supplied bounds; dormant deposit entries threaded + attested dormant (user-adjudicated 2026-07-13; PB2.4, builds insert as PB2.4b+)

**(i) Scope re-ground at HEAD — the WH-D row is stale in three ways.** The F-13 pointers (L487-504 / L494 / L530-563 / L552) predate F-14 / F-16 / F-17 / F-19 / F-20 / PB2.1; at `70d300b` the legs are `_swapExactInFeeTokenToDepositTokenViaVault` (`limitRaw: 0`, L524) and `_addLiquidityOneSidedToBodenseeViaVault` (`minBptAmountOut: 0`, L583), and the shared `_swapFeeAndDeposit` primitive (L468) has four consumers, not one: `onAfterSwap` (the audited per-swap path; conversion venue = `params.pool`); `routeYieldFee` (PB2.1 / PB-D6, built post-audit — caller-pinned to the controller's `routeYieldFeeToHook`, conversion venue = the source pool, amount = a full per-pool `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` accumulation); and `routeGovernanceDeposit` / `routeIncendiaryDeposit` (D17 fast-path only — a non-ZCHF-family token reverts `UnsupportedFeeToken`, so no swap leg, add-leg exposure only; DORMANT — zero callers across src/ and script/: Stage K bonds and Stage L boost deposits route via `SwapAndDepositToBodensee.donate`, zero-BPT DONATION with no `minBptAmountOut` surface, and `setGovernanceModule` / `setIncendiaryModule` remain unset by current design).

**(ii) onAfterSwap — ACCEPT, 0/0 unchanged; the quantifying sandwich sim is the evidence artifact, not a code change.** The WH-D dust rationale holds for this path: each event routes ≈ 50% × one trade's swap fee per token (a $100k swap at 0.30% routes ≈ $150), and capturing even half requires skewing the conversion venue ~50% against the hook at a skew-capital fee cost orders of magnitude above the take — fee-negative at every realistic size. Bounding this path is REJECTED on three independent grounds: (a) circularity — spot-derived bounds move with the same-bundle skew (`quoteSvZCHF` is constellation-spot averaging; fully circular when `params.pool` is the token's only venue), and the L3 EMA anchors only Bodensee/AuMM, so no lagged reference exists for arbitrary fee tokens on arbitrary pools — the WH-D "no oracle to derive a bound" statement stands for this path; (b) hot-path gas — the bound would tax every fee-charging swap in every pool to guard dust; (c) an F-14-class liveness regression — a bound tripping inside settlement-embedded `onAfterSwap` (organic volatility, thin venue) reverts the user's swap, the exact failure class P-D12 (1) engineered away. Severity stays S5 Low on this half. The sim (PB2.4d) measures extraction vs attacker cost at realistic sizes on the mainnet-fork stack — the artifact the WH-D disposition already called for.

**(iii) routeYieldFee / routeYieldFeeToHook — BUILD caller-supplied bounds; the dust rationale never covered this path.** The controller entry (`authenticate`-gated, per-pool `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` throttle per PB-D6) routes an epoch (~14 days) of accumulated yield fees in one call — non-dust once governance sets the yield fee (settable to the 50% cap; zero until set, and the rail exists precisely for when it is set) — and any public-mempool transaction with unbounded slippage is auto-exploited by generalized sandwich bots; on a thin-to-moderate venue the extraction math flips profitable, and governance-gated timing is no defense — the bot bundles around the transaction when it appears. Fix shape (pinned): two caller-supplied `uint256` params, `minDepositTokenOut` + `minBptAmountOut`, appended after the existing args and threaded `routeYieldFeeToHook → routeYieldFee → _routeYieldFeeUnlocked → _swapFeeAndDeposit`; `_swapFeeAndDeposit` gains both params, passing `minDepositTokenOut` as the `limitRaw` of `_swapExactInFeeTokenToDepositTokenViaVault` (EXACT_IN minimum-out — ENFORCED only when the swap leg runs; inert on the ZCHF→svZCHF ERC-4626 fast path and the same-token no-op, both rate-exact with no sandwich surface, the asymmetry documented in NatSpec) and `minBptAmountOut` into `_addLiquidityOneSidedToBodenseeViaVault`'s `AddLiquidityParams.minBptAmountOut` (enforced on every route — phase 2 always runs); `onAfterSwap`'s internal call passes `0, 0` — the hot path is behavior-identical. No oracle, no new coupling: governance computes bounds off-chain at fire time; the Vault enforces natively (`SwapLimit` / `BptAmountOutBelowMin`); a tripped bound reverts the route call only, and the PB-D6 stamps-after-success throttle means the pool's epoch is not burned — retry with a fresh bound. Events unchanged — `YieldFeeRouted` et al. keep their signatures; the bounds are visible in calldata.

**(iv) Dormant entry points — thread both params + dormancy attestation.** `routeGovernanceDeposit` / `routeIncendiaryDeposit` gain the same two params while `IAureumFeeRoutingHook` is open — one parameterized `_swapFeeAndDeposit` shape, no second variant; under D17 only their add leg is live, so `minBptAmountOut` is the real future footgun and `minDepositTokenOut` is inert there (fast-path callers pass 0). ATTESTED: threading is future-safe interface hygiene, not a wiring commitment — Stage K / Stage L stay on zero-BPT `donate` and the module setters remain unset by current design. Signature ripple (enumerated per G16; PB2.4b re-verifies with fresh greps): `IAureumFeeRoutingHook` (three declarations, L140 / L155 / L169) + `AureumFeeRoutingHook` (three external entries + three `_*Unlocked` callbacks — the `abi.encodeCall` payloads carry the two extra words — + `_swapFeeAndDeposit` + the two leg privates) + `AureumProtocolFeeController.routeYieldFeeToHook` (pass-through; concrete-only, no interface declares it) + the three route-call-site test suites (`test/unit/AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol`, `test/unit/AureumProtocolFeeController.t.sol`, `test/fork/AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol`; the F09 PoC imports the interface without route call sites — no ripple). Verified free pre-build: `minDepositTokenOut` — zero hits across src/, test/, script/, docs/.

**(v) Ledger split + build roster.** The F-13 row (`AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md:60`) exits SPLIT at PB2.4e: the PB-D6 yield path exits **Fixed** (caller-supplied bounds), the dormant entries are hardened + attested dormant, and `onAfterSwap` remains **Accepted-risk** at S5 Low with the sim as quantifying evidence; scope note that PB2.1 added a non-dust consumer post-audit (the 2026-07-01 Accepted-risk reasoning was written against the per-swap path only). Build roster: PB2.4b `src/fee_router/IAureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` + `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` + `src/vault/AureumProtocolFeeController.sol` (one commit — interface + impl + pass-through compile together); PB2.4c unit tests (Vault-native bound reverts on the three entries; `onAfterSwap` 0/0 regression; controller pass-through + throttle-not-stamped-on-bound-revert); PB2.4d fork witness (bounded route green at realistic bounds) + the quantifying sandwich sim (sim file placement pinned at the PB2.4d prompt); PB2.4e whitehat-ledger row update + plan Completion Log rows.

## PB-D10 — F-15 disposition: ACCEPT with poke-invariant sim evidence — suppression bounded at fully-poked truth; both snapshot fixes rejected as regression-bearing (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.5, evidence inserts as PB2.5b+)

**(i) Ground truth at HEAD `43d3509` — the sharpening the WH-G.4 row lacks.** `governanceWeight(holder)` returns the poked checkpoint `_holderWeight[holder]` (`VotingWeight.sol` L110-112), not a live position recompute; `totalSupply()` returns `_totalQualifiedWeight` (L114-116); `poke` maintains the invariant `_totalQualifiedWeight == sum of _holderWeight[*]` (L24; L130-131 underflow argument). Any weight that can enter the veto numerator (`vetoProposal` L284) is therefore already a summand of the L286 denominator: a never-poked holder vetoes at weight 0, and the defender's "inflation" moves the denominator toward fully-poked truth — it cannot invent weight beyond it.

**(ii) ACCEPT — Accepted-risk stands, S9 Low stands, the Stage-Q deferral clause retires.** The WH-G.4 2026-07-01 disposition leaned on "deferred to Stage Q"; Stage Q narrowed to the audit engagement at the 2026-07-11 restructure, so P-bis owns the standing rationale — four points: (1) truth ceiling — a coalition at or above 10% of fully-poked true qualified weight cannot be suppressed by any poke strategy, and a coalition clearing 10% only of an under-counted accumulator was never a G-D19 kill under a fully-poked reading of "10.00% AuMT-weighted vote-share"; (2) open numerator, vetoers move last — the threshold is re-evaluated at every veto call across the 28-day `VETO_WINDOW_BLOCKS = 201_600` window, and every holder the defender pokes in becomes live veto ammunition (inflation recruits its own counterparty) — the opposite geometry to F-06's frozen-turnout-over-still-inflatable-denominator after `endBlock`, so "propagate the F-06 snapshot" overstates the kinship; (3) real-stake gates on poke-able weight — 60-day EMA maturity + 14-day cliff + gauge approval + the F-05 freshness gate; no flash weight; (4) downstream bounds — admission is revocable (`revokeVaultClass` onlyGovernance) and an admitted class matters only if a pool adopts it and clears the 52% QG gate.

**(iii) Both fix shapes rejected as regression-bearing.** Full F-06 transplant (numerator + denominator snapshotted at `createdBlock`): `getPastVotes(vetoer, createdBlock)` disenfranchises every holder not poked before proposal creation — post-creation pokes can no longer restore veto power, so a proposer timing the proposal against community dormancy locks out the entire 28-day window, strictly worse than the finding; the repair is a Pending/`VOTING_DELAY`-class delay window (new constant + a G-D19 window-semantics amendment + struct and test rework) — disproportionate to a Low. Denominator-only snapshot (`getPastTotalSupply(createdBlock)`, numerator live): a proposal created before the first-ever `poke` reads snapshot supply 0, so every veto reverts on division-by-zero and the veto window is structurally bypassed — where the live read self-heals per (iv) — plus the `getPast*` current-or-future-block revert edge (a same-block veto) and a quiet semantics change to what the G-D19 live bar means. Prior art points the same way twice: the F-06 fix design affirmatively scoped the live reads out (`AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md`, the F-06 "Scope boundary (confirmed)" paragraph — a different consumer, cumulative accrual, no frozen-numerator geometry), and F-08 took the dedup fix while deferring checkpointed veto weight as optional defense-in-depth.

**(iv) Attested edge behavior (not a disposition change).** `totalSupply() == 0` with a zero-weight veto call reverts on division-by-zero (Panic 0x12) at L286 — the whole call unwinds, including the L283 `hasVetoed` write; no state mutates. Any nonzero-weight vetoer self-heals the denominator: their weight entered `_totalQualifiedWeight` at their own `poke`, so the denominator is at least their weight; against an otherwise-empty accumulator the first nonzero-weight veto trips the threshold at ratio 100% — fail-toward-veto. Pinned here so the Stage-Q auditors meet it as attested behavior, not a rediscovered surprise.

**(v) Evidence + closure roster.** PB2.5b — `test/whitehat/F15_vetoDenominatorInflation.t.sol` against the REAL `VotingWeight` wired into `VaultClassRegistry` (the direct-set `MockVotingWeight` cannot demonstrate the poke dynamics — the accumulator mechanics are the finding), on the `MockEMASampler` + `MockRecorder` harness pattern of `test/unit/VotingWeight.t.sol` (F-05-retrofitted freshness stamps via `_configurePosition`). Three pinned faces: (1) suppression-is-real — a thin coalition (at or above 10% of the pre-inflation accumulator, under 10% of truth) is held below the bar by defender pokes of dormant third-party weight; (2) truth-ceiling kill — a coalition at or above 10% of fully-poked truth lands the veto despite maximal defender inflation; (3) recruit-flip — a defender-poked dormant holder joining the veto moves the ratio toward the trip. Plus the (iv) edge pins: the zero-weight-versus-empty-accumulator panic revert and the first-nonzero-weight auto-kill. Unit-only, no fork — pure governance arithmetic, no mainnet dependency. PB2.5c — the F-15 ledger row rewrite (Accepted-risk standalone + evidence pointer, the PB2.4e shape) + the plan Completion Log rows. Verified free pre-build: no `test/whitehat/F15_*` file exists; `F15_` zero hits across src/, test/, script/.

## PB-D11 — PB-D2 amendment: VaultClassRegistry is one-shot-sealed, not re-settable — its handoff reroutes to the PB3.4 production bind (post-K, target AureumGovernance); PB2.6 narrows to the five-rotation script (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.6 pre-flight blocker)

**(i) The finding — PB-D2 (i) misclassifies `VaultClassRegistry`.** `setGovernanceContract` (`VaultClassRegistry.sol` L221-225) is an F-D23 one-shot self-sealing setter — gated on the dedicated `governanceSetter` role, not on current governance, and it zeroes that role on use; P-D31 already recorded "there is no later repair path." The production orchestration fires it BEFORE `DeployStageK`: `DeployStageP.s.sol` L126 binds `governanceContract = address(this)` (the multisig identity) with `DeployStageK` following at L130 — so on the uncorrected trajectory the slot seals at the multisig and `revokeVaultClass` (L311, the ONLY `onlyGovernance` surface in the contract; propose / veto / finalize are permissionless) becomes permanently uncallable after Safe dissolution — the exact stranding PB-D2 exists to prevent. A PB2.6 rotation call against the sealed slot reverts `OnlyGovernanceSetter`; the rotation script must not include it.

**(ii) Corrected re-settable slice — exactly five.** `EmissionDistributor.setGovernanceContract` (L202) / `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.setGovernanceContract` (L174) / `TVLOracle.setGovernanceContract` (L152) / `EfficiencyOracle.setGovernanceContract` (L135) — all `onlyGovernance` self-rotations — plus `SwapAndDepositToBodensee.setDonateAuthorizer` (L286, multi-shot per G-D21). PB2.6 = these five rotations multisig → `AureumGovernance` + the P-D36-style fork witness on the orchestrator-deployed stack + the `pauseManager` inert-pointer attestation + the one-line `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` roster-wording reconciliation — the PB-D2 deliverable otherwise unchanged. `VaultClassRegistry` drops out of the rotation set.

**(iii) `VaultClassRegistry` disposition — Option 1 of the three adjudicated: fix at the production bind.** The one-shot fires ONCE, in the PB3.4 un-deferred production `run()`, positioned AFTER `DeployStageK`, target = the `AureumGovernance` instance from K's return — never the multisig; `DeployStageP.deploy()` (the fork path, currently the pre-K L126 bind to `address(this)`) mirrors the reorder at PB3.4 so fork validation matches production, with the P10 fixture blast radius sized at the PB3.4 pre-flight (I13). Rejected: (Option 2) source-fixing the setter to `onlyGovernance` re-settable — reopens a Stage-G frozen contract and undoes the F-D23 / P-D31 one-shot invariant, and multisig-held `revokeVaultClass` during the OQ-10 window is not load-bearing (new-admission defense is the permissionless veto; revocation targets already-admitted classes and can live at on-chain governance from the bind); (Option 3) accept-the-brick — a permanent exclusive-capability loss contradicting PB-D2's own "no permanent operational slice" exit, and not analogous to the `pauseManager` slots, whose role is NON-exclusive via the authorizer while `revokeVaultClass` is exclusive to `governanceContract`.

**(iv) Pinned residual — the bind preserves caller identity, not executability.** `AureumGovernance.execute()` is a typed three-way dispatch (`ProposalType` GaugeChallenge / CompositionChallenge / FeeChange — `AureumGovernance.sol` L86 / L343-L363) with no generic-call hatch and no `revokeVaultClass` leg, so after the PB3.4 bind the function has a live authorized caller but no proposal path that reaches it; and because the slot seals on that bind, reachability can only ever come from an `AureumGovernance`-side code path. Mainnet is not yet deployed, so a typed revoke leg remains addable at source before Stage R — recorded as an explicit Stage-Q audit input / pre-R decision, NOT a P-bis deliverable and NOT silently accepted. Do not describe the outcome as "on-chain-governance-controlled from genesis" without this qualifier. OQ-10 window trade-off ACCEPTED: the multisig never holds `revokeVaultClass`; during the window, class defense = the permissionless veto (PB-D10), and admitted-class revocation waits on the residual path above.

**(v) Doc reconciliations.** The plan's PB2.6 roadmap line (`STAGE_P_BIS_PLAN.md` L74) drops `VaultClassRegistry` from the rotation enumeration in this same commit — a cold session drafting PB2.6 from the stale line would scope a reverting call, so the wording fixes before materialization per §12. K-D9's stranding rationale (`STAGE_K_NOTES.md` L35) listed `proposeVaultClass` among the functions a governance rebind would strand — stale: `proposeVaultClass` is permissionless (`VaultClassRegistry.sol` L248-252); the VCR member of that list is properly `revokeVaultClass`, and K-D9's core "typed dispatch, no generic-call hatch" observation is precisely the (iv) residual — STAGE_K_NOTES stands as the dated record, reconciled here. PB-D2 (i)'s "re-settable" characterization of `VaultClassRegistry` stands as the dated record, superseded in part by this entry; PB-D2 (ii) (`pauseManager`) and the net §xxix conclusion are unchanged.

## PB-D12 — PB-D2 second amendment: the emission-layer rotation strands the operational-wiring family under AureumGovernance v1 — rotations pinned to dissolution-time, the freeze witnessed as v1 truth, ossify-vs-extend consolidated to one Stage-Q / pre-R input (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.6 re-scoped)

**(i) The finding — PB-D2 never chose the post-dissolution operating model.** Its rotation deliverable was scoped without a per-function reachability triage — the cited "P9.5 authority-lifetime review" resolves to the P-D31 deploy-time seal-authority analysis (the three-class construction-binding split), not a post-rotation triage — and `AureumGovernance.execute()` is a typed three-way dispatch with no generic-call hatch (`AureumGovernance.sol` L360-L374; the K-D9 structural fact, `STAGE_K_NOTES.md` L35), so rotating an emission-layer `governance` slot to `AureumGovernance` strands every `onlyGovernance` function behind a caller with no path to invoke it — including `setGovernanceContract` itself, making each rotation terminal. PB-D2 also carries an internal timing contradiction: "firing these rotations at the K-handoff point... joins the deploy orchestration" vs "dissolution = fire the rotations + dissolve the Safe after the OQ-10 window" — deploy-time rotation would strand window-period wiring (the M/N-pattern launches, the PB-D7 hop-roster seed) and is REJECTED; dissolution-time is the only coherent reading.

**(ii) The stranded-family triage (grounded at HEAD).** MOOT: `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.distribute()` — the F-0 bootstrap rail accrues only through `month10EndBlock` and is a permanent zero-activity fixture (H-D13) before any OQ-10-window dissolution. ALREADY-BOUND ONE-SHOTS (frozen at their production values by construction): `setMintRouter` both sides (channel `MintRouterAlreadySet`; distributor L240). RE-POINTING VALVES LOST, no named future consumer: `setIncendiaryRegistry` (re-settable, bound at Stage L), `EfficiencyOracle.setEmissionsRecorder` (deprecation valve). LOAD-BEARING PERPETUAL: `EmissionDistributor.setAuMTContractForPool` — the emission spine of every future pool launch (`DeployStageM.s.sol` L111-113 precedent; the CompositionChallenge execute leg is gauge-only — QG check → `revokeGauge` → `replaceSlot` → `registerGaugeFromComposition`, `AureumGovernance.sol` L363-L368 — so a challenge-admitted pool without the bind records nothing, scores nothing, confers no weight); the `TVLOracle` trio `setTokenUnderlying` / `addConstellationPool` / `addHopUnderlying` — valuation wiring for future tokens and venues (PB-D7's roster growth presumes governance seeding); `EfficiencyOracle.setFeeRecorder` — the documented Phase-2 fee-feed wiring, unset by the production orchestration (`address(0)`, `recordFees` fail-closed dormant, NatSpec reserving "later upgrades"); `SwapAndDepositToBodensee.addAuthorizedDonator` / `removeAuthorizedDonator` — the roster-evolution lever the rotated `donateAuthorizer` gates. SAME FAMILY, DIFFERENT OWNERS: `revokeVaultClass` (PB-D11 (iv)) and the hook's `setTrustedRouter` — a "persistent, repeatable governance lever" behind the ONE-SHOT `setGovernanceModule` (`AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` L265-L270 / L307-L314), a PB3.3 target decision. NOT in the family: `EMASampler` — no `onlyGovernance` surface (`updateEMA` permissionless).

**(iii) Adjudication — Option D: build the dissolution-time artifact now; ossify-vs-extend stays open for Stage Q.** Rejected at P-bis: (A) full ossification accepted outright — premature against four documented Phase-2 intents (the `setFeeRecorder` NatSpec, the router lever "over time", the PB-D8 (iii) challenge binds, the PB-D7 roster growth) without auditor weight or an explicit product freeze; (B) a fourth typed wiring leg on `AureumGovernance` — a real governance-surface expansion against the locked K-D6 three-type design, wrong cadence on the eve of the audit freeze; a Stage-Q / pre-R decision. The five rotations are INVARIANT across A and B — they fire at dissolution either way, and B only changes whether the rotated-to authority has legs — so the PB2.6 build neither blocks on, nor prejudges, the open choice.

**(iv) PB2.6 re-scope (supersedes the PB-D2 "joins the deploy orchestration" clause).** The rotation script is a STANDALONE dissolution-time ops artifact — five calls, multisig-run as its last act after the OQ-10 window: the four emission `setGovernanceContract(AureumGovernance)` rotations + `SwapAndDepositToBodensee.setDonateAuthorizer(AureumGovernance)`. The fork witness runs on the orchestrator-deployed stack, simulates the dissolution-time rotations, and asserts BOTH faces: the rotations land (each slot == the `AureumGovernance` instance; the multisig can no longer call the rotated surfaces) AND the freeze is witnessed (the (ii) load-bearing members revert for every caller under `AureumGovernance` v1 — the freeze explicit, not accidental). The `pauseManager` inert-pointer attestation + the one-line `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` roster-wording reconciliation ride PB2.6 unchanged.

**(v) Posture, residual, and reconciliations.** P-bis records the post-rotation freeze as CURRENT-V1 TRUTH, not a constitutional forever — A vs B stays open. One consolidated, named Stage-Q audit input / pre-R decision (the PB-D11 (iv) pattern, widened to the family): the post-dissolution operational model for the (ii) load-bearing set + `revokeVaultClass` (PB-D11) + the hook `setTrustedRouter` / `setGovernanceModule` target. PB3.3 consequence: `setGovernanceModule` is one-shot and must aim at a permanent identity; under D that parks the router lever until Q chooses A (allowlist frozen) or B (reachable via the new leg) — sized at the PB3.3 pre-flight. PB-D8 (iii) reconciliation: "a future challenge-path replacement takes its binds at its own launch as a property of that flow" holds only while a live wiring authority exists (the multisig window); after dissolution it is void under A and returns via the new leg under B — the sentence stands as the dated record with this qualifier. PB-D2 (i)'s rotation-deliverable clause is superseded in part (timing + scope) by this entry and PB-D11; PB-D2's net "§xxix stands unamended" conclusion is unchanged, with "no permanent operational slice" now qualified by the open A/B choice.

### PB10 — `vm.prank` AND `vm.expectRevert` both latch onto a chained public-getter call, not the intended target (recurrence of P-D38, now indexed)

Recurrence of **P-D38** (`STAGE_P_NOTES.md` L509-517) at PB2.6c (2026-07-14). P-D38 recorded that `vm.prank(x)` is consumed by a chained public auto-getter STATICCALL (`orchestrator.emissionDistributor()`), so the intended call runs as the test contract — but it lived only in the `P-D*` process series and never reached the CLAUDE.md §11 lesson index, so a cold session drafting `test/fork/DissolutionGovernanceHandoff.t.sol` reintroduced the exact `vm.prank(caller); orchestrator.X().setY(...)` shape. This entry indexes it (PB10) so the next fork-test author greps it before drafting.

**Extension — `vm.expectRevert` latches the same getter.** P-D38's failure mode was a SILENT wrong-`msg.sender` (a 0-mint, no revert). PB2.6c-fix2 surfaced the second single-shot cheatcode: `vm.expectRevert(...)` also targets "the next call," which is the getter `orchestrator.emissionDistributor()` — a getter never reverts, so the harness reports "next call did not revert as expected" and the intended reverting setter never runs. Both single-shot cheatcodes (`vm.prank`, `vm.expectRevert`) latch the chained getter; only `vm.startPrank` / `vm.stopPrank` persists across it.

**Rule.** Cache every contract handle into a local BEFORE any single-shot cheatcode, so the intended call is the sole "next call": `IEmissionDistributor ed = orchestrator.emissionDistributor(); vm.prank(caller); vm.expectRevert(...); ed.setGovernanceContract(x);`. Chains inside `assertEq` / `assertTrue` (no cheatcode involved) are unaffected and need no caching.

**Cross-reference:** P-D38 (`STAGE_P_NOTES.md` L509-517, the `vm.prank` origin — missed the §11 index, hence this recurrence); fixed at PB2.6c-fix2 (`b850e8c`), `test/fork/DissolutionGovernanceHandoff.t.sol`.

## PB-D13 — PB2.7 D36 permanent fix DESCOPED: the one-file namespacing scope is stale and the parallel-safe exit gate unevidenceable — `--threads 1` promoted from F-D11 workaround to permanent canonical fork-invocation posture; the D36 branch-(b) parameterization records as RB-006 (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.7)

**(i) The finding — the plan's one-file scope is stale by an order of magnitude.** The PB2.7 wording ("env-key namespacing in `test/fork/DeployAureumVault.t.sol`") inherits the D36 world of 2026-04-25 — three fork files, one racing pair on one key (`FEE_ROUTING_HOOK`: `AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol` × `DeployAureumVault.t.sol`; `STAGE_D_NOTES.md` L446). Measured at HEAD `7e9d71f`: 224 `vm.setEnv` sites across 16 fork-test files (`StagePIntegration.t.sol` 48, `StageNIntegration.t.sol` 26, `DeployStageK.t.sol` 16), all writing the same canonical key names (`GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG`, `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK`, `VAULT`, `AUMM`, `GAUGE_REGISTRY`, `EMISSION_DISTRIBUTOR`, …) with per-contract values into the process-global env; 48 test contracts across the 25-file fork dir, and six of the 16 writer files hold multiple sibling contracts over a shared setEnv fixture (`StageGIntegration.t.sol` 7, `PilotPools.t.sol` 3, `CCBEngine.t.sol` / `StageMIntegration.t.sol` / `StageNIntegration.t.sol` / `StagePIntegration.t.sol` 2 each), so Foundry's contract-level parallelism races inside a single H-D40 canonical `--match-path` file run, not just across files. Namespacing one file removes one racer of ~48; the sub-step's own exit gate — "retire `--threads 1` once regression proves parallel-safe" — is unreachable at its stated scope.

**(ii) The real fix re-opens the P10-validated deploy plumbing inside the audit-candidate window.** No parameterized bypass exists on the consumer side: every deploy script's `deploy(address)` parameterizes only the deployer/governor identity, and the address wiring is `vm.envAddress` on fixed literal keys inside `deploy` itself (`DeployAureumVault.s.sol` L126-L133 is the pattern; all 16 `script/*.s.sol` read env keys). D36's branch (b) — explicit config-argument passing — therefore means config-struct entry points across the 16-script deploy surface plus a rewrite of every fork-fixture setUp (the 224 sites), re-validating the P10 evidence base on the eve of the freeze, for zero on-chain benefit. Branch (a) scaled — per-file key prefixes — requires script-side prefix indirection, polluting the exact broadcast path PB3 Sepolia and Stage R mainnet execute with test-only plumbing. Both rejected at P-bis cadence.

**(iii) The retirement gate is unevidenceable on the project's RPC posture.** "Regression proves parallel-safe" needs a green parallel fork run as evidence; per D35 + H-D40, combined parallel fork execution against Ankr free-tier hangs and rate-limits independently of env races (the 25-plus-minute indefinite-hang pattern is why per-file `--match-path` is the canonical invocation). The only mode the fix would enable — a combined parallel fork run — is the mode the RPC substrate independently forbids: a perfect fix could not be proven, and a partial one would flake unattributably.

**(iv) Risk posture — zero protocol surface; the workaround is already the documented canon.** The race lives entirely in test-harness env handoff. Broadcast paths execute one script per process against real shell env — no `vm.setEnv`, no contract-level concurrency, no race; nothing here touches deployed bytecode or the audit's contract surface. `--threads 1` has been the documented fork invocation in every stage plan from D36/H-D40 onward; this lock promotes it from workaround-pending-retirement to permanent canonical posture. The unit suite (Part A) carries zero `vm.setEnv` sites and keeps default parallelism — the posture costs no wall-clock anywhere.

**(v) Residual + reconciliations.** RB-006 records D36 branch (b) — config-struct entry points on the 16 deploy scripts + fork-fixture migration off `vm.setEnv` — as deferred hardening, revisit-gated on an RPC-posture upgrade (paid tier or local archive node) or post-audit cadence; the register also needs a new hardening-class bullet (test-fixture isolation), both landing at PB2.7b. Doc roster: the plan L22 tracker row + L75 sub-step re-word to this descope in this same commit (PB2.7a); the `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` workstream-A "D36 permanent fix / F-D11" bullet closes at PB2.7c; the plan Completion Log rows + the Decisions-table back-fill (PB-D6 through PB-D13; the index stalled at PB-D5) at PB2.7d; the CLAUDE.md §11 rewrite at PB2.7e (the D36 carry-forward bullet drops to the Dropped-as-CLOSED line, the §11 D36 index line gains "permanent per PB-D13", the resume anchor advances to PB2.8). Dated records stand unamended per convention: D36's "Permanent fix (deferred)" paragraph and H-D40's "remains deferred per §11 carry-forward" clause read as history, superseded by this entry.

## PB-D14 — PB2.8 mechanism amendment: `vm.transientStore` absent from the pinned toolchain — the direct `ReentrancyGuard.selector` witness lands via roll-plus-accrue mock reentry; scope narrows to `BodenseeBootstrapChannel` (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.8)

**(i) The finding — the plan names a cheatcode the pinned forge-std does not have.** The PB2.8 wording ("direct `ReentrancyGuard.selector` test via `vm.transientStore`", plan L84 + the L23 tracker row) inherits the H3.7e deferral note (`0fda303`, 2026-05-18). Verified at HEAD: `lib/forge-std/src/Vm.sol` (v1.15.0 per CLAUDE.md §2, 134 KB) carries zero case-insensitive hits for `transient` / `tload` / `tstore` — no transient-storage cheatcode exists at the pinned dep, and acquiring one is a §8c forge-std bump, rejected for one test (re-pins the toolchain provenance on the eve of the audit freeze).

**(ii) Scope narrows to the channel — the Stage-G sibling is already covered.** `test/unit/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.t.sol` holds direct `ReentrancyGuard.selector` witnesses for both guarded entries (`testReentrancyGuardFires` L378-L389, `testDonateReentrancyGuardFires` L540-L550) via the MockVault reentry switch, landed at Stage G. The only unwitnessed guard is `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.distribute()` L268: H3.7e's `test_Distribute_RevertWhen_Reentrant` re-enters from the mock's `getPoolTokenInfo` (inside `_distributeCallback`, `_EXECUTING_SLOT` already true) but the inner call dies at step 1 `NoPendingAccrual` — `pendingAccrual` zeroes before unlock — so the test asserts the bubble-up, never the guard selector.

**(iii) Mechanism locked — roll-plus-accrue inside the mock's reentrant branch (public-API reentry).** New MockVault flag `reentrancyAttackWithAccrual` + `enableReentrancyAttackWithAccrual()`; the `getPoolTokenInfo` reentrant path gains a second arm: `VM.roll(block.number + 1); channel.accrue(); channel.distribute();` with `VM` a `Vm` handle at the canonical cheatcode address (`0x7109709ECfa91a80626fF3989D68f67F5b1DD12D`; `import {Vm} from "forge-std/Vm.sol"` added to the test file). The inner `distribute()` then passes `NoPendingAccrual` (fresh one-block accrual — nonzero deep in the bootstrap window, the same mechanics the outer `accrue()` at `GENESIS_BLOCK_ + 1_000` already demonstrates), passes `MintRouterNotSet` / `mintFor` (router2 bound), and dies at the L268 `tload` — `ReentrancyGuard()` bubbles up through the mock to the outer call; the new test asserts `vm.expectRevert(BodenseeBootstrapChannel.ReentrancyGuard.selector)` on a cached `ch2` handle (PB10 discipline). Rejected: `vm.store` slot-seeding of `pendingAccrual` (a B22-style layout pin — couples the witness to the storage layout for no added proof value) and the forge-std bump per (i). The existing bubble-up test STAYS — it pins the distinct stale-accrual-reentry behavior; the new test is a sibling, not a rewrite.

**(iv) Deliverables + doc roster.** PB2.8b: `test/unit/BodenseeBootstrapChannel.t.sol` — the mock flag + enable fn + branch arm + `test_Distribute_RevertWhen_Reentrant_DirectGuard` (harness mirrors the L477 shape: ch2 gov = vault, router2 one-shot bind, `_rollTo(GENESIS_BLOCK_ + 1_000)`, outer `accrue()`); regression = the file-scoped unit run + the Part A split-form sweep. Rejected: a §8c Foundry / solc bump to chase coverage — P-D42's risk-ledger argument (a tooling artifact must not perturb the byte-identical Vault profile, CLAUDE.md §2) stands, sharper on the eve of the audit freeze; coverage re-eval rides to the post-audit / newer-toolchain gate alongside the Stage-Q hevm / Act bundle (the residual per-branch reachability is the hevm remit per P-D42's grain note).

## PB-D16 — PB2.11 WK.18 disposition: F-04's target surface is Fixed (60-day EMA gate), so the thin-venue sim exits BUILT as a populated-roster positive-control + economics quantifier, not a bug hunt; the R1-blocked could-not-assess closes with fork-grade evidence (user-adjudicated 2026-07-14; PB2.11)

**(i) The finding — WK.18's exploit leg is closed; what remains is the R1-blocked could-not-assess.** WK.18 produced F-04 (spot-`tvl()` → governance-weight atomic pump, S4/S7 Low, Confirmed), RESOLVED at `3d80ac8`: `VotingWeight._positionPower` (L163) now reads the 60-day `EMASampler.tvlEMA` behind the `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS = 60 × BLOCKS_PER_DAY` seed-age gate (L168 — unseeded/young → 0) + the `EMA_STALENESS_BLOCKS = BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` freshness gate, never the spot `ORACLE.tvl`; the `F04_spotTvlManipulation.t.sol` PoC (4/4) proves the gate on the K3 mock. The atomic pump is structurally dead. What the "thin-venue populated-roster fork sim" carry-forward names is the WK.18 COULD-NOT-ASSESS — R1-blocked at Stage K (pilots had no svZCHF path, `tvl = 0`), routed to the populated-roster sim — plus two folded threads: the WH-H "thin-venue `_constellationRatio` pump economics + tournament-ranking-input ROI" and the WH-P6 `addConstellationPool` double-append (a non-Miliarium venue holding 2+ same-underlying tokens counts its ratio twice; governance-controlled + EMA-damped + OQ-22 Phase-1 design-scoped).

**(ii) Mechanism (grounded at HEAD).** The pump vector: `_venueRatio` (`TVLOracle.sol` L307-326) reads a venue's LIVE `balancesLiveScaled18`, so a flash-swap skewing a thin venue's `balBase` / `balQuote` moves its `(balQuote·1e18)/balBase`; `_directRatio` (L247+) is the CROSS-VENUE ARITHMETIC MEAN across all eligible venues holding (underlying, SVZCHF), so a populated roster of N venues dilutes a single thin-venue pump to ~1/N of its ratio delta; `tvl(pool)` (L334) sums `balance × _constellationRatio`, so the pump moves spot `tvl`. The F-04 fix breaks the atomic path THERE: spot `tvl` feeds `EMASampler.updateEMA` (`(2·spot + 59·EMA)/61`, 1/day cadence — `EMASampler.sol` L145-147), so one same-tx pump moves the 60-day EMA by ≤ 2/61 ≈ 3.3% of the (spot − EMA) gap, and `_positionPower` reads that gated EMA under the F-9 era root (4th/3rd root), so the deliverable weight delta is (3.3%-of-gap)^¼ diluted by 1/N ≈ 0. A material EMA move requires SUSTAINING the pumped venue balance across ~60 days of daily samples — continuous skew-capital cost for a quartic-root, mean-diluted gain.

**(iii) The sim — BUILT as a populated-roster fork positive-control + economics quantifier (PB2.4d / PB2.5b precedent).** A new `test/fork/` populated-roster sim on the REAL Vault, three faces: (1) POSITIVE CONTROL — against a gauge-approved recorder position on a pool with a MATURE seeded EMA, an atomic flash-swap / DONATION pump of a thin constellation venue moves `governanceWeight` by ≈ 0 in the same tx as `poke` (the 60-day gate holds; a freshly-seeded venue confers 0), the fork-grade analogue of the F04 mock PoC; (2) CROSS-VENUE DILUTION — with ≥ 2 eligible venues for one underlying, the `_directRatio` mean moves ~1/N under a single-venue pump, so a populated roster strictly weakens the pump vs the sparse-roster R1 scenario; (3) SUSTAINED-PUMP ECONOMICS — one bounded scenario quantifying the skew-capital cost to hold the pump across enough daily `updateEMA` samples to move the gated EMA, vs the quartic-root mean-diluted weight / tournament-score gain, showing fee-negative (the PB2.4d sandwich-sim shape). The `addConstellationPool` double-append (thread iii) rides face (2) as a governance-controlled + EMA-damped attestation, not a code change. The exact fixture composition (the StageK governance-weight stack — VotingWeight + TVLOracle + EMASampler + MiliariumRegistry + constellation pools — layered onto the M/N populated roster) is resolved at the PB2.11b pre-flight by reading both fixture setUps; the mechanism and the three assertion faces are locked here.

**(iv) Doc roster.** PB2.11a: this lock + the plan L25 tracker row + L89 roadmap bullet re-word + the Decisions-table PB-D16 row. PB2.11b: the `test/fork/` populated-roster sim build (fixture composition pinned at its pre-flight; D35 / D36 split-form `--threads 1` invocation). PB2.11c: the whitehat-ledger WK.18 could-not-assess closure annotation (`AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md`) + the plan Completion Log rows + the CLAUDE.md §11 rewrite (resume anchor → PB2.12, the LAST-in-A hevm / Act bundle). F-04 stays Fixed (this adds fork-grade evidence, no status change); the WH-H / WH-P6 folded threads exit attested-bounded.

### PB11 — an em-dash (U+2014) is invalid inside a Solidity string literal — solc 0.8.26 rejects it (invalid-character-in-string-literal, observed as Error 8936); §10's em-dash house style is comments / NatSpec only

Caught at PB2.11b-vi, the face-3 compile of `test/fork/WK18ThinVenuePumpSim.t.sol`: an assertion-message string carried a copy-pasted em-dash and solc 0.8.26 rejected the file at parse with the invalid-character-in-string-literal error (observed as Error 8936). A plain `"..."` string literal admits only printable ASCII plus the defined escape set — U+2014 is representable only in a `unicode"..."` literal or via a `\u2014` escape, neither wanted in a message that compiles into bytecode. §10's em-dash convention is a PROSE house style: it governs comments, NatSpec, docs, and commit messages (UTF-8 to solc, or ignored), but any string that reaches the compiler as a literal — revert reasons, `require` / `assert` / `revert` messages, forge assertion-message arguments, event strings — must be ASCII.

**Rule.** When prose is copied into a Solidity string literal, convert every em-dash to ASCII (`-`, `--`, or reword); after a Solidity edit that pasted prose, grep the diff for U+2014 inside double-quotes before compiling. §10 stops at the quote mark.

### PB12 — a fork sim that rolls `block.number` to mature an EMA also ramps `timeFactor`; pre-roll past the 180-day ON_RAMP cap before measuring, or the delta conflates both

`VotingWeight._positionPower` (`src/governance/VotingWeight.sol` L187-L194) scales pool power by `timeFactor = min(timeInPool, ON_RAMP_PERIOD_BLOCKS) / ON_RAMP_PERIOD_BLOCKS`, where `timeInPool = block.number - eqb` and `ON_RAMP_PERIOD_BLOCKS = 180 * BLOCKS_PER_DAY` (`AureumTime.sol` L36). A sim that advances `block.number` first to mature the 60-day EMA and then across the pump-measurement window keeps `timeFactor` climbing during the measurement, so the observed `governanceWeight` delta conflates the EMA / poolPower change under test with a still-ramping on-ramp fraction. At PB2.11b-vi face-3 the pre-fix baseline carried a 0.33x `timeFactor` artifact: `weightBaseline` read 1.93e18, and pinning `timeFactor` first lifted it to 5.78e18 (~3x), accounting for the discrepancy before any pump was applied.

**Rule.** Pre-roll the measured position past the 180-day `ON_RAMP_PERIOD_BLOCKS` cap BEFORE the measurement window, so `timeFactor` pins at 1.0 and the delta isolates the term under test. Any `block.number`-scaled factor in the read path (on-ramp, qualification, staleness) is a confound candidate whenever the harness also rolls time to build state — pin it or subtract it.

### PB13 — net-new prose for Cursor to insert goes INSIDE the CURSOR PROMPT between explicit begin-insert / end-insert banners, never a separate review fence

Caught at PB2.11c-a. The verbatim insert text (a whitehat-ledger annotation) was placed in a separate "review-only, do-not-paste" fence ABOVE the CURSOR PROMPT so the user could proofread it without it reaching Cursor — but the CURSOR PROMPT then told Cursor to insert text Cursor never received, so Cursor got the instruction with an empty payload and correctly stopped. The §8e.1 two-block split moves the terminal-verify SHELL commands out of the Cursor payload; it does not license moving the INSERT content out — that content is exactly what Cursor must have.

**Rule.** Net-new verbatim-insert prose lives inside the CURSOR PROMPT block, delimited by explicit begin-insert / end-insert banner lines, so Cursor receives it together with the instruction; the whole CURSOR PROMPT is the user's proofread surface, so no separate review fence is needed. Cross-ref: the `draft-fence-paste-hazard` memory (the inverse hazard — a review fence pasted to Cursor by reflex) and the §8e.1 two-block rule.

### PB14 — a file's zero-hit grep sentinel must be run against the drafted §8e.1 payload before handoff, not only against the saved file after

Caught at PB2.12d4b, the operator-reconciliation pass over `formal/act/fee_routing_hook.act`. The spec carries a zero-hit grep sentinel on its banned legacy tokens (the three-character not-equal operator; the disjunctive word-operator), enforced in USER VERIFY against the saved file. The first draft of the fix defeated that sentinel at the source: the Operators comment block, rewritten to explain which legacy token had been removed, quoted that token literally — so the reconciliation note itself re-introduced the exact sequence the sentinel bans, and a faithful Cursor transcription would have saved a file that failed its own zero-hit grep. Cursor's replace was complete and correct to the letter of the instruction; the defect lived in the drafted payload, which smuggled the banned token in through a descriptive comment. The landed fix describes the removed token by name and never quotes it — as this very entry does.

**Rule.** When a §8e.1 targets a file that carries a zero-hit grep sentinel — a banned operator, an em-dash inside a string literal (PB11), a forbidden import — run that sentinel against the drafted payload text before handoff, not only against the saved file in USER VERIFY. A payload that names the banned token, even to record that it was removed, defeats the file's invariant the instant Cursor transcribes it faithfully; describe such a token by name rather than quoting it. Cross-ref: PB11 (the em-dash-in-string-literal sentinel), PB13 (insert-payload discipline), and the `draft-fence-paste-hazard` memory.

## PB-D17 — PB2.12 hevm/Act disposition: hevm 0.58.0 local symbolic runs (native arm64 asset + resident z3); Act v0.2.0 source-only, so .act specs ship as audit-handoff artifacts with proving attested firm-side; new top-level formal/; exit BUILT (user-adjudicated 2026-07-15; PB2.12)

**(i) Toolchain probe (2026-07-15).** Both repos moved orgs — `ethereum/hevm` → `argotorg/hevm`, `ethereum/act` → `argotorg/act` (the Argot Collective's own org; install sources pin there). hevm latest: `release/0.58.0`, shipping a native `hevm-arm64-macos` release asset — no Rosetta, no local build; SMT backend: the machine-resident z3 4.15.4 (Homebrew). Act latest: `v0.2.0`, source-only — zero binary release assets; the local prover toolchain (ghc / cabal / nix) is absent from the machine (arm64 macOS). Probe lesson: the first API probe read empty because curl without `-L` stops at the moved-org 301 redirect stub — not rate-limiting (57/60 quota remained); an API probe that filters through grep must carry a `"message"` term so error bodies surface instead of greping to empty.

**(ii) Act posture — specs-as-artifact, proving firm-side.** Acquiring a Haskell / nix toolchain (or a Docker build route) on the eve of the audit freeze is rejected — §8b footprint for zero on-chain benefit, and `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` L411 already contemplates the audit firm commissioning Act properties. The `.act` specs for all four surfaces are authored as audit-handoff artifacts (the L380 deliverable is "specs and results"); `formal/RESULTS.md` attests "authored; machine-check firm-side" per Act property. If the firm's environment proves them, that is Stage-Q value on the same artifacts; if it amends them, the spec text is the negotiation surface.

**(iii) Surfaces.** The four L380 surfaces, mapped: S5 the fee-routing hook (`AureumFeeRoutingHook` + the `AureumProtocolFeeController` seam); S7 the CCB multiplier / EMA engine; emission accrual (`EmissionDistributor` / `BodenseeBootstrapChannel` / the AuMM cap + halving — the L411 candidate properties land here: 21M cap never exceeded, era-boundary emission exactness, no admin-key redirect path); S8-relabeled the authorizer window (`AureumGovernanceAuthorizer` timelock / emergency window + the `Vault.setAuthorizer` one-shot migration). Per-surface property rosters are pinned at each sub-step's own pre-flight (the PB2.11b fixture-composition pattern), not here.

**(iv) Layout + exit.** New top-level `formal/`: `formal/act/` (the .act specs), `formal/hevm/` (runner scripts + captured output), `formal/RESULTS.md` (the property → status map: proved / bounded-with-named-residual / firm-side). Exit BUILT = all four surfaces spec'd; hevm symbolic green-or-named-residual on the tractable subset; RESULTS.md complete; tool versions pinned in this entry. A surface neither tool reaches exits with a named residual routed to Stage Q — still BUILT so long as its spec exists. No binary enters the repo: the hevm executable installs user-machine-local outside the tree; RESULTS.md records the `hevm version` output + the binary's sha256. CLAUDE.md §3 gains the `formal/` line at the close sub-step.

**(v) Build roster.** PB2.12a: this lock + the plan L26 tracker row + L91 roadmap bullet + the Decisions-table PB-D17 row. PB2.12b: the §8b install beat (user-run: fetch `hevm-arm64-macos` from the pinned `release/0.58.0` URL, chmod, `hevm version`, record provenance). PB2.12c—f: per-surface spec + symbolic run, one surface per sub-step, hook → CCB/EMA → emission accrual → authorizer window. PB2.12g: the `formal/RESULTS.md` roll-up + the plan Completion Log rows + the CLAUDE.md §11 rewrite (workstream A CLOSED; resume anchor → PB3.1).

**(vi) As-built (2026-07-16; PB2.12g).** The bundle landed across PB2.12a—g1; exit BUILT confirmed. Three as-built notes against the (iv) sketch:

- *Layout.* The hevm harnesses live at `test/formal/` (four `*Proofs.sol` files), not the sketched `formal/hevm/`: the ordinary `forge build` compiles them and `hevm test --root .` discovers them from the forge artifacts, so no separate runner directory or captured-output tree is needed. `formal/` holds `act/` (the nine specs) + `RESULTS.md` only; `RESULTS.md` §3 records the deviation, and that attested index supersedes captured output. The hevm binary stays user-machine-local (its sha256 in `RESULTS.md` §1); no binary enters the repo, as (iv) required.

- *Attestation.* 52/52 PASS under the canonical `hevm test --match prove_ --solver bitwuzla --smt-timeout 300 --max-iterations 10 --root .` invocation — 9 hook + 6 CCB + 30 emission + 7 authorizer — attested at PB2.12f1 (`6eec86d`). PB2.12f2 and g1 touch only `formal/` (zero Solidity deltas), so the attestation binds the harness set bit-for-bit through the close.

- *Residual taxonomy.* The (iv) "named residual" concept resolved into four classes (`RESULTS.md` §7): cross-contract fragment, dialect boundary, environment read, and the one Tier-1 named residual (P-S1/P-S2 solver exhaustion). The environment-read class is new at PB2.12f2 — `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer.canPerform` reads `block.number` with no external call, so it is neither a cross-contract fragment (the S5/S7 residuals) nor a dialect-arithmetic boundary (`blockEmissionRate`); it is flagged for the firm to model directly if its v0.2.0 grammar witnesses the block reference. P-H4 is a vacant property ID (no proof, no residual), noted in `RESULTS.md` §5.1 so the firm does not hunt a missing proof.

CLAUDE.md §3 gains the `formal/` line and §11 closes workstream A (resume anchor → PB3.1) at the PB2.12g3 CLAUDE.md close; the plan Completion Log family rows land at PB2.12g4.

## PB-D18 — PB2.13 lock: OQ-23 reopened — delta_global re-keys to the all-Active-gauge aggregate, delta_basket withdrawn (damping inherent in F-5/F-6 proportionality), zero-TVL disposition attached (user-adjudicated 2026-07-16; PB2.13)

**(i) Trigger + adjudication.** A 2026-07-16 mid-session directive pair reopened OQ-23. First directive: `delta_global` must track total protocol TVL — all gauged/eligible pools — not the Miliarium-only aggregate OQ-23 (iii.b) shipped; a third `delta_basket` channel damping Miliarium-vs-non-Miliarium relative drift was proposed alongside. The same-day follow-up withdrew `delta_basket`: the damping is already inherent in the allocation rule — every gauged eligible pool earns emission proportional to its EMA-60 TVL over the total-protocol EMA-60 TVL (F-5/F-6 one-denominator proportionality), so inter-basket drift self-adjusts with no multiplier lever. Pre-flight confirmed this in both spec and code: `EmissionDistributor.recordScore` scores Miliarium and non-Miliarium gauges into one `totalScore` denominator (H-D15/H-D17; non-Miliarium `CCB_mult = 1` per F-D9), `11_formulas.md` F-8 already reads "direction of total protocol TVL EMA", and `03_theoretical_foundation.md` §vii explicitly denies a protect-the-constellation lever. F-8 stays two-channel; the spec needs no edit — the §vii-reversal framing and the F-8/§vii items queued into PB2.10 at the g3b anchor are dropped; the deviation is confined to FINDINGS OQ-23 (iii.b) and the code implementing it.

**(ii) delta_global aggregate.** `currentGlobalAgg = sum(emaSampler.tvlEMA(gaugeRegistry.gaugeAt(i)) for i < gaugeRegistry.gaugeCount())` — the currently-Active gauge set (P-D13 `_activeGauges` EnumerableSet; Revoked pools leave the set, so no status filtering). Raw unconditional sum: no maturity/staleness gating on the aggregate — newly-gauged TVL is genuine protocol growth, the shipped Miliarium leg is equally ungated, and the 0.1% `DEAD_ZONE` absorbs sampling noise. `lastProtocolAggregateEMA` re-keys to this aggregate (the slot name finally matches its claim); F-D18 cold-start semantics carry over — the zero sentinel (never written, or a genuinely zero aggregate) yields `delta_global = 0` for that epoch.

**(iii) delta_intra decoupled, unchanged.** The existing Miliarium enumeration loop is retained solely to feed `miliariumAvg = miliariumAgg / MILIARIUM_POOL_COUNT`; OQ-23 (iv.a) simple-mean semantics stand. `updateMultiplier` therefore walks two rosters per call: the 28-pool Miliarium sum (intra baseline) and the Active-gauge sum (global baseline); Miliarium pools legitimately appear in both.

**(iv) Constants untouched.** `STEP_SIZE` ±0.05e18 per channel, `DEAD_ZONE` 1e15, clamp [0.75e18, 1.25e18], total per-update delta in {-0.10e18, -0.05e18, 0, +0.05e18, +0.10e18} — all F-D7 / OQ-23 (i)/(ii) values unchanged. The "likely clamp/step revisit" carried in the g3b anchor evaporates with delta_basket.

**(v) Mechanism — restore the P-D22-removed shape with a live consumer.** Constructor returns 2→3-arg (`_miliariumRegistry`, `_emaSampler`, `_gaugeRegistry` placeholder, zero-address-rejected) plus the F-D20-parallel one-shot `setGaugeRegistry` / `gaugeRegistrySetter` self-sealing pair; the orchestrator seals to the concrete `GaugeRegistry` after the G-stack deploys (F-engine-first deploy order preserved — mirror of the Stage-J `setMiliariumRegistry` handoff). `gaugeCount()` / `gaugeAt(uint256)` land as additive symbols on `src/ccb/IGaugeRegistry.sol` (the interface's additive-symbols clause; `GaugeRegistry` already implements both concretely at L254/L259); the G16 inheritor sweep (`grep -rn "is IGaugeRegistry"`) is the first build action of PB2.13d. Pre-seal posture: a placeholder returning `gaugeCount() = 0` yields a zero aggregate → neutral `delta_global` per (ii). `updateMultiplier` stays Miliarium-gated (only the aggregate universe widens); `getMultiplier` is untouched — EmissionDistributor, GaugeRegistry, EMASampler, ICCBMultiplier all zero-change. Construction blast radius enumerated up front per the P-D22 lesson, 12 sites: `script/DeployStageF.s.sol` L112; `test/formal/CCBEngineProofs.sol` L167/L188/L203; `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol` L85/L111/L116; `test/fork/` StageHIntegration L81, StageIIntegration L59, StageJIntegration L45, CCBEngine L192, DeployStageJ L33.

**(vi) Zero-TVL disposition (second 2026-07-16 directive).** Concern: a pool at zero TVL must not earn emissions, deltas notwithstanding. Traced pre-lock: the F-8 deltas are multiplier-side and F-5 is multiplicative (`CCBScore.score = tvlEMA.mulDown(multiplier)`), so a zero or gated-to-zero EMA collapses the score to zero regardless of the multiplier — deltas cannot resurrect a dead score. The distributor gate `_gatedTvlEMA` (F-04/F-05 mirror) reads unseeded, immature (< 60d), or stale (> 14d unsampled) EMAs as 0 — return-0-not-revert — so any third party can permissionlessly `recordScore(pool)` a drained-and-abandoned pool to zero its forward share. Residual windows to evidence at PB2.13h: (a) drain transient — a drained-but-still-sampled pool earns on its geometrically decaying EMA (2/61 daily, ~21d half-life) until decay, staleness, or re-record zeroes it (the WK18 anti-pump smoothing running in reverse); (b) stale-score window — `poolScore` persists between `recordScore` calls, bounded by the permissionless re-score incentive (other LPs de-dilute themselves) and post-Year-1 by the F-10 bottom-tier caps; (c) dust-LP capture — H-D24 stranding covers only `poolTotalLP == 0` and F-17 `syncPosition` covers only phantom records, so live dust units capture whatever residual allocation the decaying score still earns; (d) equal-regime carve-out — during Months 0–12 a zero-TVL Miliarium pool still earns the F-1/F-3 equal-split leg (`recordScore` L482), spec-mandated §xxviii/H-D6 semantics, note-only. Exit per the PB-D5 discipline: unit pins for the multiplicative kill + gate self-clearing, fork-grade economics on (a)–(c) if the pins leave material doubt, then Fixed / as-designed-with-evidence / Accepted-risk.

**(vii) Build roster.** PB2.13a — this lock. b — `STAGE_P_BIS_PLAN.md` PB2.13 section insert + Decisions-table PB-D18 row (restores plan-line citability for the family's §8e.1 prompts). c — FINDINGS OQ-23 reopen: (iii.b) universe superseded to all-Active-gauges, (iv.a) decoupled-but-unchanged, delta_basket recorded-and-withdrawn with the F-6 rationale, zero spec-side edits. d — `CCBMultiplier` rebuild per (ii)–(v). e — unit: restored `MockGaugeRegistry` with enumeration, the F-D20-mirror setter trio, delta_global math against a mixed Miliarium + non-Miliarium roster, cold-start/zero-aggregate pins. f — fork + deploy-wiring: the 12 construction sites, `DeployStageF` third arg, orchestrator `setGaugeRegistry` seal + wiring assertion in the P10 family. g — formal: `CCBEngineProofs.sol` ctor sites + mirrored `setGaugeRegistry` proofs (suite 52 → ~55, §8b user-run re-attest), `ccb_multiplier.act` creates/transition additions + residual-footer rewrite to the two-aggregate contract, `formal/RESULTS.md` rows. h — zero-TVL disposition per (vi). i — close: Completion Log family rows, §11 anchor amendments (the delta_basket sentence, the clamp/step clause, the PB2.10 F-8/§vii item drop), workstream A closes → PB3.1.

**(viii) Zero-TVL disposition — resolved as-designed-with-evidence (PB2.13h; zero code change).** The (vi) concern exits Fixed-by-existing-gates with the residual windows quantified, not merely asserted. Resurrect-proof core: `test/unit/ZeroTvlDisposition.t.sol` pins the F-8 multiplicative kill at the clamp ceiling — an unseeded or deeply-stale EMA scores exactly zero under a 1.25e18 multiplier, with a fresh-mature control (`16_000e18 × 1.25e18 = 20_000e18`) proving the multiplier is live, so the zeros are the gate's kill and not a dead rig; the library-level identity (`tvlEMA == 0 ⇒ score == 0`, either operand) stays pinned by `test/unit/CCBScore.t.sol`. Per-window: (a) drain transient — as-designed-with-evidence: `ZeroTvlDrainDecayTest` on the real `EMASampler` measures the `(59/61)^n` decay at 49–51% of seed by 21 daily samples and under 5% by 90, so the drained pool's tail is self-terminating and the F-10 14-day staleness gate (`F10_emaScoreGate.t.sol`) hard-caps it regardless; (b) stale-score window — as-designed-with-evidence: the `windowB` pair pins both edges, `poolScore` persisting across five stale epochs (the exposure) and any stranger's permissionless `recordScore` self-clearing it to zero (the bound, matching the `EmissionDistributor` L454 NatSpec); (c) dust-LP capture — as-designed-with-evidence: `ZeroTvlDustCaptureTest` shows a 1-wei holder captures the full tranche wei-exactly while the score lives and captures zero the epoch after the self-clear, so the magnitude a dust unit can seize is exactly the decaying/stale tail already bounded by (a)+(b) — (c) adds no exposure beyond them, only the observation that H-D24 stranding (`poolTotalLP == 0`) and F-17 `syncPosition` (phantom records) leave live dust uncovered by design; (d) equal-regime carve-out — note-only, unchanged: the Months 0–12 F-1/F-3 equal-split leg paying a zero-TVL Miliarium pool is spec-mandated §xxviii / H-D6 semantics, not a defect. No-fork-escalation rationale: the (vi) exit made fork-grade economics conditional on the unit pins leaving material doubt; they do not — every window's bound is an exact quantifier (wei-exact capture, the analytic `(59/61)^n` half-life, boundary-exact gates), not an estimate needing live-state confirmation, and the forward-direction (anti-pump) economics were already established on real mainnet state by the WK18 fork sim at PB2.11 (the reverse-direction drain rides the identical smoothing constant). The fork clause therefore does not fire; PB-D18 (vi) closes as-designed-with-evidence, the gates being the F-04/F-05/F-10 fixes already shipped. Closes roster item h; i (the family close) is next.

## PB-D19 — GENESIS_BLOCK = broadcast block + 100,800 (one epoch / 14 days): the emission clock decouples from deploy time; achieved via one env value, zero code change (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.4-pre, locked ahead of sequence)

**(i) Decision.** The production `GENESIS_BLOCK` env value is `broadcast_block + 100_800` — one `AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` (100,800 blocks = 14 days at Sepolia's 12s parity per PB-D1), not the broadcast block itself.
