The pad is the deploy-to-liquidity runway: AuMM's pre-genesis `return 0` (`AuMM.blockEmissionRate` L128) holds the entire F-0 schedule dormant until the padded block, so the faucet-funding + pool-initialization + EMA-seeding window burns zero schedule time. The offset is the locked quantity; the absolute value is computed at broadcast (PB3.5) from the then-current block, so no literal is committed now.

**(ii) Rationale — why decouple, and why 100,800.** Genesis-equals-deploy permanently forfeits the idle-window LP tranche: unseeded EMAs leave `totalScore == 0`, so the H-D15 empty-score guard advances `lastAccrualBlock` without accruing (`EmissionDistributor` L176 init + the L140 NatSpec) — the interval is skipped, never banked, and it is the richest month-0 F-0 A-leg. The Bodensee bootstrap tranche is formula-accrued and recoverable via `distribute()`; only the LP leg is lost. A future genesis eliminates the loss outright — the schedule starts pristine at the chosen block. 100,800 = one epoch is the natural unit (it matches the qualification cliff and the OQ-4 cadence); overshoot is a pure no-op (pre-genesis is dead time at zero cost), undershoot merely re-creates the genesis-equals-deploy forfeiture bounded to the shortfall. Stage-R-faithful: mainnet must never burn schedule while liquidity trails, so the posture is rehearsed, not worked around.

**(iii) Zero code change — verified 2026-07-17.** The posture is already supported by construction; achieving it touches ONE env value, no contract / script / fixture. Three facts traced at HEAD: (a) `script/DeployAuMM.s.sol` L33 reads `GENESIS_BLOCK` from env straight into the AuMM constructor — no `block.number` reference, no clamp; the emission contracts' constructor NatSpec accepts any `uint256` ("deploy-time correctness is governance's responsibility"). (b) The orchestrator's sole genesis assertion (`script/DeployStageP.s.sol` `_assertPostConditions` L143-152) is the P-D14 four-way CROSS-CONTRACT equality (gaugeRegistry == efficiencyOracle == emissionDistributor == AuMM), never a comparison against `block.number` — a future genesis satisfies it trivially because all four derive from `aumm.GENESIS_BLOCK()`, the single source. (c) The deploy path fires no schedule-clock action — zero `accrue` / `distribute` / `updateEMA` / `recordScore` / `poke` calls in any script — so a future genesis has nothing to collide with at deploy; the P9.5 binds + pilot `seedFoundingPool` (01/05/14) are registry writes only.

**(iv) PB3.4 rehearsal — the same knob as production.** The un-deferred `DeployStageP.run()` reads `GENESIS_BLOCK` from env, so the PB3.4 fork rehearsal runs WITH the future-offset value (broadcast + 100,800) and no fixture edit — the rehearsal exercises the exact production configuration. The existing fork fixtures stay genesis-equals-now, UNCHANGED (`new AuMM(block.number, ...)` at `StageGIntegration.t.sol` L150, `PilotPools.t.sol` L133, `AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol` L139, `SwapAndDepositToBodensee.t.sol` L93, `CCBEngine.t.sol` L141): they exercise mechanism via `vm.roll` relative to genesis, not launch timing, so a future offset would only force a pre-roll and prove nothing new. Requirement: PB3.4 does not exit until one rehearsal pass runs the orchestrated `run()` with a future GENESIS_BLOCK and re-confirms the four post-conditions green.

**(v) Evidence pins — the one path P10 never ran (optional, PB3.4-scoped, non-blocking).** The pre-genesis / H-D15-anchor interaction was never exercised by the P10 harness (its fixture ran genesis equal to block.number). Two targeted unit pins to add before relying on the posture: (1) a pre-genesis `_accrueGlobal` touch is an empty-interval no-op that snaps `lastAccrualBlock` forward without accruing (the L176 `lastAccrualBlock = genesisBlock_` init + the H-D15 empty-score short-circuit); (2) no pool score can mature to nonzero within the pad — the 60-day EMA maturity gate (432,000 blocks) exceeds the 100,800-block pad by design, so even a deploy-time EMA seed cannot confer score before genesis. Test-only, zero src change; PB-D-worthy as evidence discipline but does NOT gate the PB3.5 broadcast — a red would surface a mechanism surprise, not block the config.

## PB-D20 — PB3.2 lock: testnet token-stub architecture — base-level envOr override resolver with literal-derived STUB_ keys, Tier D = env-map, real Aureum RPs over 18/18 stub 4626s, two templates with named Tier-A instances, permissionless mint (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.2)

**(i) Resolution mechanism — base-level override resolver, one file.** Inside `deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol`'s existing TokenConfig-building loop, each `cfg.tokens[i]` and each `cfg.rateProviders[i]` resolves through `vm.envOr(key, original)` with the (ii) key derivation; default is passthrough — with no `STUB_*` env set the resolved address IS the config literal, so mainnet/fork behavior is byte-identical and the P10 suites run untouched. One file changes; the 26 config libraries (04/07 stay descoped per PB-D8/O-D6) and the 28 wrappers stay frozen. Overrides swap ADDRESSES only: `tokenTypes`, `normalizedWeights`, `paysYieldFees` are never touched, and the script-side QG re-assertion keeps reading the pre-override `cfg.*` values — class-faithful stubs then reproduce the mainnet QG sums at the factory. Fail-fast guard: an override resolving a non-zero WITH_RATE `rateProviders[i]` to `address(0)` reverts (a zeroed RP would flip both QG legs and Vault registration semantics). Rejected: per-config N-D7 extension (re-opens 26 config signatures + 26 wrappers pre-audit); on-chain registry lookup (adds a deploy dependency and still needs the base edit).

**(ii) Env-key derivation — fixed here so PB3.2e does not invent it.** `key = string.concat("STUB_", vm.toString(<address>))` — `vm.toString` renders the EIP-55 checksummed form with the `0x` prefix retained, e.g. the ixEDEL literal keys as STUB_0xe4a10951f962e6cB93Cb843a4ef05d2F99DB1F94; dotenv-legal (alphabetic start, alnum/underscore body). The base resolver and the Tier-D map emitter both derive through the same `vm.toString` call, so producer/consumer key drift is impossible by construction. The emitted map ALSO carries the existing NAMED protocol keys — `SV_ZCHF`, `SUSDS`, and the N-D7 RP keys (`WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER`, `YSYBOLD_RATE_PROVIDER`, `WOETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `SFRXETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `SCRVUSD_RATE_PROVIDER`) — because the protocol-level scripts (DeployDerBodensee, DeployStageG/H, the hook script) read named keys directly and never pass through the miliarium resolver.

**(iii) Sepolia RP posture + the F-11 decimals consequence.** Every WITH_RATE slot gets a REAL Aureum `ERC4626RateProvider` (P-D2 — RPs deploy real; audit-inheritance preserved) wrapping that slot's stub 4626; ixCasper slot-03's composite collapses on testnet to a plain `ERC4626RateProvider(stub waEthwstETH)` injected via the existing `WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER` key; zero new RP contract classes. F-11 binds 18/18 INCLUDING `asset()`: every RP-wrapped `StubERC4626` is 18-dec shares over an 18-dec stub-underlying `StubERC20` — even where the mainnet asset is 6-dec — a recorded testnet fidelity deviation (rate-path decimals normalize to 18/18; STANDARD raw-balance slots keep their real mainnet decimals). Tier-A underlyings (stub ZCHF, stub USDS) are roster extras beyond the 71 pool-config literals: they back svZCHF/sUSDS's `asset()` chain, the hook's `IERC4626.deposit(assets, receiver)` pull leg, and the PB2.2 TVLOracle hop roster.

**(iv) Templates + Tier-A packaging.** Exactly two templates land as the first files of `test-stubs/` (create-checked absent 2026-07-17): `StubERC4626.sol` — fixed 1:1 NAV per P-D23 (`convertToAssets(shares) = shares`, rate reads 1e18), the full read + `deposit`/`withdraw` surface the hook and the RP consume, constructor-set name/symbol/asset — and `StubERC20.sol` — constructor-set name/symbol/decimals. The P8.1 bespoke-contract packaging SOFTENS to named INSTANCES of these two templates: svZCHF and sUSDS deploy as StubERC4626 instances, ixEDEL as a plain 18-dec StubERC20 instance; the P-D23 shape lock (fixed 1:1 NAV; plain deterministic ixEDEL, no DTF re-implementation) holds unchanged.

**(v) Mint policy.** Permissionless uncapped `mint(address,uint256)` on both templates — testnet self-service for deploy seeding and frontend testers; distortable-testnet-economics accepted as rehearsal reality. Exact 4626 mint/deposit/withdraw mechanics (share-mint vs underlying-mint-and-deposit; withdraw bounded by held underlying) are PB3.2b Must-match detail, not re-adjudicated here.

**(vi) Tier D + build ladder.** Tier D resolves P8.4's "or" to the ENV-MAP arm — no on-chain `TestnetTokenRegistry.sol`: the stub-instance deploy script EMITS the committed Sepolia env fragment (the STUB_-literal pairs + the named protocol keys per (ii)). Ladder: a — this lock + the plan-side PB-D20 row/annotation; b — the two templates; c — template unit tests (4626 surface, 1:1 NAV, decimals, open mint, F-11 compatibility); d — the base resolver + the no-env-set byte-identity proof (P10 suites green untouched); e — the roster classification (the 71 config literals + the Tier-A extras: token-vs-RP, class, decimals read off the mainnet fork, user-run §8b) + the stub-instance deploy script + the emitted map; f — Completion Log rows + the §11 advance.

## PB-D21 — PB3.2e lock: testnet stub-instance deploy architecture — mainnet-fork iterate-the-real-configs materialization, drift-free STUB_ derivation, two RP override paths, fork-derive-now / broadcast-at-PB3.5 (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.2)

**(i) Deploy-script shape — one fork-run script, iterate the real configs.** `test-stubs/DeployTestnetStubs.s.sol` runs against a mainnet fork (the "read off the mainnet fork, user-run §8b" of PB-D20 (vi)) and materializes every testnet stub by iterating the 26 config libraries directly — 21 zero-arg `XxxConfig.config()` calls plus 5 placeholder-arg calls (the N-D7 param configs: `IxAetheronConfig.config(sfrxEthRp, wOethRp)`, `IxCasperConfig.config(waEthwstEthCompositeRp)`, `IxLibertasConfig.config(scrvUsdRp, ysyBoldRp)`, `IxMagnixConfig.config(ysyBoldRp)`, `IxAurixConfig.config(ysyBoldRp)`) — then processes each returned `PoolConfig`'s parallel `tokens[]` / `tokenTypes[]` / `rateProviders[]` arrays uniformly. Drift is structurally impossible per PB-D20 (ii): the producer derives each `STUB_` + `vm.toString(addr)` key from the SAME `cfg.tokens[i]` / `cfg.rateProviders[i]` literal the d1 resolver reads at deploy time, so a producer/consumer key mismatch cannot arise. Rejected: a hand-authored ~71-literal roster table, which re-introduces exactly the transcription drift the lock forbids. The 26 heterogeneous `config()` signatures are accepted as 26 explicit calls with uniform post-processing rather than refactoring the frozen 26 libs (PB-D20 (i)).

**(ii) Classification + dedup.** Restating PB-D20 (iii) as the materialization rule: a WITH_RATE token slot → a fresh 18-dec `StubERC20` underlying + a `StubERC4626` over it + a real `ERC4626RateProvider(stub4626)` (`getRate()` = 1e18), 18/18 regardless of the mainnet asset's decimals (the recorded F-11 testnet-fidelity deviation); a STANDARD token slot → a `StubERC20` at the token's REAL mainnet `decimals()` read off the fork — the only fork read the script needs. Dedup by mainnet address across all 26 pools: a token or RP appearing in multiple configs materializes ONE stub, first-seen wins, so the emitted map carries one pair per unique literal.

**(iii) Two RP override paths.** Hardcoded non-zero RP literals — the RP constants in the 21 pure configs (e.g. `FWSTETH_RATE_PROVIDER`) — override via `STUB_<mainnet_rp>` = the `ERC4626RateProvider` of that slot's token stub, resolved in the d1 loop. The 5 N-D7 named-key RPs (PB-D20 (ii): `SFRXETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `WOETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `YSYBOLD_RATE_PROVIDER`, `WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER`, `SCRVUSD_RATE_PROVIDER`) — which enter `cfg.rateProviders[i]` as `_config()` params, not literals — override via their NAMED keys = the same `ERC4626RateProvider`, NOT `STUB_` pairs (the d1 resolver sees the already-injected named-key value and passes through). The script maps each named key to its token via a fixed table (`sfrxETH` → SFRXETH, `wOETH` → WOETH, `yBOLD` → YSYBOLD, `waEthwstETH` → WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE, `scrvUSD` → SCRVUSD); every mainnet composite collapses to a plain `ERC4626RateProvider(stub)` on testnet per PB-D20 (iii).

**(iv) Tier-A named-token keys.** svZCHF / sUSDS are literal WITH_RATE cfg tokens with hardcoded RP constants, so they materialize through the (ii)/(iii) normal path; the script ADDITIONALLY emits them under the `SV_ZCHF` / `SUSDS` named keys the protocol-level scripts (hook, TVLOracle) read directly (PB-D20 (ii)). Their 18-dec `StubERC20` underlyings ARE the Tier-A stub ZCHF / stub USDS — the extras beyond the pool-config token literals — backing the svZCHF/sUSDS `asset()` chain, the hook's `IERC4626.deposit` pull leg, and the PB2.2 TVLOracle hop roster.

**(v) Broadcast posture + env-map artifact (address model user-adjudicated 2026-07-17: plain CREATE, real map at PB3.5).** Stubs deploy via plain CREATE — no CREATE2 / deterministic-factory machinery — so stub addresses are environment-specific, not stable across forks. e2 runs on a mainnet fork with NO broadcast solely to validate coverage (every WITH_RATE / STANDARD slot resolves to a stub) and to emit a fork-sample map as the structural artifact: its key set is canonical — the mainnet literals — while its values are throwaway fork addresses. The PB3.4 rehearsal and the PB3.5 broadcast each DEPLOY the stubs in-process and set the `STUB_` / named keys via `vm.setEnv` before the pool deploys read them; the rehearsal does not source the committed file. The real `test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` with live Sepolia addresses is regenerated and committed at the PB3.5 broadcast under per-command §8b approval, keeping every real-testnet broadcast in one gated place; the map carries the STUB_-literal pairs + the named keys (the 5 N-D7 + `SV_ZCHF` + `SUSDS`).

**(vi) Sub-rung split.** e1 — this lock. e2 — `test-stubs/DeployTestnetStubs.s.sol` + a mainnet-fork validation run asserting the roster covers every WITH_RATE and STANDARD slot across the 26 configs with zero passthrough leaks (no unresolved literal). e3 — the emitted `test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` (committed) + its coverage attestation. The f rung (Completion Log + §11 advance) is unchanged.

## PB-D22 — PB3.3 lock: canonical Router redeploy + the F-09 trusted-router seat — permit2 at Balancer's pin, P9-granular deploy script, Sepolia module-aim at the multisig, Aureum-branded version string (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.3)

**(i) permit2 dependency (§8c chat-level approval, 2026-07-17).** `Uniswap/permit2` installed at Balancer's own toolbox pin `cc56ad0f3439c502c246fc5cfcc3db92bb8b7219` (the rev `lib/balancer-v3-monorepo/pvt/solidity-toolbox/package.json` names), with an explicit `permit2/=lib/permit2/` line in `remappings.txt` (pin-every-dependency convention) and the `foundry.lock` rev entry. Interfaces-only: `Router` → `RouterCommon` imports `IPermit2` + `IAllowanceTransfer`; Aureum deploys no permit2 contract — the runtime instance is the canonical cross-chain `0x000000000022D473030F116dDEE9F6B43aC78BA3` (Sepolia presence verified at PB3.1). G10-class compile-probe green: the unmodified canonical `Router.sol` compiles under the pinned via_ir/9999 profile (9 files, zero warnings — no P-D27-class ICE). Landed at PB3.3a (`0a35646`). P-D26's zero-permit2 posture was Stage-P-scoped and is superseded on schedule; O-D5's "no contract to author" holds — the Router compiles from the pinned submodule, byte-unmodified.

**(ii) Deploy script.** `script/DeployRouter.s.sol` on the P9 granular pattern: `vm.envAddress` reads for `AUREUM_VAULT`, `WETH_ADDRESS`, `PERMIT2_ADDRESS` (all three explicit — no baked defaults, per the E-D23/P9 explicit-wiring posture), deploying `Router(IVault(vault), IWETH(weth), IPermit2(permit2), ROUTER_VERSION)` with the version string a script constant per (iv). WETH is environment-specific: mainnet WETH on the fork witness; the Sepolia WETH9 address is read and verified §8b at the PB3.5 broadcast, never hardcoded from memory.

**(iii) Sepolia module-aim (one-shot) — the multisig itself.** The hook's `_governanceAdmin` (seeded `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` at the hook deploy) fires `setGovernanceModule(GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG)`; the multisig then operates the persistent `setTrustedRouter` lever directly. Ordering per WH-P6 S10 (b): the orchestrator's `setAuMTContractForPool` recorder binds precede any seat — the module-aim + seat are post-orchestration steps at the PB3.4 rehearsal and the PB3.5 broadcast. Rejected for Sepolia: aiming at `AureumGovernance` — v1 carries no trusted-router proposal leg (FeeChange / GaugeChallenge / CompositionChallenge only), so the lever would park permanently, the Router could never seat, and the recorder dispatch would stay fail-closed dormant for the whole testnet phase; a dedicated RouterAdmin shim — a new authored contract + audit surface against the workstream-B posture. SCOPE: this binds the SEPOLIA hook instance only. The mainnet hook is a separate deployment whose one-shot fires at Stage R, informed by the Stage-Q A/B adjudication (the PB-D5 (v) allowlist-frozen vs new-leg input), which remains OPEN — nothing here pre-commits it.

**(iv) Version string.** Aureum-branded constant: "Aureum V3 Router v1 (Balancer V3 Router, pinned 68057fda)" — truthful provenance for an Aureum-operated deployment of the canonical bytecode. Matching Balancer's mainnet version blob verbatim rejected: identity-misleading for an Aureum instance, and a from-memory confabulation risk — the mainnet string can be cross-checked on Etherscan at PB3.5 verification without being copied.

**(v) Fork witness.** Realizes the production-router leg P-D26 (3) deferred to P-bis: on a mainnet fork, deploy the Router against the fork-deployed Aureum Vault + canonical permit2 + mainnet WETH; the multisig aims the module and seats the Router; a REAL Router liquidity add (the permit2 two-step approval flow) then drives the recorder — F-09 attribution credits the true LP, and a not-yet-seated router records nothing (fail-closed negative). Test-file shape and assertion roster are sized at the d-rung sub-step, not locked here.

**(vi) Build ladder.** a — the permit2 install (`0a35646`, DONE). b — this lock + the plan-side PB-D22 Decisions row / PB3.3 roster annotation. c — `script/DeployRouter.s.sol` + its compile check. d — the fork witness. e — Completion Log rows + the §11 advance. The live Sepolia sequence (deploy → aim → seat) executes at PB3.5 under per-command §8b approval.

## PB-D23 — PB3.4 lock: the production `run()` un-defer — composed sub-`run()` orchestration under an env-keyed governor, F/G/H run-entry normalization, the PB-D11 post-K VaultClassRegistry bind, mainnet-fork rehearsal at future genesis (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.4)

**(i) `run()` composition model.** The un-deferred `DeployStageP.run()` mirrors `deploy()`'s spine step-for-step but composes the sub-scripts' `run()` entries (each self-broadcasting as the env governor per (ii)); the orchestrator's own binds — `efficiencyOracle.setEmissionsRecorder`, the three pilot `seedFoundingPool` calls, the CCB seal, the post-K VaultClassRegistry bind per (iii) — fire under the orchestrator's own `vm.startBroadcast(governor)` blocks so their on-chain sender is the governor. Production performs NO `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` env override: `deploy()`'s `_setupEnv` writes `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG = address(this)` (the fork unified-governor identity) and would brick a broadcast, so the production path splits or bypasses it — the `VAULT_EXPLORER` / `SVZCHF` passthrough aliases may still be set, `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` is left alone — and `_assertBaseLayerGovernor` checks the base-layer authorizer against the ENV governor, not `address(this)`. Dual-path CCB seal, deliberately NOT unified: the fork `deploy()` keeps the `f.sealGaugeRegistry` forward (CREATE sender = the script, `gaugeRegistrySetter` pinned to `address(f)`, `sealAuthority` = the orchestrator); production `run()` seals via a DIRECT governor `ccbMultiplier.setGaugeRegistry(gaugeRegistry)` — under broadcast the CCBMultiplier's CREATE sender rewrites to the governor, so the one-shot setter authority lands on the signable identity and no forward exists on-chain. `deploy()`'s prank spine is otherwise untouched (P10 fixtures unaffected except the (iii) mirror).

**(ii) F/G/H run-entry normalization.** `DeployStageF` / `DeployStageG` / `DeployStageH` `run()` are the tree's only bare-`vm.startBroadcast()` + `_deploy(msg.sender)` entries — correct CLI-direct, broken under composition (msg.sender = the sim-local orchestrator would bake into on-chain ctor authority slots; it fails loud at the in-script handoffs, not silent). Normalize all three to the J/I/M/N/L/K convention: read `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` into `governor`, then `vm.startBroadcast(governor)` and `_deploy(governor)`. Zero in-tree callers of the three `run()`s (fixtures call `deploy()`), so the blast radius is the three run bodies alone; `deploy()` bodies untouched.

**(iii) The PB-D11 (iii) VaultClassRegistry bind — materialized in BOTH spines.** `vaultClassRegistry.setGovernanceContract` moves from its pre-L position (`DeployStageP.s.sol` L135) to AFTER `DeployStageK`, target `address(governance)` (K's returned instance) — never the multisig — in `deploy()` and `run()` alike, so fork validation matches production. Blast radius sized per I13: exactly one assertion — `StagePIntegration.t.sol` L562 flips to `address(orchestrator.governance())` and its "K never rebinds" comment rewrites; no other in-tree consumer reads the VCR governance seat against the orchestrator identity, and the PB2.6 dissolution witness carries no VCR premise. The PB-D11 (iv) residual is UNCHANGED: post-bind, `revokeVaultClass` has a live authorized caller and no typed `AureumGovernance` execute leg — the Stage-Q input stands, not reopened here.

**(iv) Sepolia governor identity — operational constraint, no code assert.** `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` on Sepolia = the funded deployer EOA `0xA851478dbee97375E784e9b98c0D7D599662bF85` (the PB3.1 identity), acting multisig for the testnet phase and the PB-D22 (iii) module-aim target. The PB3.5 broadcast key MUST equal this address (broadcast-as-governor signs the granular deploys and the orchestrator binds); recorded here as the ops constraint — forge fails a mismatched real broadcast naturally, so no in-script assert is added.

**(v) Rehearsal — mainnet fork, one process, future genesis.** The rehearsal chain runs on a MAINNET fork, not Sepolia: the PB-D21 stub derivation reads real mainnet `decimals()` off the fork (STANDARD slots), literals are P10-parity, D35/D36 posture holds. One-process sequence: stubs deployed in-process → `STUB_`/named env keys set → `GENESIS_BLOCK = fork block + 100_800` env (consumed at the base-layer `DeployAuMM`, NOT inside `DeployStageP.run()` — PB-D19 (iv)'s "run() reads GENESIS_BLOCK" wording means the FLOW reads it; the harness sets it before the base layer) → base layer via the granular `run()`s (STUB-resolved pools) → `DeployStageP.run()` → the four post-conditions re-asserted → `DeployRouter.run()` + the module-aim + `setTrustedRouter` seat (PB-D22 (iii)). Harness form: a fork-test file, with RUNG 1 a G10-class nesting probe — depth-2 `startBroadcast` through composed `run()`s has no in-tree precedent (the fixture nests one level only), so the probe lands before the harness shape commits; fallback if nested broadcast misbehaves in test context: the harness drives the sub-`run()` sequence directly around a `forge script`-invoked `DeployStageP.run()` fork dry-run (user-run, §8b). PB3.5 carry, NOT PB3.4-blocking: the stub-map regeneration on real Sepolia cannot read mainnet `decimals()` — the mechanism (a decimals table in the map, or a dual-fork read) is a PB3.5 pre-flight item.

**(vi) The PB-D19 (v) pins — promoted IN.** The two optional unit pins build at the PB3.4 e-rung: (1) a pre-genesis `_accrueGlobal` touch is an empty-interval no-op snapping `lastAccrualBlock` forward without accruing; (2) no pool score matures to nonzero within the 100,800-block pad (the 432,000-block EMA maturity gate exceeds it by design). Unit-scope, zero src change, non-blocking for PB3.5 per PB-D19 (v).

**(vii) Build ladder + supersession.** a — this lock + the plan-side PB-D23 Decisions row / PB3.4 roster annotation. b — the F/G/H run-entry normalization. c — the `run()` un-defer + the (iii) bind reorder in both spines + the L562 fixture mirror, P10 suite re-run fork-green. d — the nesting probe, then the rehearsal harness fork-green at future genesis. e — the two (vi) pins. f — Completion Log rows + the §11 advance. SUPERSEDED in part: the `run()` docstring's "production is per-granular-script Safe batches (P-bis, P-D23/P-D26)" — the base layer STAYS per-granular user-run at PB3.5; the F→K orchestration becomes the single composed `DeployStageP.run()`. (The docstring's P-D23/P-D26 cites are Stage-P codes, distinct from this PB-D23.)

## PB-D24 — PB-D23 (i)/(vii) amendment: the composed-`run()` handle-capture mechanism — extend the F/G/H public-storage exposure to J/K/L, assign in `_deploy`, split PB3.4c into c1/c2/c3 (user-adjudicated 2026-07-17; PB3.4c pre-flight blocker)

**(i) The finding — sub-script `run()` entries expose deployed handles asymmetrically.** PB-D23 (i) locks `DeployStageP.run()` to compose the sub-scripts' `run()` entries but never fixed HOW the orchestrator captures the handles those deployments produce — needed for env-chaining (F reads `MILIARIUM_REGISTRY`), the post-K binds (the VaultClassRegistry bind needs `governance`), and the four post-conditions (`governance` / `authorizer` / `gaugeRegistry` / `efficiencyOracle` / `emissionDistributor` / `ccbMultiplier`). At HEAD: F/G expose via public storage + a `run()` return (`f.tvlOracle()` … populated inside `_deploy`); H returns `emissionDistributor` and stores `bodenseeBootstrapChannel` (the `DeployStageP._orchestrate` L119 read); I/M/N are wiring-only (read env, no handle to capture). BLOCKERS: `DeployStageJ.run()` (L31-36) drops the `_deploy` return with no storage slot — so `MILIARIUM_REGISTRY` cannot be set for F; `DeployStageK.run()` (L76-87) discards all four `_deploy` locals — so the VaultClassRegistry bind and two post-conditions have no `governance` / `authorizer`. `DeployStageL.run()` (L77-82) discards its `IncendiaryRegistry` — SOFT (stored in the orchestrator for test parity, not functionally read inside `run()`). The production `run()` MUST call each sub's `run()` (self-broadcasting as the env governor), never `deploy()` — `vm.startPrank` records nothing under a real `--broadcast` and cannot nest inside the orchestrator's broadcast — so the handle must come back from the `run()` entry.

**(ii) Mechanism — Option 1: extend the F/G/H public-storage pattern to J/K/L.** Add public storage slots — `DeployStageJ.miliariumRegistry`, `DeployStageK` the four (`votingWeight` / `governance` / `authorizer` / `minterRouter`), `DeployStageL.incendiaryRegistry` — assigned inside `_deploy` / `_deployAndWire` (the F/G/H idiom: `miliariumRegistry = new MiliariumRegistry(...)` writes the slot), so BOTH entry points populate storage and `run()` needs no special branch. The orchestrator's `run()` then reads `j.miliariumRegistry()`, `k.governance()`, etc. after each sub-`run()`, exactly as `deploy()` reads `h.bodenseeBootstrapChannel()` today. K stores all four for one-to-one parity with the L140 `deploy()` mirror — hard needs are `governance` + `authorizer`; `votingWeight` / `minterRouter` are storage / post-condition parity. L is included for symmetry though soft. `run()` signatures stay void — public storage is the capture path, no return is added (F/G already return, harmless, not required and not extended to J/K/L). Additive only: the slots + one assignment relocation each; `_deploy` / wire logic and every `deploy()` return are unchanged; no test reads the new slots (fixtures capture via `deploy()` returns) → near-zero blast radius.

**(iii) Rejected alternatives.** (a) Sub-scripts `setEnv` their own handles — a broadcast-simulation-only side effect, murkier `run()` semantics. (b) The orchestrator computes CREATE addresses via `computeCreateAddress` — nonce-fragile under broadcast (the CREATE sender rewrites to the governor). (c) Handle-returning wrapper entry points — entry-point proliferation. All strictly worse than mirroring the F/G/H storage idiom already in tree.

**(iv) PB3.4c split (amends the PB-D23 (vii) single `c` rung — underspecified on capture, not scope creep).** c1 — J/K/L public-storage handle exposure (`_deploy`-side assignment); greppable standalone, no `DeployStageP` touch. c2 — `DeployStageP.deploy()` / `_orchestrate` VaultClassRegistry bind reorder (L135 pre-L → post-K, target `address(governance)`) + the `StagePIntegration.t.sol` L562 fixture mirror; P10-verifiable on the prank spine alone. c3 — the `run()` un-defer: replace the `ProductionOrchestrationDeferredToPbis` revert with the composed production flow (compose the sub-`run()`s + capture the c1 handles + the orchestrator's own `vm.startBroadcast(governor)` bind blocks + the `_setupEnv` split leaving `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` alone + the `_assertBaseLayerGovernor` env-governor variant + the direct-governor CCB seal per PB-D23 (i)), needing the c1 handles and the c2 bind order. The d / e / f rungs (nesting probe + rehearsal, the PB-D19 (v) pins, Completion Log + §11) are unchanged.

## PB-D25 — PB3.4d2 lock: the production-`run()` rehearsal harness — standalone mainnet-fork fixture, real-EOA governor, in-process stub replay at future genesis (user-adjudicated 2026-07-18; PB3.4d)

**(i) Purpose and the two adjudicated axes.** PB-D23 (v) specifies the rehearsal one-process sequence (stubs → env → future `GENESIS_BLOCK` → base layer via granular deploys → `DeployStageP.run()` → the four post-conditions re-asserted → `DeployRouter.run()` + module-aim + `setTrustedRouter`) but left two mechanism axes open; both resolved here. **(a) Governor identity — a real EOA**, distinct from the harness and the orchestrator, held in a fixture constant and written into `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` before the base layer is built. The base-layer `AureumAuthorizer` bakes that EOA into its `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` slot (a constructor arg, not the CREATE sender — so the harness may still create the base layer itself), `_assertBaseLayerGovernorProduction` then passes against it, and the composed sub-`run()`s plus the orchestrator's own bind blocks all broadcast as that EOA. This faithfully exercises the `run()`-vs-`deploy()` distinction — production reads a real governor, never `address(this)` — which an `address(orchestrator)` governor would blur (rejected, (iv)). No funding is needed: test broadcasts are simulated, not gas-paying. **(b) Base-layer source — a standalone duplicate.** A new fork-test fixture that does NOT inherit `StagePIntegrationFixture`; it duplicates the ~200-line base-layer assembly with the three stub/governor/genesis deltas baked in. Zero P10 touch — `StagePIntegrationFixture` (the P10 close gate, 13/13) stays byte-identical and is not re-run as a regression surface. The duplication cost is accepted for that isolation (the fixture-refactor-with-seams alternative rejected, (iv)); the base layer is P-D31 Tier-A scaffolding for the thing under test (`run()` + the Router leg), not itself the subject.

**(ii) Stub replay mechanism — in-process `DeployTestnetStubs` + one additive getter.** The harness runs `DeployTestnetStubs.run()` in `setUp` on the mainnet fork (STANDARD slots read real `decimals()` per PB-D21 (ii)), then replays its full emission as env: it loops the `(key, val)` pairs `DeployTestnetStubs` already accumulates in `envKeys`/`envVals` — both the `STUB_<literal>` pairs and the named keys (`SV_ZCHF`, `SUSDS`, `WAETHWSTETH_COMPOSITE_RATE_PROVIDER`, `SFRXETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `WOETH_RATE_PROVIDER`, `YSYBOLD_RATE_PROVIDER`, `SCRVUSD_RATE_PROVIDER`) — and `vm.setEnv`s each. This is the same emission the e2 map captured into `sepolia-stubs.env` and the same the PB3.5 Sepolia broadcast will consume, so the rehearsal and the live deploy read one drift-free source per PB-D20 (ii). It requires the only change to an existing file in PB3.4d2: an additive public getter on `DeployTestnetStubs` exposing the pairs (`envPairCount()` + `envPairAt(uint256) returns (string memory, address)`); `run()` and every existing behavior are untouched (the d2a rung). The roster already covers the der-Bodensee base-layer tokens — susds and svZchf are WITH_RATE Miliarium tokens (ixHelvetia / ixCasper), so their stub 4626s land as the `SUSDS` / `SV_ZCHF` named keys and their real RPs (0x1195… / 0xf32d…) land as `STUB_` keys — so the harness stubs nothing separately: the bodensee configs read the stub 4626s off the named keys and their stub RPs off the `STUB_` keys of the real RP literals.

**(iii) The four deltas from `StagePIntegrationFixture`.** The standalone harness mirrors the fixture's base-layer assembly except: (1) `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` = the real EOA governor, not `address(orchestrator)` — flowing into the authorizer slot, the hook `moduleAdmin` / `pauseManager`, and the AuMM `minterAdmin`; (2) the `STUB_`/named replay of (ii) precedes every pool deploy, so `_resolveStub` / `_resolveRateProvider` swap in the stub roster and the fixture's inline real-token RP creations (sfrxETH / wOETH / scrvUSD / ysyBOLD / the waEthwstETH composite) are replaced by the replayed named keys; (3) `GENESIS_BLOCK = block.number + 100_800` (one epoch, PB-D19), not `block.number` — the four post-conditions assert cross-contract genesis EQUALITY, so the uniform future offset propagates and they still hold; (4) the test body calls `orchestrator.run()` (the composed production spine), not `orchestrator.deploy()` (the prank spine). The Router leg then appends `DeployRouter.run()` + the governor's one-shot module-aim-at-self + `setTrustedRouter` (PB-D22 (iii)), reusing the RouterIntegration seat pattern but with `gov` = the EOA governor.

**(iv) Rejected alternatives.** (a) `address(orchestrator)` as governor — matches the fixture convention and needs no distinct identity, but broadcasts as a contract, blurring the exact real-governor semantics the `run()` path exists to rehearse; the EOA is barely more code and strictly more faithful. (b) Refactoring `StagePIntegrationFixture` with virtual/override seams (governor / stub-vs-real / genesis) — DRY and one base-layer source of truth, but it touches the P10 close-gate fixture and forces a full 13/13 regression re-run to prove no perturbation; the isolation of an untouched P10 gate outweighs the duplication. (c) Re-deriving the stub env inside the harness (re-reading configs, re-deploying stubs by hand) — reintroduces the drift `DeployTestnetStubs` was built to eliminate; the additive getter replays the authoritative emission instead.

**(v) Build-ladder decomposition (amends the PB-D23 (vii) single `d` rung — the nesting probe was d1; this is d2).** d2a — the additive `envPairCount` / `envPairAt` getter on `DeployTestnetStubs` (additive, `forge build` green, no behavior change). d2b — the standalone rehearsal-harness fixture: `setUp` running the in-process stub replay + the future-`GENESIS_BLOCK` + the EOA-governor base-layer assembly (split further at authoring if the ~200-line assembly exceeds one baby-step — d2b1 setUp / stub / env, d2b2 base-layer). d2c — the test body: `orchestrator.run()` + the four post-condition re-assertions, fork-green (`--fork-url mainnet --threads 1` per D35/D36). d2d — the Router leg: `DeployRouter.run()` + module-aim + `setTrustedRouter` + a seated-add smoke assertion. The e-rung (the two PB-D19 (v) pre-genesis unit pins) and the f-rung (Completion Log + §11) are unchanged from PB-D23 (vii).

## PB-D26 — PB3.4d2c amendment to PB-D20 (i): the stub override must reorder, not only re-address — an unconditional ascending sort with lockstep weight permutation (user-adjudicated 2026-07-25; PB3.4d2c fork-run blocker)

**(i) The defect, and the run that surfaced it.** The first end-to-end rehearsal fork run (`test/fork/StagePRunRehearsal.t.sol`, at PB3.4d2b2b) reverted in `setUp` with the Vault's `TokensNotSorted()`. `deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol`'s `run()` assembles its `TokenConfig[]` in raw config-array order (L69–L77), substituting `_resolveStub(cfg.tokens[i])` slot by slot, and hands `cfg.normalizedWeights` to `create()` unpermuted (L109); the script contains no sort. The 26 config libraries are authored in ascending mainnet-address order — precisely the ordering Balancer V3 pool registration requires — but the PB-D20 resolver swaps each literal for a freshly-CREATEd stub whose address is uncorrelated with the original, destroying that invariant slot-wise. The run's own emitted stub map is the proof: ixHelvetia resolved to (`0x037e…`, `0xfD07…`) and ixEdelweiss to (`0x072d…`, `0x3B45…`, `0x839E…`, `0xfD07…`), both incidentally ascending and both registered, while ixAurebit resolved to (WBTC `0xa11E…`, Aave Prime GHO `0xcea2…`, cbBTC `0x7db8…`, ixEDEL `0x839E…`, svZCHF `0xfD07…`), where `0xcea2` exceeding `0x7db8` breaks the order at the third slot and reverts. The two passes were luck — one chance in two and one in twenty-four — not design; every multi-token pool is exposed, which is all 26.

**(ii) Scope — a production-path defect, not a rehearsal artifact.** The failing surface is `script/pools/deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol`, the single base script all 26 Miliarium pool deploys inherit, and the stub roster it mis-orders is the same one the PB3.5 Sepolia broadcast consumes. The rehearsal is the messenger, not the patient: absent this fix the live testnet deploy fails identically at the first pool whose resolved addresses are not ascending, and fails mid-broadcast against a funded deployer rather than in a test. That is the PB-D23 (v) rehearsal earning its place — the defect surfaces in a fork run at zero cost instead of in the PB3.5 broadcast window.

**(iii) Why neither standing gate caught it.** The P10 close gate (`StagePIntegration.t.sol`, 13/13) runs with no `STUB_` key set, so `_resolveStub` is pure passthrough per PB-D20 (i) and pool registration sees the mainnet literals in their authored order — the ordering hazard is structurally invisible on that path. The PB3.2e2 stub-roster fork validation exercised `DeployTestnetStubs` end to end and asserted its emission (67 `STUB_` pairs, 7 named keys, zero passthrough leaks) but never drove a pool registration through the resulting map, so it proved the addresses resolve and never that they order. The defect lived exactly in the seam between the two gates; the rehearsal is the first surface that composes both.

**(iv) The fix — an unconditional ascending sort with lockstep weight permutation.** `run()` sorts the resolved `TokenConfig[]` and the `normalizedWeights[]` together, ascending by resolved token address, immediately before `create()`. The permutation is lockstep and non-negotiable: `normalizedWeights` is a parallel array indexed by slot, so sorting tokens without carrying weights would silently seat one leg's weight on a different token — a mispriced pool that registers cleanly and reverts nothing, strictly worse than the `TokensNotSorted()` it replaces. `tokenType`, `rateProvider` and `paysYieldFees` travel inside the `TokenConfig` struct and permute with it for free. The Quality-Gate loop (L79–L84) is an order-independent sum over `cfg.*` by index, so it stays where it is, reading pre-override config exactly as PB-D20 (i) specifies.

**(v) Why the mainnet path is provably unperturbed.** With no `STUB_` key set `_resolveStub` returns its argument unchanged, so the resolved array IS `cfg.tokens` — already ascending by authorship. A sort applied to an already-sorted sequence is the identity, and the accompanying weight permutation is likewise the identity, so the mainnet and mainnet-fork paths produce byte-identical `create()` arguments before and after this change. The claim is checked rather than asserted: the P10 close gate re-runs at the verification rung and must return 13/13 unchanged. This is also why the sort is unconditional rather than gated on whether an override applied — an always-on no-op is exercised by every existing mainnet-path test, whereas a testnet-only branch would ship logic no standing gate ever executes.

**(vi) Rejected alternatives.** (a) Gating the sort behind a stub-applied predicate — keeps the mainnet path untouched by construction rather than by no-op reasoning, and is marginally easier to argue to an auditor, but confines the sorting logic to the one path with the least coverage; the unconditional form is preferred precisely because the mainnet tests exercise it. (b) Grinding stub addresses via CREATE2 salts so each pool's resolved slots preserve config order — leaves every deploy script untouched and keeps PB-D20 (i) literally true, but reverses the PB-D21 (v) plain-CREATE lock, adds real deploy cost and complexity, and is likely unsatisfiable: tokens recur across pools (svZCHF appears in most of the roster), so a single stub address would have to satisfy every containing pool's ordering simultaneously. (c) Re-authoring the 26 config libraries into a stub-friendly order — the configs mirror canonical mainnet addresses and their order is a property of those addresses, not a free choice; no single order is correct for both the real and the stub roster.

**(vii) The PB-D20 (i) amendment, the ladder, and the gates.** PB-D20 (i)'s standing wording — that the override touches swap ADDRESSES only, while `tokenTypes`, `normalizedWeights`, `paysYieldFees` and the QG re-assertion keep reading pre-override `cfg.*` — is amended here: the override still reads no overridden VALUE into those arrays, but `normalizedWeights` is now REORDERED in lockstep with the tokens it indexes, and the script's L42–L46 NatSpec is corrected to say so at the fix rung. Ladder: d2c1 — the sort plus the NatSpec correction in `deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol`, `forge build` green. d2c2 — verification in one rung on two gates: the P10 close gate re-run at 13/13 (the mainnet-invariance proof of (v)) and the rehearsal `setUp` clearing all 26 pool deploys. d2c3 — the four post-condition re-assertions in the rehearsal test body, the original PB-D25 (v) d2c content, fork-green. The d2d Router leg and the e / f rungs are unchanged.

## PB-D27 — PB3.5 lock: the Sepolia go-live — a committed decimals table, a multi-broadcast runbook with EOA-scoped cycle prediction, the DeployDerBodensee stub-RP adaptation, and the upstream-factory testnet disposition (user-adjudicated 2026-07-26; PB3.5)

**(i) Scope.** PB3.5 is the live Sepolia broadcast plus explorer verification — no new protocol surface. The base layer STAYS per-granular user-run per PB-D23 (vii); the F-to-K orchestration stays the single composed `DeployStageP.run()`. REJECTED: collapsing the base layer into `DeployStageP` or any mega-orchestrator that deploys both, which would rewrite PB-D23 (vii) to buy a convenience the runbook already provides. Every broadcast command is user-run under per-command §8b approval; §8c keeps mainnet banned, and D-D6's Stage-R mainnet reservation is untouched by anything here.

**(ii) The decimals artifact — a committed table, fail-fast, no uniform-18.** Discharges the PB-D23 (v) PB3.5 carry. `_ensureStandard` (`test-stubs/DeployTestnetStubs.s.sol` L130) reads `IERC20Metadata(token).decimals()` off the mainnet literal — the single fork read of PB-D21 (ii) — and on live Sepolia that literal has no code, so the staticcall returns empty and the decode reverts. Mechanism: the mainnet-fork derivation run additionally emits one `DECIMALS_<mainnet_literal>=N` pair per STANDARD slot, committed alongside the address map; the Sepolia stub deploy reads the table with NO default and reverts on a missing key. The fork derivation remains the single source of truth per PB-D21 (i), so producer/consumer drift stays structurally impossible. REJECTED: (a) uniform 18-decimal stubs for STANDARD slots — discards the decimal fidelity PB-D21 (ii) deliberately preserved, and PB-D26 is the standing evidence that latent decimals and ordering assumptions surface at `create()` time rather than at review time; (b) a dual-fork read inside the broadcast script — dual-RPC state in a broadcasting process, for a value already derivable offline.

**(iii) The multi-broadcast runbook — cross-process env threading.** PB-D21 (v)'s "the PB3.5 broadcast DEPLOYs the stubs in-process ... via `vm.setEnv`" is rehearsal-true and live-false: `vm.setEnv` mutates one process, and each `forge script --broadcast` is its own process reading `.env` at start. AMENDED for the live case: addresses persist to `.env` between invocations, never through `vm.setEnv`. The runbook is a committed operator document at `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_RUNBOOK.md` — cited from the plan and the Completion Log, never inlined into `STAGE_P_BIS_PLAN.md`, because its audience is the broadcast operator rather than the stage reader — naming (a) the ordered invocation list, (b) for each step, which stdout line or `broadcast/*.json` field becomes which `.env` key, (c) that the regenerated stub map is merged into `.env` before `DeployDerBodensee`, which is the first consumer of both the named token keys `SV_ZCHF` / `SUSDS` and, after (v), the `STUB_` rate-provider keys, (d) that `.env` stays gitignored and untracked — verified true at lock time — with `DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY` never committed and never echoed. The emitted stub map is already `.env`-shaped (`DeployTestnetStubs` L175), so the capture is a redirect, not a parser. A `broadcast/*.json` rewriter stays OUT of the PB-D27 mechanism: it may land later as an operator convenience, but the auditable primary path is explicit capture-and-write, one §8b beat per broadcast.

**(iv) The base-layer address cycle — EOA-scoped prediction, verified by a Sepolia-fork dry run.** Under `--broadcast` forge never puts the script contract on-chain: the inner CREATEs are transactions from the deployer EOA, so every rehearsal prediction formula is structurally wrong live. `computeCreateAddress(address(hookScript), 1)` and the harness-creator CREATE3 (`StagePRunRehearsal.t.sol` L130-L138) are harness artifacts, not production formulas. The live cycle is four-deep, not two: `DeployAureumVault` reads BOTH `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` and `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` (L127-L128); the hook needs the vault; der Bodensee's CREATE3 final salt is `keccak256(abi.encode(msg.sender, block.chainid, BODENSEE_SALT))` with `msg.sender` the EOA under broadcast and the FACTORY as CREATE3 creator (`BasePoolFactory.sol` L126-L127); the factory needs the vault. So the hook and Bodensee predictions must project forward past the vault step's own three EOA CREATEs — `AureumAuthorizer`, `AureumProtocolFeeController`, `AureumVaultFactory`, with the Vault itself a factory-side CREATE3 (`DeployAureumVault.s.sol` L177-L214) — and re-reading the live nonce between steps does not escape that projection. `DeployAureumVault.run()`'s own `msg.sender` prediction (L95-L97) is already live-correct; only its INPUTS need EOA-scoped derivation. Mechanism, three parts: (1) the runbook pins the exact EOA transaction count of every step, derived from a dry run rather than read off the source; (2) a Sepolia-fork dry run at the REAL deployer's live nonce executes the entire sequence and asserts every predicted address against the actual CREATE, run immediately before the live broadcast — a mainnet-fork rehearsal cannot substitute, the nonce is wrong; (3) script-side self-asserts are ADOPTED in addition to (2), not instead of it: `DeployDerBodensee` asserts the created pool equals `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` and `DeployFeeRoutingHook` asserts the deployed hook equals `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK`. Both keys already hold the prediction by then, because the vault step consumed them, so the assert costs one env read and no new env surface, and it converts a silent mid-sequence divergence into an immediate abort. Accepted failure mode, recorded: a mis-projection seals the vault's immutables before it is observable, so recovery is a full sequence restart on fresh addresses — tolerable on testnet gas, and the reason (2) is mandatory rather than optional.

**(v) `DeployDerBodensee` — stub-RP adaptation, not a Sepolia descope.** The file hardcodes the two mainnet rate providers (L108 sUSDS `0x1195BE91e78ab25494C855826FF595Eef784d47B`, L115 svZCHF `0xf32dc0eE2cC78Dca2160bb4A9B614108F28B176c`), so as written it would register stub tokens against addresses carrying no Sepolia code. CHOSEN: adapt the script, so the committed production path and the broadcast path stay one file. REJECTED: descoping the script for Sepolia and inlining a runbook `create()` — that recreates the P-D34 "the fixture bypasses the script" gap on a live chain, the precise coverage hole clause (vi) exists to close. The adaptation is narrow and drift-free: both literals ALREADY carry `STUB_` keys in the committed map (`test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` L8, L10), emitted through PB-D21 (iii)'s hardcoded-literal path from the same cfg constants the miliarium resolver reads, shared across several of the 26 configs rather than unique to one. Shape: mirror `_resolveStub` / `_resolveRateProvider` (`deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol` L50-L63) onto the two rate-provider slots; `_tokenConfig` goes `private pure` to `private view` for the cheatcode env reads; `_normalizedWeight` stays `pure`. Dual-use is deliberate and must not be tightened: `_resolveStub` is `envOr`, so a missing `STUB_` key passes the mainnet literal through unchanged, which is correct for Stage R and silent failure on Sepolia — the mitigation is (iii)(c), the map loaded before `DeployDerBodensee`, NOT converting these reads to fail-fast `envAddress`, which would oblige Stage R to publish an identity map for keys it has no reason to set. Everything else in the file is already live-correct and stays byte-identical: the runtime ascending sort (L46-L52) already satisfies the PB-D26 invariant, the `create()` argument list matches the rehearsal's inline call exactly, and the three tokens are already env-keyed. Recorded testnet-fidelity consequence: both yield-bearing legs keep `paysYieldFees: true` against fixed-1:1-NAV `StubERC4626` rate providers, so yield fees accrue nothing on Sepolia — the same F-11 deviation PB-D21 (ii) already records, not a new one.

**(vi) Pre-broadcast fork witness — mandatory.** `DeployDerBodensee.run()` has zero coverage on every path: the P10 fixture and the PB3.4 rehearsal both inline `wpf.create(...)` for the creator-scoped CREATE3 reason P-D34 records. A witness drives the SCRIPT rather than an inline create, with stub tokens and stub rate providers on a fork, asserting the pool registers, the three legs land in ascending address order with weights in lockstep, and the resolved rate providers are the stub instances rather than the mainnet literals. This is the same class of hole PB-D26 caught for pool ordering, and it lands before the broadcast regardless of how (v) resolves.

**(vii) Ops gates — measurement before accumulation.** Order is fixed: measure, then grind. (1) A gas measurement on the `StagePRunRehearsal` spine (user-run, §8b) fixes the SepETH target as a number; fork gas is not Sepolia gas exactly but bounds the order of magnitude, and the live decision is 0.05 SepETH on hand against 67 stubs plus 26 pool create-and-initialize pairs plus roughly 20 stack contracts. This discharges the PB-D19 gas carry, which deferred the budget to the PB3.4 rehearsal. (2) `ETHERSCAN_API_KEY` provisioned before verification — empty in `.env` at lock time, and roughly 100 contracts need it. (3) `DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY` handling: keystore versus paste-at-broadcast is recorded here as a posture, never the key itself; the key is entered by the user alone and stays off-repo per PB3.1. (4) `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` on Sepolia equals the funded deployer EOA `0xA851478dbee97375E784e9b98c0D7D599662bF85` per PB-D23 (iv); the broadcast key MUST be that address, enforced operationally rather than by an in-script assert.

**(viii) D-D6 NatSpec reconciliation — housekeeping, non-blocking.** Four scripts carry flat "fork-only scope; no production broadcast path" NatSpec (`DeployFeeRoutingHook.s.sol` L26, `DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory.s.sol` L14, `DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` L19, `DeployStageF.s.sol` L43). D-D6 itself (`STAGE_D_PLAN.md` L88) reserves MAINNET for Stage R and says nothing about testnets, so a Sepolia broadcast does not violate it — but the wording goes stale the moment PB3.5 broadcasts these files. A one-line-per-file Sepolia-clarifying amendment lands with the runbook, ahead of the first live broadcast. Gates nothing.

**(ix) `AureumWeightedPoolFactory` — Sepolia deploys the upstream factory; the Aureum factory identity is a named Stage-R gate.** No granular script deploys `src/factory/AureumWeightedPoolFactory.sol`: `script/DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory.s.sol` L37 constructs the UPSTREAM `WeightedPoolFactory` bound to the Aureum Vault — the E-D16 naming trap — and both production-shaped spines cast that address to the Aureum type (`StagePIntegration.t.sol` L200, `StagePRunRehearsal.t.sol` L208), while only the StageG, PilotPools and CCBEngine fork fixtures plus the unit suite construct the real contract. The two `create()` parameter lists are identical, so the selector collides and `AureumWeightedPoolFactory(factoryAddr).create(...)` (`deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol` L113) dispatches successfully against an upstream instance and executes the UPSTREAM body: the factory-level `QualityGateUnsatisfied` guard (`AureumWeightedPoolFactory.sol` L100) is skipped SILENTLY rather than reverting. Consequence, recorded plainly: every Miliarium pool in the P10 13/13 close gate and in the 9/9 PB3.4 rehearsal was created without factory-level QG. Admitted-set outcomes are nevertheless unaffected — `MIN_ERC4626_WEIGHT = 52e16` is duplicated verbatim in the script-side loop (`deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol` L27, enforced L84-L90) and in the factory (L30) — so what is absent is the second, contract-level barrier, not the gate. CHOSEN for Sepolia: the upstream factory serves both der Bodensee and the 26 Miliarium pools, one address in both `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` and `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY`, script-side QG operative — exactly the configuration the two validated spines exercised, so the broadcast rehearses a proven path rather than a newly wired one. The decisive constraint against deploying the real Aureum factory at P-bis: `GaugeEligibility.approvedFactory` is a SINGLETON immutable (`GaugeEligibility.sol` L26, L175) hard-enforced through `isPoolFromFactory` (L373, L391), and `DeployStageG.s.sol` L118 reads it from `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` — the same key `DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` L38 reads for the Bodensee factory. Two factories therefore require splitting an overloaded env key AND changing a `DeployStageG` env read, re-opening P10-validated deploy plumbing pre-audit — the precise cost PB-D13 declined for D36 — days before a broadcast, to add a barrier whose threshold is already enforced upstream of it. Recorded as a pre-mainnet gate rather than built: Stage R deploys the real Aureum factory, requiring (1) the `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` / `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` overload split into distinct Bodensee-factory and pool-factory keys, (2) `DeployStageG` reading the pool-factory key so `approvedFactory` matches the Miliarium pools' actual creator, (3) a determination whether der Bodensee needs `isPoolFromFactory` provenance anywhere, since under a split it is no longer from the approved factory. A new RB register row carries the trap itself: the Aureum factory exposes no positive identity marker, so any future mis-set factory key silently disables its quality gate. `DeployStageG.s.sol` L56's NatSpec, which names `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` as the AureumWeightedPoolFactory, is false on every existing path and is corrected at the (viii) rung.

**(x) Build ladder.** a1 — this lock. a2 — the plan-side PB-D27 Decisions row plus the PB3.5 roster annotation. b — the (ii) decimals table: the mainnet-fork emission extension, the committed artifact, the Sepolia STANDARD-path consumer read with no default, and the fork re-run attestation. c1 — the (v) `DeployDerBodensee` stub-RP adaptation plus its (iv)(3) self-assert. c2 — the (iv)(3) `DeployFeeRoutingHook` self-assert. d — the (vi) fork witness. e — the (iii) runbook document at `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_RUNBOOK.md`, carrying the (iv) prediction model, the per-step EOA transaction counts, the capture-to-`.env` key map, the (viii) NatSpec one-liners and the (ix) `DeployStageG` L56 correction. f — the (vii)(1) gas measurement (user-run, §8b) recorded into the runbook, fixing the SepETH target. g — the (ix) RB register row. h — the (iv)(2) Sepolia-fork dry run at the live deployer nonce, every prediction asserted; the unrecoverable-immutables gate, never folded into another rung. i — the live broadcast (user-run, per-command §8b), the regenerated `test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` committed with live addresses per PB-D21 (v), and explorer verification. j — Completion Log rows plus the CLAUDE.md §11 advance.

## PB-D28 — PB-D27 (iv)(1)/(x) amendment: the per-step EOA transaction counts defer from rung e to rung h — the runbook lands with the derivation method and an unpopulated count table (user-adjudicated 2026-07-26; PB3.5e pre-flight blocker)

**(i) The tension.** PB-D27 (iv)(1) requires the runbook to pin the exact EOA transaction count of every broadcast step, derived from a dry run rather than read off the source. PB-D27 (x) places the runbook at rung e and the Sepolia-fork dry run at rung h — four rungs later — and forbids folding h into another rung. As written the two clauses cannot both hold: rung e must either run a dry run early, which is the h rung under another name, or read the counts off the source, which (iv)(1) rejects by name.

**(ii) Resolution — counts defer to h; everything else lands at e.** The two quantities the runbook carries separate cleanly. A step's EOA transaction count is STRUCTURAL: it follows from that script's own CREATE and call sequence and is invariant under the deployer's nonce. A predicted address is NONCE-DEPENDENT, which is precisely why (iv)(2) pins the dry run to the REAL deployer's live nonce immediately before the broadcast. Only the second genuinely needs h. Rung e therefore authors the runbook complete in every other respect and carries a count table that is present, structurally complete down to one row per step, and EMPTY — every count cell reading a literal PENDING-h marker — alongside the derivation method h will execute. Rung h's scope grows by exactly one obligation: derive each count from its own run and populate the table before any broadcast command is issued. h remains the single unrecoverable-immutables gate and is still never folded into another rung.

**(iii) Rejected.** (a) A counting-only simulation inserted ahead of e — a new §8b beat running each script under `forge script` without `--broadcast` against Sepolia on placeholder env. Two steps defeat it: the c1b `BodenseeAddressMismatch` and c2 `HookAddressMismatch` self-asserts revert under any placeholder prediction that is not already the answer, so those counts would be read out of reverted traces rather than clean runs — and the beat produces nothing h does not produce anyway, at the cost of a second network round against a chain whose state the run never advances. (b) Source-derived counts written now and flagged unconfirmed. (iv)(1) rejects source-reading by name and the reason survives the flag: a number on the page is a number an operator can act on, and the failure mode of an undercount is a mis-projected address sealing a vault immutable — the exact unrecoverable class (iv) exists to convert into an abort. An empty cell carrying a PENDING marker cannot be misread as an answer; an unconfirmed number can.

**(iv) What rung e still delivers, unchanged.** The ordered invocation list; the per-step capture map naming which stdout line or `broadcast/*.json` field becomes which `.env` key; the requirement that the regenerated stub map merge into `.env` before `DeployDerBodensee`, its first consumer; the `.env` hygiene statement, re-verified 2026-07-26 — `.gitignore` L12 carries `.env`, `git ls-files` shows only `.env.example` tracked, and `DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY` is never committed and never echoed; the (iv) prediction model in prose, including the PB3.5d finding that a bare `vm.startBroadcast()` resolves the CREATE3 final salt against the broadcast sender rather than against the script contract; the (viii) NatSpec one-liners; and the (ix) `DeployStageG.s.sol` L56 correction. The runbook is operable end to end at rung e for every purpose except gas-budget arithmetic, which is rung f's `StagePRunRehearsal` measurement and is separately deferred there.

**(v) Ladder.** e0a — this lock. e0b — the plan-side PB-D28 Decisions row. e1 — the runbook document at `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_RUNBOOK.md` per (iv). e2 — the (viii) D-D6 NatSpec one-liners across `DeployFeeRoutingHook.s.sol` L26, `DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory.s.sol` L14, `DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` L19 and `DeployStageF.s.sol` L43. e3 — the `DeployFeeRoutingHook.s.sol` orchestrator-attribution corrections deferred out of PB3.5c2 as c2b. e4 — the (ix) `DeployStageG.s.sol` L56 correction. Rungs f through j are unchanged except that h gains the (ii) count-population obligation.

## PB-D29 — PB-D27 (iv)(2) amendment: the pre-broadcast dry run is two-phase — counts sourced chain-invariantly, chained projections computed analytically, per-step pre-flight interleaved with the broadcast (user-adjudicated 2026-07-26; PB3.5h pre-flight blocker)

**(i) Mechanism chosen — `forge script` simulation, not a fork-test harness.** PB-D27 (iv)(2) calls for a Sepolia-fork dry run at the REAL deployer's live nonce. CHOSEN: `forge script <path> --rpc-url $SEPOLIA_RPC_URL --sender <deployer>` WITHOUT `--broadcast` — the live code path minus the send, so no harness-versus-production divergence has to be reasoned about, and forge reports the transaction list it would have sent. REJECTED: a `test/fork/SepoliaDryRun.t.sol` harness in the shape of `StagePRunRehearsal`. Its disqualifying gap: live, every EOA transaction advances the nonce, CREATEs and plain calls alike; in fork-test EVM semantics only CREATE advances it, and `DeployStageP.run()` alone fires many setter calls. A harness measuring nonce deltas would undercount every call-making step and reproduce exactly the harness-artifact class PB-D27 (iv) exists to eliminate, at the one gate whose failure mode is unrecoverable.

**(ii) Three fidelity semantics are already witnessed; no probe rung is needed.** Broadcast attribution rewrites plain-CREATE origin to the broadcaster — `DeployAureumVault` predicts `computeCreateAddress(msg.sender, nonce + 2)` (L163) and self-asserts `FactoryAddressMismatch` (L203-L205), green across both the P10 13/13 close gate and the PB3.4 rehearsal 9/9, which it could not be were the CREATEs originating at the script contract. Constructor `msg.sender` resolves to the broadcaster — `BroadcastNestingProbe.t.sol` L99, where `ProbeTarget.deployer` is asserted equal to the governor. The CREATE3 final salt hashes the broadcast sender — `DerBodenseeScriptWitness.t.sol`, PB3.5d. Recorded here so no future session re-probes settled ground.

**(iii) The literal reading of (iv)(2) is not executable — two independent mechanics.** (a) Simulation sends nothing, so the deployer nonce is identical across invocations: every step simulated before any broadcast places its CREATEs at the same live `n0`, never at the chained nonce it will occupy live, so a pre-broadcast simulation sequence cannot verify a chained projection even in principle. (b) Steps past the vault consume live predecessors — `DeployDerBodensee` reads `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` and calls `create()` on it (L67), so before the factory is broadcast that step reverts against an address carrying no code, for a reason unrelated to prediction; the hook, the 26 pool scripts, `DeployStageP.run()` and the Router inherit the same dependency. A single upfront full-sequence dry run is therefore impossible, not merely awkward.

**(iv) Resolution — two phases.** PHASE A, before any broadcast: obtain every per-step transaction count, compute all projections analytically, and write `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` and `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` into `.env` before step 2 consumes them. PHASE B, interleaved with the broadcast: immediately before each broadcast step, re-run that step in simulation against its now-live predecessors and confirm the address it reports equals the projection. PB-D27 (iv)(2)'s "run immediately before the live broadcast" therefore lands per step rather than once upfront — the only placement where a simulation has both live predecessors and a nonce matching what the next send consumes.

**(v) Count sourcing — chain-invariance is what makes phase A possible.** PB-D28 (ii) already establishes the split: a step's EOA transaction count is STRUCTURAL, following from its own CREATE and call sequence and invariant under the deployer's nonce, while a predicted address is nonce-dependent. Counts are equally invariant under the CHAIN. So steps that run standalone on Sepolia take their counts from a Sepolia simulation — `DeployTestnetStubs`; `DeployAureumVault`, which self-asserts only its own factory and therefore tolerates placeholder Bodensee and hook values; `DeployAuMM` — and steps needing live predecessors take theirs from a MAINNET simulation at the same `--sender`, where those predecessors exist. This does not reopen PB-D27 (iv)(2)'s "a mainnet-fork rehearsal cannot substitute, the nonce is wrong": that clause constrains ADDRESSES, which stay Sepolia-derived throughout. No count is read off the source, so PB-D27 (iv)(1) holds unamended.

**(vi) The projection arithmetic, recorded once.** `n0` is `vm.getNonce(deployer)` on Sepolia immediately before step 1. `nonce_at_step_k = n0 + the sum of the counts of steps 1 through k-1`. `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY = computeCreateAddress(deployer, nonce_at_step_3)`. `DER_BODENSEE_POOL = CREATE3.getDeployed(keccak256(abi.encode(deployer, 11155111, BODENSEE_SALT)), predictedFactory)` — this carries no nonce term of its own beyond the one fixing the factory, because CREATE3 depends only on creator and salt and the final salt hashes the broadcast sender. `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK = computeCreateAddress(deployer, nonce_at_step_6)`, the only projection requiring counts for steps 3 through 5. Any deviation from the recorded counts invalidates every projection downstream of it, which is why section 5 must be populated before a broadcast rather than alongside one.

**(vii) What the three self-asserts do and do not cover.** `FactoryAddressMismatch`, `BodenseeAddressMismatch` and `HookAddressMismatch` (PB-D27 (iv)(3), landed at PB3.5c1b and c2) fire identically under simulation and under broadcast, so phase B's pre-flight inherits them for free and a divergence aborts before the send rather than after it. They remain no substitute for phase A: the vault seals `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` and `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` as immutables at its own step, so by the time the Bodensee or hook assert can fire, a wrong projection is already sealed and recovery is a full sequence restart on fresh addresses — the accepted failure mode PB-D27 (iv) records, and the reason phase B is mandatory at every step rather than only the three asserting ones.

**(viii) Ladder.** h0a — this lock. h0b — the plan-side PB-D29 Decisions row. h1 — the runbook procedure section documenting phases A and B, the count-sourcing rule and the projection arithmetic. h2 — the phase-A count run, user-run under §8b, counts pasted back. h3 — `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_RUNBOOK.md` section 5 populated from h2, retiring every PENDING-h cell. Phase B executes inside rung i, per broadcast step, against the populated table.

## PB-D30 — PB-D29 (v) amendment: the base layer deploys the fee-routing hook BEFORE der Bodensee, reducing phase A to four Sepolia-standalone counts and retiring the mainnet-simulation clause (2026-07-26; PB3.5h1 pre-flight blocker)

**(i) The defect in PB-D29 (v).** That clause routes the counts of predecessor-dependent steps to "a MAINNET simulation at the same `--sender`, where those predecessors exist". This is false for the one step that motivated it: `DeployDerBodensee` calls `create()` on `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY`, an Aureum-bound weighted-pool factory that exists on no chain until this deployment creates it. Mainnet carries Balancer's infrastructure, not Aureum's, so a mainnet simulation of that step fails for exactly the same missing-code reason a Sepolia one does. The clause could not have been executed as written.

**(ii) The reorder that dissolves it.** Under the PB-D27 order the hook is step 6 and der Bodensee step 5, so projecting the hook's nonce required der Bodensee's transaction count — the one count no pre-broadcast simulation can produce. The two steps are independent in both directions, verified at source: `AureumFeeRoutingHook`'s constructor zero-checks `derBodensee_` and then only stores it (`DER_BODENSEE = derBodensee_`), its sole external call being `ZCHF = IERC20(IERC4626(address(svZchf_)).asset())`, so der Bodensee needs no code for the hook to deploy; and `DeployDerBodensee.run()` reads no `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` key at all, so the pool is created hookless and needs no hook code either. CHOSEN: swap them — the fee-routing hook becomes step 5, der Bodensee step 6. The vault still seals both addresses as immutables at step 2, so both remain projections regardless of which is built first.

**(iii) Phase A collapses to four counts, all Sepolia-standalone.** Der Bodensee carries no nonce term of its own — `CREATE3.getDeployed` depends only on the creating factory's address and the EOA-scoped salt per PB-D29 (vi) — and nothing after the hook requires a projection, so phase A needs counts for steps 1 through 4 only: `DeployTestnetStubs`, `DeployAureumVault`, `DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory`, `DeployAuMM`. Each makes zero external calls during construction, so each simulates standalone on Sepolia against projected addresses that carry no code yet: the weighted-factory chain is pure storage (`WeightedPoolFactory` to `BasePoolFactory` stores `_creationCode`, `SingletonAuthentication` stores `_vault`, no vault call anywhere in the chain), and the vault step self-asserts only its own factory and so tolerates placeholder Bodensee and hook values per PB-D29 (v).

**(iv) The mainnet-simulation clause is retired, not kept as a fallback.** No phase-A step needs it. PB-D29 (v)'s Sepolia-standalone half stands unchanged; its mainnet half never fires and its stated rationale was wrong, so it is withdrawn rather than left dormant for a future reader to act on. The chain-invariance of transaction counts established at PB-D29 (v) and PB-D28 (ii) is a separate claim, unaffected, and still holds.

**(v) Ladder.** h0c — this lock. h0d — the plan-side PB-D30 Decisions row. h1 onward is unchanged in shape, with two consequences carried into it: the runbook's section 6 swaps its step 5 and step 6, and section 5's count table is populated for steps 1 through 4 before any broadcast, the remaining rows filling from the broadcast itself at rung i since no projection reads them.

## PB-D31 — `DeployTestnetStubs` carries no broadcast wrapper: a silent production-path defect found by the rung h phase A dry run (2026-07-26; PB3.5h2 blocker)

**(i) The defect.** `test-stubs/DeployTestnetStubs.s.sol` contains zero broadcast cheatcodes. Its four CREATEs — `new StubERC20`, `new StubERC4626` and `new ERC4626RateProvider` at L130-L132 in `_ensureWithRate`, and `new StubERC20` at L144 in `_ensureStandard` — execute in the script's own simulation context, so `forge script ... --broadcast` against Sepolia sends nothing while still emitting the complete 67-pair `STUB_` map and seven named keys through `console2.log`. Every address in that map has no code on the target chain. Proven by contrast at PB3.5h2 rather than by reading: under identical invocation against Sepolia, `DeployAureumVault` wrote `broadcast/DeployAureumVault.s.sol/11155111/dry-run/run-latest.json` and `DeployTestnetStubs` wrote no artifact at all. Every other base-layer script carries between one and three `startBroadcast` calls; this one carries zero.

**(ii) Why it is silent, and where it would surface.** The section 6 step 1 capture instruction takes the emitted map into `.env`, and nothing in that instruction can distinguish a live address from a simulated one. Steps 2 through 4 never touch a stub, so the sequence proceeds normally and every self-assert passes. First contact is step 5, whose hook constructor calls `IERC4626(svZchf_).asset()` against a codeless address and reverts. By then step 2 has sealed `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` and `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` as immutables, so recovery is a full sequence restart on fresh addresses — the accepted-but-expensive PB-D27 (iv) failure mode, reached for a reason that has nothing to do with the prediction arithmetic that mode was written to guard.

**(iii) Why no prior gate caught it.** The same shape as PB-D26. `StagePRunRehearsal` never drives this script through a broadcast: it replays the roster in-process through the additive `envPairCount` / `envPairAt` getters at L230 and L237, added at PB-D25 (ii) for exactly that purpose, so the stubs are constructed by the test contract and the missing wrapper is invisible. The P10 close gate never touches the script at all, binding real mainnet literals instead. And the committed `test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` is a fork sample by construction — its own header line says so — so its existence was never evidence the script could deploy anywhere. PB-D21 (v)'s "live Sepolia addresses regenerated at PB3.5" assumed a capability the file never had.

**(iv) Fix.** Wrap the deployment work inside `run()` in `vm.startBroadcast()` / `vm.stopBroadcast()`, matching the bare-broadcast convention `DeployAuMM` (L35) and `DeployRouter` already use, with the sender supplied by `--sender` at invocation. The `console2.log` map emission, the `_assertCoverage` gate and the `_standardDecimals` env reads stay OUTSIDE the broadcast: they are cheatcode and view work, not transactions, and sweeping them inside would put non-transaction calls into the broadcast sequence.

**(v) Blast radius.** `StagePRunRehearsal` alone. Under `vm.startBroadcast` the CREATEs are attributed to the broadcaster rather than to the script contract, so every stub address changes; the rehearsal reads addresses through the getters rather than asserting literals, so it should absorb that, but the 9/9 fork suite is re-run as the gate rather than assumed. The P10 close gate is untouched by construction. `test-stubs/sepolia-stubs.env` is regenerated with live addresses at rung i per PB-D21 (v) regardless, so its fork-sample contents are not a migration concern.

**(vi) Counts already measured, unaffected by this defect.** Phase A at PB3.5h2 measured `c2 = 4` for `DeployAureumVault` (three CREATEs plus the `factory.create()` call), `c3 = 1` for `DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory` and `c4 = 1` for `DeployAuMM`, each read from its dry-run artifact's `transactions` array. `c1` is undefined until (iv) lands, because the broadcast wrapper is precisely what makes those CREATEs countable. Live Sepolia gas price at measurement was 2.214 gwei, putting the PB3.5f 367,079,280-gas spine at roughly 0.81 SepETH and confirming the PB-D19 one-to-two SepETH target against live data; the vault step alone estimates 0.0578 ETH against a deployer holding 0.05, so funding is a hard blocker on any broadcast, not a headroom margin.

**(vii) Ladder.** h2a — this lock. h2b — the `run()` broadcast wrapper. h2c — the `StagePRunRehearsal` re-run as the no-regression gate. h2d — the phase-A `c1` count, obtainable only once h2b has landed. h3 — section 5 populated from c1 through c4.

## PB-D32 — PB3.5i blocker: the compiled Vault overruns EIP-170 because the toolchain never matched Balancer's — the per-contract optimizer runs and the OpenZeppelin pin both diverge, and the artifact is reproduced against the deployed mainnet runtime as oracle (user-adjudicated 2026-07-27; PB3.5i blocker)

**(i) The defect.** `AureumVaultFactory.create()` reverts `INITIALIZATION_FAILED` on live Sepolia at deployer nonce 90. The cause is EIP-170: our compiled `Vault` runtime is 28,304 bytes against the 24,576 limit, so CREATE3's inner CREATE returns empty code and CREATE3 reports that as an initialisation failure. Confirmed by `eth_call` and `eth_estimateGas` against two independent RPCs at a 50M gas ceiling — neither a gas nor a provider condition. `VaultExtension` at 23,247 and `VaultAdmin` at 15,304 both fit. Balancer's live mainnet Vault at `0xbA1333333333a1BA1108E8412f11850A5C319bA9` is 24,538 bytes, inside the limit by 38 — essentially the same source, 15 percent smaller.

**(ii) Why no gate caught it.** `forge config` reports no `code_size_limit` and forge does not enforce EIP-170 in simulation. Every fork gate that stood up a Vault — the P10 close gate 13/13, the PB3.4 rehearsal 9/9, and the whole Stage B through P vault surface — was validating an artifact no chain will accept. The failure was reachable only by a live broadcast, and it surfaced at the first one.

**(iii) The two divergences, measured.** Balancer's deployed artifacts come from hardhat via `pvt/common/hardhat-base-config.ts`, not from their foundry profile, and that config carries per-contract overrides: `Vault.sol` and `VaultExtension.sol` compile at `runs: 500` while the package base is 9999, which is why the non-overridden `VaultAdmin` fits for us and the two overridden contracts are the two that diverge. `VaultFactory.sol` is not overridden, so the 9999 reading taken from its verified Etherscan source at `0xAc27df81663d139072E615855eF9aB0Af3FBD281` and recorded at `foundry.toml` L6 was correct for that contract and wrong only in being generalised to the package. Measured on our toolchain: Vault 28,304 at 9999, 25,161 at 500, 24,918 at 200 — so matching their runs is necessary and not sufficient, and no runs value tested clears the limit. The second divergence is `yarn.lock` L1317, which resolves `@openzeppelin/contracts` to exactly 5.0.0 against our 5.6.1; `Vault.sol` L5—L10 imports six symbols from it and the `solidity-utils` tree beneath imports more. Netting our metadata-free output against mainnet's CBOR tail leaves roughly 676 bytes unexplained by runs alone, and the OpenZeppelin pin is the only remaining identified source-level difference. It is NOT quantified — (xi) rung i1 exists to settle that before any mechanism lands.

**(iv) The finding beneath the finding — artifact inheritance was never true.** CLAUDE.md §2 states the Vault contracts are byte-identical to audited Balancer V3 code, and `foundry.toml` L11 escalates that to a dependency on byte-level toolchain parity. The claim holds for the `.sol` files and fails for the artifact: the audited and deployed Vault contains OpenZeppelin 5.0.0 library code, ours contains 5.6.1, and the two were compiled at different optimizer settings. Our Vault has never been the audited Vault at the bytecode level, on any branch, at any stage.
