### PB17 — a tool that reads `.env` for its own configuration does not make a shell variable work on the command line invoking it: the shell expands first, and the shell has never read that file

Caught at the PB3.8h9g pre-flight, on a command drafted straight from the runbook's own documented invocation form. `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_RUNBOOK.md` wrote the RPC URL as a shell variable reference in nine places and had done since PB3.5, and every one of them fails in a plain shell with `error: a value is required for '--rpc-url <RPC_URL>' but none was supplied`. The mechanism is ordering rather than configuration: zsh expands the variable against its own environment at the moment the line is submitted, before `forge` or `cast` is launched at all, and nothing in the shell's environment holds that key, because `.env` is read by foundry and by nobody else. The working form is the `foundry.toml` `[rpc_endpoints]` alias — `--rpc-url sepolia` — where foundry performs the substitution itself, from the file it actually reads. Section 1 of the runbook stated the mechanism correctly and drew the wrong conclusion from it: "foundry auto-loads `.env`, and that is the whole mechanism" is true of foundry's own env reads and silently false of shell-side expansion on the very commands that sentence governs. What makes this a lesson rather than a typo is that the defect survived a live deployment. PB3.5 broadcast the base layer, 87 stubs and twenty-five pools using these commands, because the operator hit the error, substituted the alias by hand, and moved on without the document ever being corrected — PB-D48 (i) records the rung-h9 dry run in the working alias form against a runbook that says otherwise, and the divergence sat unremarked in both. A loud failure that a human silently routes around leaves the artifact wrong for whoever reads it next, who may be a cold session or the same operator mid-stall: section 9's R2, R3 and R6 had carried the broken form into the recovery procedure for a stalled 114-transaction broadcast, which is the worst place in the repository for a command that cannot run. The rule is that a command form in an operator document is unverified until it has been executed verbatim from a clean shell, and that a documented form diverging from what the operator actually types is a defect in the document rather than a habit to keep in the head. Fixed at h9f4, which also had to leave one command deliberately unlike the other eight, because foundry issue 4346 reports `--resume` not resolving aliases at all. This is the same shape as PB15 and PB16: in all three a passing outcome was mistaken for a passing check, because nobody asked what the command had actually examined.

## PB-D50 — PB3.8i2: the live constellation disproves H-D9's exactness lemma, because a bare balance ratio is parity-exact only at equal weights; `_venueRatio` gains the weight term and H-D9 is amended rather than silently patched (user-adjudicated 2026-08-02; PB3.8i2)

**(i) What the seeded roster showed, and the closed form that explains it without remainder.** With all 26 Miliarium pools capitalised and no swap yet executed, `tvl()` should equal the seeded accounting value under PB-D46's flat one-dollar-per-token convention, since at t=0 there is no market for a live price to diverge from. It does not: the roster sums to 1,271,467.991493383742761200 svZCHF against roughly 987,000 expected once slot 02's deliberately unpriced allocation is removed, and the per-pool ratios cluster on two discrete values, 1.287 and 1.374, rather than scattering. ixHelvetia closes in one line. It holds svZCHF at 80 percent and sUSDS at 20, seeded 11,200 and 2,800; exactly nine constellation venues hold both sUSDS and svZCHF, namely slot 01 plus the eight Standard-template pools 15, 16, 17, 21, 23, 24, 25 and 26; `_venueRatio` returns 80/20 equals 4 at Helvetia and 26/26 equals 1 at each Standard venue; `_directRatio` takes the unweighted mean, giving (4 + 8)/9 = 4/3; and 2,800 times 4/3 plus 11,200 is 14,933.33 recurring, which is the on-chain reading 14933333333333333332400 to the repeating digit. The two clusters follow from the same graph: those eight sUSDS venues are precisely the 1.374 cohort, and the 1.287 cohort is the pools holding no sUSDS.

**(ii) The code is faithful to its lock; there is no drift to report.** `_venueRatio` at `src/emission/TVLOracle.sol` L307-L326 returns `(balQuote * 1e18) / balBase` and H-D9 at `docs/STAGE_H_NOTES.md` L57-L59 specifies exactly that, stating the implied rate "equals `balanceSvZCHFLeg18 / balanceULeg18`" and adding, in the same clause, "with no pool-type-specific curve math (StableMath Jacobian, weighted ratios) consumed". The weights were excluded deliberately and in writing. So this lock does not allege that the implementation departed from its specification — it alleges that the specification's justifying property is false on the pool shapes the constellation actually contains.

**(iii) The defect is H-D9's accuracy lemma.** H-D9 rests its posture on one claim: the balance-ratio approximation is "exact at AMM parity and degrades smoothly off-parity bounded by arbitrage equilibrium". For a Balancer weighted pool at parity, arbitrage drives the spot price to market, and the balance ratio therefore satisfies `bal_quote / bal_base = P* * (w_quote / w_base)`. The balance ratio equals the true price at parity if and only if `w_quote == w_base`. That condition holds for a StableSwap venue and for a 50/50 weighted pool, and for nothing else. The Miliarium roster is 80/20, 26/16, 46/18 and 32/26 — no venue in the constellation satisfies it, and der Bodensee does not either. The lemma is not approximately true here; it is false at the exact state it names as its exactness case.

**(iv) The error is a fixed multiplicative bias, not off-parity degradation, and trading does not wash it out.** Because the relation in (iii) holds at any market state rather than only at t=0, `_venueRatio` reports the true price multiplied by `w_quote / w_base` permanently. At t=0 the bias is visible only because the seeded truth is independently known. Under live trading it becomes invisible while remaining exactly the same size, and `_directRatio`'s roster averaging then averages a set of biased estimators rather than converging on anything — the averaging dilutes manipulation, as OQ-8 intends, but it does not dilute a systematic bias shared by every venue of the same shape. The daily EMA per OQ-5a-bis absorbs sampling noise and is likewise powerless against a constant factor.

**(v) Why no gate caught it, and why the shape of the gap is the reason.** 928 unit tests, 55 hevm proofs, the P10 mainnet-fork close gate and the full white-hat sweep all pass over this path. A mock venue built at equal weights makes `w_quote / w_base` exactly 1.0, so the bias evaluates away and the lemma reads as true; a test asserting internal consistency between two oracle reads never compares either against an independent truth. It took a live heterogeneous roster with an externally known seeded value to expose it, which is precisely the artefact rung i2 created and precisely what no fixture had previously been.

**(vi) H-D9's own escape clause has fired, earlier and more cleanly than it anticipated.** The lock reserves curve-derived spot pricing "for the case where mainnet telemetry shows materially wrong F-7 rankings". Slots 11 and 17 both received 70,000 dollars of seed and read 71,894.896030245746684800 and 96,161.562696912413345400 svZCHF, a 33.75 percent spread between identically capitalised pools at t=0 with no trading. Slots 08 and 23 carry identical weight vectors of 0.16, 0.16, 0.26, 0.16, 0.26 and identical 47,000-dollar allocations and read 6.73 percent apart, differing only in constituents and therefore in which venue subset prices them. F-7 rankings drive gauge outcomes and emission shares, so this is the named failure mode, observed pre-mainnet rather than after it.

**(vii) Disposition: FIX, with H-D9 amended rather than patched around.** Accept was available and is rejected. It would ship a LOCKED decision whose central exactness claim is disproved on the live constellation, with Sepolia evidence any auditor reproduces from the deployment record in an afternoon, and it would carry a known F-7 ranking distortion between identically capitalised pools into Stage Q as accepted risk. Amending when the escape clause fires is the posture the lock itself provisioned. The amendment is narrow and must be stated as such: H-D9 conflated "no Jacobian" with "no weights", and only the second half is withdrawn.

**(viii) Scope of the fix, and what is deliberately not touched.** `_venueRatio` returns `(balQuote * 1e18 * wBase) / (balBase * wQuote)`, or an equivalent ordering chosen for precision, which is the minimal term making "exact at parity" true for unequal-weight weighted pools. This is not StableMath and introduces no Jacobian, no invariant evaluation and no swap-fee term. Unchanged: the `tokenToUnderlying` governance map and its PB-D42 self-map convention, `_directRatio`'s unweighted cross-venue mean and its two-leg roster enumeration, `_twoHopRatio` and the PB-D43 hop roster, `_constellationRatio`'s svZCHF identity, `tvl()`'s summation shape, and the daily EMA cadence. The estimator's structure is retained; only its per-venue term is corrected.

**(ix) The fix introduces a trust surface the current arithmetic does not have, and that must be gated rather than assumed.** Balances reach the oracle from `IVaultExplorer.getPoolData`, which is Vault-authoritative. Normalized weights do not — they are read from the pool itself via `IWeightedPool.getNormalizedWeights()`, and `src/gauge/GaugeEligibility.sol` L381 already records the consequence as F-12: provenance is what makes a pool's self-reported weights trustworthy, because the canonical hook's `onRegister` does not gate by factory. A governance-added constellation venue that is not from the approved Aureum weighted-pool factory could therefore report arbitrary weights and steer every price that venue contributes to. Every venue in the roster today has that provenance, the Miliarium set by construction and der Bodensee likewise, so the exposure is latent rather than live — but the build rung must settle it explicitly, and the two candidate shapes are a factory-provenance assertion mirroring GaugeEligibility or an equal-weight fallback for venues that cannot answer. It is a typed-domain question under section 12 and is resolved in notes before code.

**(x) Cost, named rather than discovered.** This reopens `src/emission/TVLOracle.sol`, audited-candidate emission-path source, before Stage Q — the PB-D13 shape, and the ground on which the phase-4 idempotency rebuild was rejected. It amends a Stage-H LOCKED decision rather than a P-bis one, so `docs/STAGE_H_NOTES.md` carries an amendment block pointing here. Re-gating covers the Part-A unit suite, the fork suites touching TVLOracle and EfficiencyOracle, and any hevm or Act surface asserting the old ratio, since `formal/` carries a CCB and oracle leg. The estimate is that this is cheaper than an audit finding that the emission oracle's stated accuracy property is false on the production pool set, which is the alternative rather than doing nothing.

**(xi) Relationship to PB-D51, and the ladder.** PB-D51 records a second and independent defect surfaced in the same investigation: PB-D46 funds pools at `allocation * weight` on a flat one-dollar-per-token convention, but Balancer normalized weights are value weights, so correct funding is `weight_i * V / P_i` and Stage R must supply a real price source. Neither defect explains the other's Sepolia face — the seeding convention is self-consistent under stub fiat and produced none of the numbers in (i), while the oracle bias produced all of them and would persist under perfectly funded pools. They share only the underlying Balancer fact that weights are value weights, and they close independently. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row, then the `docs/STAGE_H_NOTES.md` H-D9 amendment block, then the `_venueRatio` change with the (ix) provenance question settled first, then the re-gate, then a re-read of the seeded roster expecting ixHelvetia at exactly 14,000 and the roster near 987,000. Section 13 of the deployment record proceeds independently and records the seeding as executed under PB-D46, attributing the t=0 spread to this lock rather than to a failed capitalisation.

## PB-D51 — PB3.8i2: Balancer normalized weights are value weights, so PB-D46 (iv)'s one-dollar-per-token funding is a testnet fiat rather than a general derivation, and Stage R must fund by value against a named price source (user-adjudicated 2026-08-02; PB3.8i2)

**(i) The Balancer fact, and what it makes of a funding rule expressed in token counts.** A weighted pool's `normalizedWeights` are shares of pool VALUE, not shares of token count. Funding a pool to its declared weights therefore requires `balance_i = weight_i * V / P_i`, where `V` is the pool's total value budget and `P_i` is the price of token i in the same unit as `V`. Funding instead by `balance_i = weight_i * V` — allocation times weight, with no division — is that formula with every `P_i` silently set to one. The two coincide exactly when every token in the pool genuinely costs the same, and diverge without limit when they do not.

**(ii) PB-D46 (iv) is correct, and is correctly scoped; this lock does not overturn it.** That clause states that PB-D41 (vii) "converts a pool's dollar allocation to per-token amounts strictly by `normalizedWeights` and then to raw units by each stub's decimals, and it names no price step because there is none: every token is notionally one dollar", derives the internal consistency itself — with `balance_i = allocation * weight_i` every leg's `balance_i / weight_i` equals the same allocation, so all legs price at parity — and closes by calling that "the only coherent choice for stubs carrying no feed". Every one of those statements is true on Sepolia. The stubs are fabricated 1:1 placeholders with no price feed of any kind, so a flat one-dollar fiat is not an approximation of some real price the script failed to look up; it is the only price that exists, and under it quantity-proportional funding IS value-proportional funding.

**(iii) The gap is a missing boundary, not a mistaken derivation.** Nothing in PB-D41, PB-D46 or the surrounding record states that the one-dollar convention is testnet-only, or that a mainnet deployment must fund by value against real prices. The load-bearing sentence reads "it names no price step because there is none" — a statement true of a stub chain and false in general — and it sits in a lock a Stage-R author would reasonably consult as the authority on how Miliarium pools are capitalised. The clause reasons its way to the right answer for the wrong-in-general reason and then stops, which is exactly the shape that carries a testnet accommodation across a chain boundary without anyone deciding to.

**(iv) What Stage R must actually do.** Fund each leg at `balance_i = weight_i * V / P_i` in raw units after decimals, with `P_i` drawn from a source named in advance and recorded, and with the resulting per-leg values asserted to reproduce the declared weights before any transaction is broadcast. The assertion matters as much as the arithmetic: an authored allocation table gates itself only against its own sum, as PB-D46 (v) already notes, so the check that a funded pool matches its declared weights has to be a separate post-condition rather than a property inherited from the table.

**(v) The failure mode if the convention crosses unremarked, and the irony in where it sits.** On mainnet the Miliarium roster holds assets whose prices differ by three orders of magnitude — WETH against USDC, wstETH against a stablecoin leg. Funding an 80/20 WETH-and-stable pool by token count puts 80 percent of the COUNT in WETH and therefore very nearly all of the VALUE there; the pool registers cleanly, passes every composition gate, reports its declared weights, and is economically a different pool from the one specified. That is precisely the class PB-D26 records — tokens sorted without their weights, "a mispriced pool that registers cleanly" — and PB-D46 (iv) invokes PB-D26 by name as its reason for reading token order and weights from the Vault rather than the config library. The clause guards against that failure through one door while holding the other open.

**(vi) Nothing on Sepolia requires remediation, and re-seeding would be wrong.** The 26 pools are correctly funded under the only price convention available to them, the seeded state is internally consistent, and the 389-transaction broadcast recorded in `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_POOL_SEEDING_LEDGER.md` stands as executed. Re-seeding to some other convention would spend gas to move from one arbitrary fiat to another, since the stubs would still carry no feed. This lock creates a Stage-R obligation and no P-bis work item.

**(vii) The price source is its own typed-domain question, and one candidate is disqualified until PB-D50 lands.** Naming `P_i` is not a detail to settle at broadcast time: the candidates differ in trust model, and a chain-read source, an off-chain snapshot committed as an artifact under the PB-D44 derive-commit-then-assert pattern, and a governance-authored table are three genuinely different postures. One is excluded outright — `TVLOracle` must not supply `P_i` while PB-D50 is open, because a funding routine that priced its legs through the same biased estimator would produce pools that look correct to the oracle and are wrong in fact, which is the worst available outcome and is self-concealing. Even after PB-D50 lands, using the oracle to fund the venues the oracle prices is circular and wants its own adjudication.

**(viii) Independence from PB-D50, stated so neither can be closed by the other.** The two findings share only the underlying Balancer fact that weights are value weights. Neither explains the other's Sepolia face: this convention is self-consistent under stub fiat and produced none of the t=0 TVL spread, while PB-D50's missing weight term produced all of it and would persist unchanged against perfectly value-funded pools. They sit on different surfaces — a deploy script and Stage-R runbook here, audited emission source there — carry different blast radii, and close independently. A single disposition must never be allowed to read as closing both.

**(ix) Disposition and ladder.** Recorded as a Stage-R gate rather than a fix, since there is no Sepolia defect to repair. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row alongside PB-D50's, then the RB register row as RB-015 so the obligation is findable from the register rather than only from a P-bis lock, then a CLAUDE.md section 11 carry-forward entry naming it a pre-mainnet gate under the section 12 blocker classification, then the Stage-R price-source adjudication per (vii) when Stage R opens. Section 13 of the deployment record proceeds independently and records the seeding as executed under PB-D46, citing this lock for the convention's boundary and PB-D50 for the t=0 spread.

## PB-D52 — PB3.8i4: the PB-D50 (ix) provenance question resolves as a factory-provenance gate ordered BEFORE the weight read and skipping rather than reverting, and the fix's true cost is a ten-contract Sepolia cascade deliberately not paid — the corrected oracle is proven on a Sepolia fork and live Sepolia stays pre-fix until Stage R (user-adjudicated 2026-08-02; PB3.8i4)

**(i) The two candidates PB-D50 (ix) named are not alternatives, and pairing them concealed that they cover different halves.** That clause offered a factory-provenance assertion mirroring GaugeEligibility or an equal-weight fallback for venues that cannot answer, as though either would serve. They do not address the same hazard. Provenance addresses trust: any contract can implement `getNormalizedWeights()` and return whatever it likes, which is exactly the F-12 proof-of-concept standing at `test/whitehat/F12_compositionGateProvenance.t.sol` L11-L16, where a hand-rolled pool returns a fabricated single-entry weight vector of `1e18`. The fallback addresses type safety: a StableSwap or bespoke venue does not implement the function at all, so the call reverts rather than lying. Choosing either alone leaves the other hole open, and the asymmetry between them is decisive — a fallback cannot substitute for a gate, because the hostile pool answers successfully and the fallback therefore never fires, while a gate can substitute for a fallback, which is what (ii) exploits.

**(ii) One gate closes both halves, provided it is ordered before the read.** `_venueRatio` calls `IBasePoolFactory(approvedFactory).isPoolFromFactory(v)` first and reads `getNormalizedWeights()` only on a venue that passed. A venue that is not a weighted pool is not from the weighted-pool factory, so the gate rejects it and the unsupported call is never made: no `try` / `catch`, no equal-weight default, and no second code path to audit. The equal-weight fallback is therefore not adopted — not because it is wrong in itself, but because a correctly ordered gate makes it unreachable.

**(iii) The failure mode is skip, not revert, because this is a pricing mean and not an admission check.** A rejected venue returns `(0, false)` and drops out of `_directRatio`'s cross-venue average, exactly as the F-19 / P-D37 uninitialized-venue guard at `src/emission/TVLOracle.sol` L309 already does. Reverting would be the wrong shape here: `tvl()` is read by `EMASampler`, `GaugeEligibility` and `EmissionDistributor`, so one mis-curated governance roster entry would brick valuation protocol-wide instead of degrading a single venue's contribution. The contrast with the precedent is the point rather than an inconsistency — `GaugeEligibility.meetsCompositionQualityGate` reverts `PoolTypeNotWhitelisted` because it is an admission decision on one candidate that must fail loud, while a mean taken over a roster must fail local. Same check, opposite disposition, and the reason is legible from what each function returns.

**(iv) The ordering is deliberately inverted relative to the precedent this mirrors.** `GaugeEligibility` reads weights at L371 and L389 and checks provenance only afterwards at L373 and L391, so a non-weighted candidate there dies on a raw call failure instead of the clean `PoolTypeNotWhitelisted` the function was written to give. That is a diagnostics wart rather than a security defect — both paths revert and nothing unsafe is admitted — but it must not be copied into the oracle, where the same ordering would additionally defeat (ii) by making the unsupported call before the gate could prevent it. Recorded here as a candidate register row against `GaugeEligibility`; no change to that contract is in scope for this lock.

**(v) `approvedFactory` is a constructor immutable, and the cascade in (vii) makes that free.** It is seeded from the same `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` environment value that `script/DeployStageG.s.sol` L127 already passes to `GaugeEligibility`, so the two consumers share one source of truth and no new governance surface, no new setter and no new one-shot seal is introduced. A governance-settable slot was available and is rejected: its only argument is avoiding a constructor change, and (vii) establishes that the constructor is re-run regardless. Satisfiability is verified rather than assumed — `script/DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` L124 creates der Bodensee through `WeightedPoolFactory.create` and the Miliarium pools are created through that same factory, so every venue in the live roster passes the gate today, which is the concrete form of PB-D50 (ix)'s claim that the exposure is latent rather than live.

**(vi) `IncendiaryRegistry` L360 is not a counter-precedent for reading the weights unguarded.** That call reads `BODENSEE_POOL`, an immutable sealed at construction to a known factory-created pool — its provenance is structural, and a gate there would assert what the deployment already guarantees. `_venueRatio` instead walks a governance-extensible roster of arbitrary addresses. The two surfaces share a function name and nothing else, their threat models are disjoint, and an unguarded read against an immutable target says nothing about a guarded read against a mutable roster.

**(vii) The cascade, named as the fix's true cost rather than left inside PB-D50 (x)'s reopens-TVLOracle phrasing.** There is no proxy and no in-place patch: changing `_venueRatio` changes the deployed bytecode, and the oracle address is held immutable by three direct consumers — `EfficiencyOracle` L24, `EMASampler` L57 and `GaugeEligibility` L32. Those three are held immutable in turn by `EmissionDistributor` L36 and L42, `CCBMultiplier` L72, `VotingWeight` L47, L53 and L59, and `GaugeRegistry` L41; and that ring is held immutable by `AureumGovernance` L42 and L48 and by `IncendiaryRegistry` L84. The transitive closure is the oracle plus nine contracts, drawn from the sixteen deployed in phase 4 at nonces 125 to 238 for 27,666,605 gas, and rung f's 62 wiring transactions at nonces 239 to 300 would have to re-run against the new address. Rewiring is not an escape even where a binding is a setter rather than an immutable: `CCBMultiplier.setGaugeRegistry` at L176 is the one-shot seal locked at PB-D18 (v) and it has already fired on Sepolia, so that binding is effectively immutable now. Redeploy is the mechanism, and supersession is recorded per section 8f rather than by deleting rows.

**(viii) Disposition on the cascade: build the fix and prove it on a Sepolia fork; do not pay the re-broadcast.** PB-D50 (xi) already specifies the evidence as a roster re-read expecting ixHelvetia at exactly 14,000 and the constellation near 987,000, and that is a fork assertion rather than a broadcast obligation. The test forks Sepolia at a block after the rung-i2 seeding, deploys the corrected oracle in-process, re-binds `tokenToUnderlying`, the constellation roster and the hop underlying onto that new in-process instance, and reads `tvl()` across all 26 pools against the real seeded balances the fork already carries. Re-bind means the oracle's own governance state and nothing else — the 26 pools are not re-capitalised, no seeding transaction is replayed, and rung i2 stands exactly as broadcast. The evidence this produces is stronger than a live re-read would be, because it holds pool state fixed and varies only the estimator.

**(ix) Live Sepolia keeps the biased oracle until Stage R, and that is recorded rather than left implicit.** The deployment record and CLAUDE.md section 11 state that the live `TVLOracle` at `0xBf78A49b1AB86247198f6660Bf5a0491007E6A64`, deployed at phase-4 nonce 126, remains pre-fix and that its `tvl()` readings carry the PB-D50 bias. Saying so is honest rather than concealing — deployment-record section 13 already attributes the t=0 spread to PB-D50 and already declines to claim the oracle matches accounting allocation, so this extends an existing disclosure rather than opening a new one. Mainnet inherits no Sepolia address, so Stage R deploys the corrected oracle fresh and the testnet's staleness costs the mainnet path nothing.

**(x) The optional redeploy rung, named but not scheduled.** If workstream C binds on truthful end-to-end numbers, the redeploy becomes a named rung sequenced after the fork proof rather than a precondition for closing this fix. PB-D41's frontend trigger was empty pools, which the seeding closed — a TVL wrong by a fixed multiplicative factor is a weaker defect than an empty pool but a real one on a public-facing surface, and it is the frontend's requirement rather than the oracle's correctness that would justify spending the cascade. Nothing in this lock schedules that rung and nothing in it forbids one.

**(xi) What this lock does not do, and the ladder.** PB-D50's disposition is unchanged: the finding is still FIX, the weight term is still the minimal correction, StableMath is still excluded, and this lock resolves the clause (ix) blocker and prices the clause (x) cost rather than revisiting either. PB-D51 is untouched and still closes independently, and its disqualification of `TVLOracle` as the Stage-R price source stands while PB-D50 is open. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row, then the RB register row for PB-D51 as RB-015 with the (iv) `GaugeEligibility` ordering wart as its successor row, then the `_venueRatio` build carrying the weight term, the provenance gate and the `approvedFactory` immutable together in one constructor change, then the unit, fork and formal re-gate, then the Sepolia-fork evidence test per (viii), then the deployment-record and section 11 statements per (ix).

## PB-D53 — PB3.8i7: the `_venueRatio` build shape — the weight term aggregates in lockstep with the balance sums over a branch the genesis roster cannot exercise, both new guards skip rather than revert, and the `approvedFactory` immutable ripples to eleven construction sites (2026-08-03; PB3.8i7)

**(i) The aggregation branch is structurally dead on the live constellation, and recording that is why this lock exists.** `_venueRatio` at `src/emission/TVLOracle.sol` L314—L321 accumulates with `+=` into `balBase` and `balQuote`, and its own NatSpec at L300 names the intent, aggregating wrappers and bases that map to the same underlying. On the seeded roster that branch never runs. `script/config/SepoliaTokenUnderlyings.sol` carries 60 pairs whose 60 underlyings are pairwise distinct, 47 self-mapping and 13 resolving away, and none of those 13 underlyings is itself a mapped pool token — so no venue in the constellation holds two tokens resolving to one underlying, and each accumulator is written at most once per venue. The map's header states the 47-and-13 split and the pairwise distinctness; the corollary it does not state, and the one that governs this build, is that the accumulation is unreachable.

**(ii) The weight term must nevertheless aggregate over exactly the partition the balances use, and the reason is dimensional rather than defensive.** A Balancer weighted pool's parity relation is per-value-share: `bal_quote / bal_base` equals `P*` times `w_quote / w_base`, where each `w` is the share of pool value carried by that leg. When several legs are summed into one balance, the share carried by the sum is the sum of their weights, so `wBase` is the sum of normalized weights over the tokens whose resolved underlying is `base` and `wQuote` the sum over those resolving to `quote`. Pairing a summed balance with a single leg's weight is not an approximation with a bounded error but a type error — a ratio whose numerator and denominator are quantified over different sets — and it is PB-D50's own defect recurring one level down.

**(iii) A single-leg implementation is observationally identical to the correct one on every artefact this stage can produce, so the discriminating test is authored rather than inherited.** Because (i) holds, the two implementations agree on all 26 venues, agree under the PB-D52 (viii) Sepolia-fork evidence test, and agree on the ixHelvetia-at-exactly-14,000 and constellation-near-987,000 readings PB-D50 (xi) specifies as the fix's evidence — a wrong build passes the entire re-gate and is confirmed by it. This is PB-D50 (v) recurring: there an equal-weight mock made `w_quote / w_base` exactly one so the bias evaluated away and the lemma read as true, here a roster carrying one token per underlying makes the sum and the single leg coincide, and in both cases the fixture's homogeneity in the one dimension under test is what hides the defect. The re-gate therefore carries a purpose-built unit venue holding two tokens that resolve to a single underlying at deliberately unequal weights, asserting a value the single-leg reading cannot produce. The branch is latent rather than dead in the protocol's own terms: `setTokenUnderlying` is a plain overwriting setter per PB-D45 (iv), so governance maps a second token onto an existing underlying whenever it likes, with no redeploy, no code change, and no event marking the moment the branch goes live.

**(iv) A weight array whose length disagrees with the token array skips the venue rather than reverting.** The disposition is PB-D52 (iii)'s and the reasoning transfers unchanged — a pricing mean must fail local where an admission check fails loud, because `tvl()` is read by `EMASampler`, `GaugeEligibility` and `EmissionDistributor` and one bad venue must not brick valuation protocol-wide. After the provenance gate this condition is close to unreachable, since a pool from the approved weighted-pool factory reports weights matching its registered tokens; close to unreachable is exactly the standard PB-D50 (v) warns against trusting, the guard costs one comparison, and without it a mismatch is an out-of-bounds index panic rather than a skip.

**(v) A zero `wBase` or `wQuote` skips for the same reason, and this guard is load-bearing rather than ornamental.** Division by zero is a `Panic(0x12)` in Solidity 0.8, which propagates out of `_venueRatio` through `_directRatio` and `_constellationRatio` into `tvl()` — precisely the protocol-wide bricking PB-D52 (iii) rejects, arriving through arithmetic rather than through a `revert` statement and therefore not caught by reading the revert surface. Both new conditions fold into the existing eligibility test at L322, which already gates on both balances being positive, so the shape of the function's single exit is unchanged.

**(vi) The arithmetic and the ordering, with headroom checked rather than assumed.** The return becomes `(balQuote * wBase * 1e18) / (balBase * wQuote)`, PB-D50 (viii)'s form with the multiplications ordered ahead of the division, which is the convention `script/SeedMiliariumPoolsSepolia.s.sol` L58 already states for the seeding arithmetic. Balances arrive 18-decimal scaled, so a venue holding a trillion whole tokens reads 1e30 and weights are bounded by 1e18: the numerator is bounded by 1e66 against a `uint256` ceiling near 1.16e77 and the denominator by 1e48, leaving eleven orders of headroom at the tightest point. The alternative ordering that computes the bare ratio first and applies the weight correction afterwards is rejected on precision, since the intermediate division truncates before the correction reaches it.

**(vii) `approvedFactory` enters as a constructor immutable, positioned with the other singular addresses, and the blast radius is eleven construction sites.** It is seeded from the same `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` value `script/DeployStageG.s.sol` L127 passes to `GaugeEligibility` per PB-D52 (v), sits after `_svzchf` and before `_initialGovernance` so the singular addresses stay grouped and the two seed arrays stay last, and carries a `ZeroAddress` check matching the four already at L127—L130. Solidity constructor arguments are positional, so every caller edits regardless of where the parameter lands: `script/DeployStageF.s.sol` L121 plus ten test sites across `test/unit/TVLOracle.t.sol` at L45, L61, L66, L71, L76, L83 and L100, `test/fork/StageHIntegration.t.sol` L72, `test/fork/StageIIntegration.t.sol` L50 and `test/whitehat/F19_uninitializedVenueLiveness.t.sol` L63. That is the I13 shape — the blast radius is sized at pre-flight rather than discovered at the first failing compile — and the five negative-construction cases at L61 through L76 each need the new argument threaded without disturbing the zero-address case they assert.

**(viii) Gate ordering, what is deliberately untouched, and the ladder.** Order inside the function is the existing `isPoolInitialized` skip at L309, then the new `isPoolFromFactory` skip, then `getPoolTokens` and `getPoolData`, then `getNormalizedWeights` — the provenance gate precedes every read it protects per PB-D52 (ii), and the F-19 / P-D37 initialization skip keeps its first position and its comment. Unchanged: the `tokenToUnderlying` resolution and its PB-D42 self-map convention, `_directRatio`'s unweighted cross-venue mean, `_twoHopRatio` and the PB-D43 hop roster, `_constellationRatio`'s svZCHF identity, `tvl()`'s summation, the EMA cadence, and every governance setter. The two NatSpec blocks that state the old return form, at L300 and L304, are corrected in the same save as the code they describe. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row, then the single build carrying the weight term, both guards, the provenance gate and the immutable together per PB-D52 (xi), then the eleven-site constructor ripple, then the unit re-gate including the (iii) discriminator, then the fork and formal re-gate, then the PB-D52 (viii) Sepolia-fork evidence test, then the deployment-record and section 11 statements per PB-D52 (ix).

## PB-D54 — PB3.8i9 pre-flight: the unit suite prices venues that do not exist, so the provenance gate and the weight read collapse a surface eleven constructor sites do not describe — mock venues become real contracts at equal weights, preserving all nineteen existing assertions, with the unequal-weight discriminators added deliberately alongside (2026-08-03; PB3.8i9)

**(i) The unit suite's venues are codeless addresses, and the surface worked only because nothing ever addressed them.** `test/unit/TVLOracle.t.sol` builds every venue as a `makeAddr` address through its `_addr` helper and registers it into the `MockVaultExplorer` imported from `test/fork/mocks/StageHMocks.sol`, which stores tokens, balances and an initialized flag and answers `getPoolTokens`, `getPoolData` and `isPoolInitialized` on the venue's behalf. No contract exists at any venue address. That was invisible while `_venueRatio` read only through the explorer — every question about a venue was answered by a different object, and the venue itself was never more than a mapping key.

**(ii) Two calls in the corrected `_venueRatio` address the venue directly, and both fail against an address holding no code.** `IWeightedPool(v).getNormalizedWeights()` is a high-level call whose return decoding requires code at the target, so it reverts; `IBasePoolFactory(approvedFactory).isPoolFromFactory(v)` returns false for an address no factory created, so the venue is skipped before the weight read is attempted at all. Either way the venue contributes nothing, and the gate happens to convert the revert into a skip — which makes the failure quiet rather than loud, and turns nineteen concrete assertions into assertions against zero. The general shape is worth naming: a mock that answers ON BEHALF OF an object lets an entire test surface run without the object existing, and that holds exactly until the code under test asks the object something itself.

**(iii) PB-D53 (vii)'s count is correct and incomplete, and the distinction matters to whoever reads it next.** Eleven construction sites is the true number of places the constructor signature must be threaded, and that half is mechanical. What it does not cover is the venue surface those constructors serve: `test/unit/TVLOracle.t.sol` carries 55 tests, of which 11 stand a venue up through the explorer's `setPool` or through `addConstellationPool`, and 19 assert a concrete `tvl()` value. Sizing the constructor ripple at pre-flight per I13 was right and did not go far enough — I13 is about a gate in a shared evaluator intercepting a whole path, and the path here is every test that prices anything.

**(iv) Mock venues are built at EQUAL weights, which preserves all nineteen assertions without recomputing one of them.** With `wBase` and `wQuote` equal the correction factor is exactly one, so every existing expected value is still the value the corrected estimator produces — those assertions were always implicitly equal-weight, having been written against a bare balance ratio. Recomputing nineteen expectations against arbitrary unequal weights would change what each test asserts while proving nothing, and would obscure the diff at precisely the moment it most needs to be readable.

**(v) Preserving equal-weight mocks is the PB-D50 (v) trap unless the unequal-weight cases are added in the same breath, so they are.** That clause diagnosed the original defect's invisibility exactly: an equal-weight mock makes the weight ratio one, the bias evaluates away, and the lemma reads as true. Keeping those mocks for continuity and adding nothing else would rebuild the same blindness around the fix rather than around the defect. The suite therefore gains venues at deliberately unequal weights whose expected values a bare balance ratio cannot produce — the equal-weight cases prove no regression, and only the unequal-weight cases prove the fix.

**(vi) The mock surface and the new test roster.** Two doubles are added beside `MockVaultExplorer` in `test/fork/mocks/StageHMocks.sol`, its existing home and already the unit suite's import source: a settable-weights weighted-pool double answering `getNormalizedWeights`, and a settable-membership factory double answering `isPoolFromFactory`. Five tests follow — an unequal-weight two-token venue proving the weight term applies at all; the PB-D53 (iii) discriminator, a venue holding two tokens that resolve to ONE underlying at unequal weights, whose expected value the single-leg reading cannot produce and which the genesis roster provably cannot supply; an unprovenanced venue proving the skip; a short weight array proving the PB-D53 (iv) skip; and a zero-weight leg proving the PB-D53 (v) skip rather than a `Panic(0x12)`.

**(vii) The re-scoped ladder, and what stays unmeasured until its own rung.** i9a the two mock doubles; i9b the eleven-site constructor thread, which needs the factory double to exist first; i9c the eleven venue-setup sites rebuilt on real mock pools at equal weights; i9d the five new tests; i9e the unit re-gate. The three non-unit construction sites are NOT sized here and are inspected at their own rung rather than assumed — `test/fork/StageHIntegration.t.sol` and `test/fork/StageIIntegration.t.sol` construct no `MockVaultExplorer` at all while `test/whitehat/F19_uninitializedVenueLiveness.t.sol` constructs four, so venue provenance differs between them and from the unit suite. The fork and formal re-gate, the PB-D52 (viii) Sepolia-fork evidence test and the PB-D52 (ix) record statements all follow unchanged behind this ladder.

## PB-D55 — PB3.8i9b3 pre-flight: der Bodensee and the Miliarium pools are created by different factories wherever the two factory env keys diverge, so the PB-D52 (ii) provenance gate exempts `BODENSEE_POOL` on the structural-provenance ground PB-D52 (vi) already established — routing der Bodensee through the Aureum factory is arithmetically viable and is rejected as a category error (2026-08-03; PB3.8i9b3)

**(i) The finding, and where it is visible.** `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` L153 creates `bodenseePool` through `wpf`, the upstream `WeightedPoolFactory`, while L165 sets `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` to the separately constructed `awpf` and L171 through L189 create the three pilot pools through `DeployIxHelvetia().run()` and its siblings, each a `MiliariumPoolDeployer` reading that key at `script/pools/deploy-miliarium-pool.s.sol` L91. Two factories, two disjoint sets of venues, and no single `approvedFactory` value that vouches for both — passing `awpf` skips der Bodensee, passing `wpf` skips all twenty-six Miliarium venues, and either way the PB-D52 (iii) skip quietly removes legitimate pools from the pricing mean without reverting and without emitting.

**(ii) Why PB-D52 (v) verified the opposite, and the pattern this completes.** That clause checked satisfiability against live Sepolia, where `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` and `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` both resolve to `0xC60E426294a06Fb95a6C1BB7A06219f794AAde8F` — one address, because `script/DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory.s.sol` L39 constructs an upstream instance per RB-008 and PB-D27 keeps Sepolia on the upstream factory. On that chain the claim is true and every venue passes. In the fork fixtures, and on mainnet once PB-D27 (ix)'s env-key split lands as its own named Stage-R gate, the two are distinct contracts and the claim is false. This is the third finding in this stage resting on a coincidence local to the current chain, after PB-D51's every-price-equal-to-one and PB-D53 (iii)'s one-token-per-underlying, and it is the RB-015 degenerate-parameter class applied to an address rather than to a number.

**(iii) Creating der Bodensee through the Aureum factory is arithmetically viable and is REJECTED on taxonomy.** The gate at `src/factory/AureumWeightedPoolFactory.sol` L92 through L100 sums `normalizedWeights` over slots that are `WITH_RATE` carrying a non-zero rate provider and requires at least `MIN_ERC4626_WEIGHT`, which is `52e16`; `script/DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` assigns AuMM `4e17` as a `STANDARD` slot with a zero rate provider and the other two slots `3e17` each as `WITH_RATE` with resolved providers, so the sum is `6e17` and der Bodensee would clear the gate with eight points of headroom. It is rejected regardless, because that factory exists to enforce a Miliarium quality bar and der Bodensee is not a Miliarium pool — PB-D39 (ii) settled the taxonomy as the twenty-eight ix-named registry slots against the single non-emission-eligible revenue destination, with no third category — so routing it there would subject a composition fixed by F-11 at forty percent AuMM to a constraint written for gauge-eligible pools, and the eight-point margin becomes a silent coupling that any future rebalance past forty-eight percent AuMM would break for a reason having nothing to do with what the pool is for. That is PB-D39's own category error in a second register, the slot salt there and the creating factory here.

**(iv) The two remaining alternatives, and why neither is taken.** A governance-curated approved-factory set would mirror the append-only `_hopUnderlyings` and `_governanceAddedPools` rosters this contract already carries and would survive a third factory, but it adds a governance surface and a setter to audited emission source before the audit, which is the PB-D13 cost PB-D50 (x) already books once and should not book twice for what is currently a two-element problem. A second immutable naming the Bodensee factory is minimal and hard-codes a two-factory world into the constructor, buying nothing the exemption does not buy while making the eventual Stage-R consolidation harder to unwind. Both are rejected — the first for pre-audit surface growth, the second for encoding an accident of the present deployment as a permanent constructor parameter.

**(v) Disposition: `_venueRatio` skips the provenance check for `BODENSEE_POOL`, and PB-D52 (vi) already wrote the justification.** That clause held that `IncendiaryRegistry` L360 may read der Bodensee's weights unguarded because `BODENSEE_POOL` is an immutable sealed at construction to a known factory-created pool, its provenance structural, and a gate there would assert what the deployment already guarantees. The oracle holds `BODENSEE_POOL` on exactly those terms, a zero-checked constructor immutable, so the reasoning applies here without extension — the same object, trusted for the same reason, in a second contract. What the exemption does not weaken is the threat model that motivated the gate: `_venueRatio` walks a governance-extensible roster of arbitrary addresses and every one of them stays gated, while the single exempted address cannot be changed after construction by governance or by anyone else.

**(vi) Three Stage-R gates now sit on der Bodensee's creation and are one decision wearing three hats.** PB-D39 (v) requires `BODENSEE_SALT` to move outside the one-to-twenty-eight Miliarium slot space before the first mainnet broadcast; PB-D27 (ix) and RB-008 require the overloaded `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` key to split from `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY`; and this lock adds the choice of creating factory, which the key split is what makes consequential for the first time. They interact rather than merely coexist — the split is what turns one factory into two, and the salt namespace and the factory namespace are both consequences of the same fact that der Bodensee is not a Miliarium pool — so Stage R adjudicates them together as a single der-Bodensee-creation decision rather than as three independent rows resolved in isolation.

**(vii) Scope, and the ladder.** The code change is one condition inside `_venueRatio` and nothing else — no constructor change, no new state, no new error, and no NatSpec beyond the line documenting the exemption — while PB-D53's weight term, both skip guards, the gate ordering and the arithmetic all stand exactly as they landed at PB3.8i8. Live Sepolia needs no action, since one factory created all twenty-seven venues there and the exemption never fires. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row, then the one-condition amendment, then PB3.8i9b3 threading `awpf` as the factory argument in the two fork fixtures and a `MockBasePoolFactory` in the F-19 whitehat test, then PB3.8i9c, i9d and i9e as PB-D54 (vii) sequences them, with the unit re-gate gaining a case asserting that a venue equal to `BODENSEE_POOL` prices without any factory membership.

## PB-D56 — PB3.8i9e: the unit re-gate is green at 957 and PB-D54 (iv)'s nineteen-preserved claim is corrected by measurement to eighteen preserved and one corrected — the correction is the fix's strongest evidence rather than a weakening of the claim, because the wrong implementation would have required no correction at all (2026-08-03; PB3.8i9e)

**(i) The measured result.** The first build since the PB3.8i8 constructor change compiles clean, and the Part-A unit suite runs 957 tests across 57 suites with zero failures and zero skips — 947 at the PB-D34 gate, plus four for `test/unit/BytecodeSize.t.sol` at PB3.5i6, plus exactly six from this ladder: one constructor guard at i9b2, two discriminators at i9d2 and three skip guards at i9d3. `test/unit/TVLOracle.t.sol` is 61 of 61 and moved 55 to 61 over those three sub-steps, `test/whitehat/F19_uninitializedVenueLiveness.t.sol` is 4 of 4, and the PB-D37 bytecode-size gate is 4 of 4, confirming `TVLOracle` still clears EIP-170 after gaining an immutable and three statements.

**(ii) PB-D54 (iv) is corrected by measurement, not by argument.** That clause states the equal-weight mock convention "preserves all nineteen assertions without recomputing one of them". The measured figure is eighteen preserved and one corrected: `test_tvl_venueMultiTokenSummingPerUnderlying` moved from `100e18` to `200e18` at PB3.8i9d1. The pre-flight that found it was mechanical rather than lucky — only two arrays in the file are built at length three, one of them the constructor seed test, so exactly one venue in the suite could carry an asymmetric leg count, and that venue is the one that moved. Every other venue holds one base leg against one quote leg, where `wBase` equals `wQuote` and the weight term is exactly one.

**(iii) The correction is the fix working, and a wrong build would have produced no correction at all.** This is the part worth recording. Under the equal-thirds weights the rebuilt helper assigns, a single-leg implementation — one taking the first matching token's weight rather than the sum over the matching set — yields `wBase` equal to `wQuote` in that same venue, leaves the ratio at `2e18`, and passes the original `100e18` assertion untouched and silently. The correct lockstep implementation yields `wBase` of `666666666666666666` against `wQuote` of `333333333333333333`, exactly two, and forces the assertion to `200e18`. So the obligation to move exactly one assertion, in exactly the venue carrying two base legs, to exactly the value the parity relation predicts, is positive evidence that the aggregation is real; the plausible wrong build is the one that would have sailed through unchanged.

**(iv) What the five new tests establish, stated so a later reader need not re-derive it.** `test_tvl_venueUnequalWeights_appliesWeightTerm` proves the term applies at all, at `400e18` where the pre-fix bare ratio gives `100e18`. `test_tvl_venueAggregatesWeightsInLockstep` is the PB-D53 (iii) discriminator the genesis roster provably cannot supply, and separates correct aggregation at `400e18` from the plausible single-leg reading at `250e18`. The three skip guards each hold a venue that demonstrably prices at `400e18` and then change exactly one thing — provenance revoked, the weight vector shortened, the quote leg's weight zeroed — so a passing zero proves the guard fired rather than proving an unrelated absence. The zero-weight case targets the QUOTE leg deliberately, since the divisor is `balBase` times `wQuote` and that is the input which panics at `0x12` without the PB-D53 (v) guard.

**(v) What this lock does not cover, and what remains.** The unit surface only: the fork re-gate, the formal surfaces under `formal/` that assert the old ratio per PB-D50 (x), the PB-D52 (viii) Sepolia-fork evidence test expecting ixHelvetia at exactly 14,000 and the constellation near 987,000, and the PB-D52 (ix) deployment-record and section 11 statements are all still open and unmeasured. Two pre-existing `erc20-unchecked-transfer` lint warnings at `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol` L669 and L683 were observed in this build, are of the RB-004 and RB-005 class, predate this ladder and are recorded here as an observation rather than an obligation.

## PB-D57 — PB3.8i9g: the provenance gate is too narrow by one factory and the mainnet-fork suite proved it — a constellation venue is a PRICING SOURCE rather than an Aureum-owned asset, so trust extends to both weighted-pool factories this system already runs, and the PB-D55 `BODENSEE_POOL` exemption retires into the general rule (user-adjudicated 2026-08-03; PB3.8i9g)

**(i) The failure and its exact mechanism.** `test_Fork_TwoHop_UsdcPricesViaUsdsHop_PropagatesToEMA` in `test/fork/StageHIntegration.t.sol` fails at `USDC priced via USDS hop post-seed: 0 <= 0`, and the cause is one line of its own fixture: `hopVenue` is created by `wpf.create(...)`, the canonical upstream `WeightedPoolFactory`, and then registered through `addConstellationPool`. The oracle in that fixture holds `awpf` as `approvedFactory` per PB3.8i9b3, so `isPoolFromFactory` returns false, `_venueRatio` skips the venue at the PB-D52 (ii) gate, and `_directRatio(usdcUnderlying, usds)` — the `h1` leg of the two-hop path — returns zero with no revert and no event. Eleven of twelve tests in that file pass, `StageIIntegration` is 4 of 4 and the P10 close gate holds at 13 of 13, so the defect is exactly one condition wide.

**(ii) Why the unit suite could not see it, and why the assertion above it made things worse.** `_setComposition` and `_setCompositionWeighted` both call `mockFactory.setFromFactory(target, true)`, so every unit venue is vouched for by construction — the suite can prove the gate FIRES, which PB3.8i9d3 does, but not that it fires ONLY when it should. The fork fixture's own preceding assertion then masked the shape: `tvl(hopVenue)` returns non-zero pre-seed because that call reads `hopVenue`'s balances and prices them through der Bodensee, which takes the PB-D55 exemption. A passing liveness check on the exempt pool sat two lines above a failing check on the general case.

**(iii) The generalization, which matters more than the fixture.** A constellation venue is a PRICING SOURCE and not an Aureum-owned asset — the roster exists to name pools with enough real liquidity to price against, and the natural mainnet case is a deep third-party Balancer pool added for exactly that reason. Gating pricing venues on Aureum-factory provenance conflates two different decisions: admission, where `GaugeEligibility` is right to demand the Aureum factory because a Miliarium pool is Aureum's own, and pricing, where the only question is whether the venue is a genuine weighted pool reporting honest weights. F-12's threat is a HAND-ROLLED contract returning a fabricated vector, not a pool from Balancer's own canonical factory, so PB-D52 (ii) was under-specified rather than wrong.

**(iv) PB-D55's exemption was a special case of this same defect and is RETIRED rather than kept alongside the fix.** der Bodensee is `wpf`-created in the fixture at `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` L153 and is created from the same `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` key at `script/DeployDerBodensee.s.sol` L87, so once the canonical factory is trusted der Bodensee passes on the general rule and the `v != BODENSEE_POOL` short-circuit asserts nothing the gate does not already grant. Keeping both would leave two mechanisms answering one question, and the special case is the one that hides the next instance — which is precisely how this defect survived PB-D55 in the first place. PB-D55's finding and its rejection of a single-factory model stand as recorded history; only its remedy is superseded.

**(v) The mechanism: two constructor immutables and a disjunctive gate.** `approvedFactory` continues to hold `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY`, which per the E-D16 naming trap recorded at `script/DeployStageG.s.sol` L62 is the CANONICAL upstream Balancer factory, and a second immutable holds `AUREUM_WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY`. A venue passes when either factory claims it. Both keys already exist in every environment set and both are already read by scripts in this tree, and neither introduces a setter, a governance surface or a one-shot seal — which is the whole reason this shape is preferred to a curated set. On Sepolia the two keys resolve to one address per PB-D55 (iii), so the disjunction is a no-op there and costs one call; on mainnet a Miliarium pool is claimed by the Aureum factory and der Bodensee by the canonical one.

**(vi) What is rejected, and on what ground.** A governance-curated factory set remains the more general answer and remains rejected FOR NOW on the PB-D13 ground PB-D50 (x) already books — it adds a setter to audited emission source before audit, and a two-element disjunction covers every factory this system actually runs; it reopens if a third factory ever appears. Exempting governance-roster-added venues while gating the registry leg is rejected as gating the wrong half, since the Miliarium registry is Aureum-only by construction and needs the check least. Editing the fixture to create `hopVenue` through `awpf` is rejected outright, because it would fix the evidence rather than the design, and is the same anti-pattern PB-D47 declined when it refused to edit a config file mainnet also reads in order to work around a testnet defect.

**(vii) Blast radius and the ladder.** The constructor gains a seventh address parameter, so the eleven-site ripple runs again — `script/DeployStageF.s.sol` gains a second `vm.envAddress` read and a ninth documented env key, the seven `test/unit/TVLOracle.t.sol` sites and the `F19` site pass the mock factory in both slots, and the two fork fixtures pass `wpf` and `awpf` in their true roles rather than `awpf` alone. Ladder: this lock, then the plan Decisions row, then the `_venueRatio` amendment carrying the second immutable and the disjunctive gate and the PB-D55 retirement together in one save, then the deploy-script read, then the constructor ripple, then a unit re-gate expecting 957 again, then this fork gate re-run expecting 12 of 12 in `StageHIntegration` with `StageIIntegration` and the P10 gate unmoved.

## PB-D58 — PB3.8i9j: the PB-D57 re-gate is green at every level, and the two-hop mainnet-fork failure it was written to close is closed — `test_Fork_TwoHop_UsdcPricesViaUsdsHop_PropagatesToEMA` passes, `StageHIntegration` reads 12 of 12, and the P10 close gate holds at 13 of 13 unmoved (2026-08-03; PB3.8i9j)

**(i) The measured result, against PB-D57's own prediction.** PB-D57 (vii) predicted a unit re-gate at 957, `StageHIntegration` at 12 of 12, `StageIIntegration` and the P10 gate unmoved. The actual unit count is 959, the two higher than predicted because PB-D57's own build added a seventh negative-construction test and a disjunction-acceptance test that were sized after the lock was written — `test_constructor_revert_zeroApprovedFactoryAureum` at PB3.8i9i2 and `test_tvl_venueFromSecondFactoryOnly_isAccepted` at PB3.8i9i4. Every other prediction held exactly: `StageHIntegration` reads 12 of 12 with `test_Fork_TwoHop_UsdcPricesViaUsdsHop_PropagatesToEMA` now passing, `StageIIntegration` holds at 4 of 4, and the P10 close gate holds at 13 of 13.

**(ii) What the P10 result specifically proves.** That gate holds every mainnet-fork integration point this stage has ever validated, including der Bodensee's pricing path, and it did not move by one test. That is the direct confirmation of PB-D57 (iv)'s retirement argument: der Bodensee, created from `WEIGHTED_POOL_FACTORY` in the real deploy script, passes the disjunctive gate on the general rule with no special case, and nothing downstream of `TVLOracle.tvl()` for der Bodensee regressed when the `BODENSEE_POOL` exemption was removed.

**(iii) The defect this closes, restated once for the record.** `hopVenue` in the two-hop fork fixture is created through `wpf`, the canonical upstream factory, and was skipped entirely when `_venueRatio` trusted only `awpf`. With both factories trusted disjunctively, the venue is claimed on the first operand and the `h1` leg of the two-hop path now returns a positive ratio rather than the zero that produced the original `0 <= 0` failure. This is the closing measurement for the chain of findings running PB-D50 through PB-D57: the weight term, the provenance gate, the aggregation lockstep, and now the gate's breadth are each proven by a test built to fail against the specific wrong shape rather than merely to pass.

**(iv) What remains, unchanged from PB-D52 and PB-D56.** The `formal/` surfaces asserting the pre-PB-D50 ratio, the PB-D52 (viii) Sepolia-fork evidence test expecting ixHelvetia at exactly 14,000 and the constellation near 987,000, and the PB-D52 (ix) deployment-record and section 11 statements are all still open. Live Sepolia was deployed before any of this ladder and still carries the original biased, single-factory oracle; nothing in this re-gate touches that state, since PB-D52 (viii) already disposed against a phase-4 re-broadcast.

## PB-D59 — PB3.8i10: the PB-D52 (viii) evidence test passes 3 of 3 against live Sepolia state — the corrected oracle reads ixHelvetia at exactly 14,000 where the live pre-fix oracle still reads 14,933.33, same fork, same block, same pools, one variable (2026-08-03; PB3.8i10)

**(i) The measurement.** `test/fork/PB52SepoliaOracleEvidence.t.sol` forks Sepolia at block 11410000, above the 11405532 that closed the rung-i2 seeding and below the 11419232 head at authoring time, stands up a corrected `TVLOracle` in process, re-binds only that instance's own governance state, and reads it beside the live oracle at `0xBf78A49b1AB86247198f6660Bf5a0491007E6A64`. Three assertions, all passing: the live oracle still returns `14933333333333333332400` for ixHelvetia; the corrected oracle returns exactly `14_000e18` for the same pool at the same block; and across all 26 registry-enumerated pools the corrected sum falls far below the biased total PB-D50 (i) recorded at 1,271,468. Nothing was re-capitalised and no seeding transaction was replayed.

**(ii) Why the premise assertion is first, and what it buys.** Asserting the LIVE reading before asserting the corrected one is what makes the second assertion mean anything: it establishes that the fork carries the state PB-D50 was measured against, so a passing 14,000 is the estimator changing rather than the pool state differing. That live figure reproduces PB-D50 (i)'s closed-form derivation to the repeating digit, 2,800 times the biased 4/3 cross-venue mean plus 11,200, and it was re-read from chain by `cast` before the test was written rather than trusted from the lock. The corrected 14,000 is exact with no truncation residue, because ixHelvetia's own venue contributes 11,200 times 0.20 over 2,800 times 0.80, which is 1.0, and each of the eight equal-weight Standard venues holding both sUSDS and svZCHF also contributes 1.0.

**(iii) Two discoveries removed a prerequisite that looked mandatory.** The test needs `setMiliariumRegistry` bound, since Leg 2 of `_directRatio` is what enumerates the 26 pools as venues per PB-D41 (iii), and `.env.sepolia` carries none of the sixteen phase-4 addresses but `TVL_ORACLE`. That gap is PB3.7's, and it was closed at PB3.7a by `script/config/SepoliaPhase4Addresses.sol`, a committed named-constant library on the `GaugeGenesisManifest.sol` shape rather than `SepoliaTokenUnderlyings.sol`'s anonymous one, because PB-D44 (ii)'s refusal of names applies to stubs whose only identity is an address and inverts where the record names each contract. Separately, identifying ixHelvetia looked to require a committed slot-keyed pool table, since PB-D46 records the dense enumeration as carrying no slot identity: that is true of `IMiliariumRegistry`, the Stage-F surface the oracle consumes, but the concrete `MiliariumRegistry` also implements `IMiliariumSlotRegistry` with `poolAtSlot` over a 28-entry array, so the test resolves slot 1 on chain and no PB3.7b rung was needed.

**(iv) The tree's first Sepolia-fork test, and the operational posture it forces.** Every other file under `test/fork/` forks mainnet. This one must be invoked file-scoped with `--fork-url sepolia` and requires `.env` to be the Sepolia canonical set, which means the fork gate is now two passes with `cp .env.sepolia .env` and `cp .env.mainnet .env` between them, always canonical to working copy and never the reverse. It lives under `test/fork/` deliberately rather than in a sibling directory: D35's unit invocation excludes `test/fork/**`, so a new top-level directory would have been swept INTO the unit pass and failed there for want of a fork URL. The env must be returned to mainnet after any Sepolia run or every mainnet fixture silently resolves its literals to codeless addresses, which is PB-D35 exactly.

**(v) What this closes and what it does not.** PB-D52 (viii) is discharged: the corrected oracle is proven against real seeded state, which was the condition on which PB-D52 declined the ten-contract redeploy cascade. That disposition is unchanged and now carries its evidence, so live Sepolia keeps the biased oracle until Stage R by decision rather than by omission. Still open: the `formal/` surfaces asserting the pre-PB-D50 ratio per PB-D50 (x), the PB-D52 (ix) deployment-record and section 11 statements disclosing the live oracle as pre-fix, the `STAGE_P_BIS_PLAN.md` Completion Log unlogged since PB3.4f2, runbook phases 5 and 6, and PB3.6 with the remainder of PB3.7.

## PB-D60 — PB3.8i12: the formal re-gate is 55 of 55, zero rework, because no hevm or Act surface asserts the venue ratio PB-D50 changed — the last unmeasured surface of the PB-D50 through PB-D59 chain is now closed (2026-08-03; PB3.8i12)

**(i) The measurement.** `forge clean && forge build --ast` followed by `hevm test --match prove_ --solver bitwuzla --smt-timeout 300 --max-iterations 10 --root .`, the PB-D17 invocation verbatim, discharges all 55 obligations: 7 in `AuthorizerProofs.sol`, 9 in `CCBEngineProofs.sol`, 30 in `EmissionProofs.sol`, 9 in `FeeRoutingHookProofs.sol`. Zero fail, zero warn, zero partial-exploration, matching the PB-D17 original attestation and the PB-D18 g3 re-attestation after the CCB rebuild count for count.

**(ii) Pre-flight found the re-gate narrower than PB-D50 (x) anticipated, and the finding is the point.** That clause named "any hevm or Act surface asserting the old ratio" as in scope. None exists. `CCBEngineProofs.sol` proves `EMASampler` against a `MockTVLOracleProof` that is deliberately not `is ITVLOracle`, since the harness's own comment states the mock exists because "EMASampler only calls `tvl()`" — the proofs treat the oracle as an opaque `uint256` source and assert nothing about how that value is computed. `ema_sampler.act` states the same boundary formally: `updateEMA` is recorded as RESIDUAL, "Not modelled as a transition" precisely because its body makes "an external call into the multi-venue oracle... outside the single-contract fragment", with the behavioural contract taking `spot := TVL_ORACLE.tvl(pool)` as a free variable rather than a defined one.

**(iii) That boundary is why 55/55 required no source change to reach.** The weight term, the provenance gate, and the disjunctive factory check all changed what `tvl()` returns; none of them changed the fact that it returns a `uint256`, which is the entirety of what the formal surfaces depend on. No harness constructs a `TVLOracle`, so none was touched by the eleven-site constructor ripple that ran twice across PB3.8i9 and PB3.8i9i, and no `.act` spec required amendment alongside `ccb_multiplier.act`'s PB-D18 g2 rewrite. The re-gate is confirmation that this architectural separation held, not a rework of the proofs.

**(iv) What this closes.** The chain of typed-domain questions and their measurements running PB-D50 through PB-D59 is now closed end to end: the weight term (PB-D50, PB-D53), the provenance gate and its breadth (PB-D52, PB-D57), the mock-venue pre-flight and its measured correction (PB-D54, PB-D56), the two-factory disjunction (PB-D57, PB-D58), the Sepolia-fork evidence against real seeded state (PB-D59), and now the formal surfaces, which needed nothing because they were never coupled to the venue-ratio internals in the first place. Nothing remains open from this chain. The live TVLOracle at `0xBf78A49b1AB86247198f6660Bf5a0491007E6A64` stays pre-fix by the PB-D52 (viii) decision, disclosed at PB3.8i11.

### PB18 — a count is evidence only if the baseline and the delta were measured in the same units: `grep -c` counts matching lines while `grep -o` piped to `wc -l` counts occurrences, and mixing the two yields a wrong number carrying the full authority of tool output

Caught twice over, first as a habit and then as arithmetic. The habit face ran four times across the PB3.8i session: an em-dash tally for a §8e.1 verify assertion was read off the drafted payload by eye, was wrong every time, and each miss spent a fix round on a save that was in fact correct. The adopted repair was to stop estimating and let a tool count the payload, which is what the `CLAUDE.md` L507 index bullet prescribes and what PB3.8k duly did — `grep -o` piped to `wc -l` over the exact insert text, returning 3, correct. The prediction was still wrong. A file total of 202 was asserted against an actual 200, because the baseline that 3 was added to had come from `grep -c "—" CLAUDE.md`, and `grep -c` reports the number of matching LINES rather than the number of matches: many bullets in that index carry two or three em-dashes apiece, so the file's 199 was never a dash count at all. Under that metric a one-line insertion moves the number by at most one whatever the line contains, and 200 was the only answer the arithmetic could have produced. Both measurements were individually correct and their combination was nonsense, which is the part worth keeping — a prediction assembled from two tool outputs under an unexamined assumption that they share units is more dangerous than an eyeball estimate, because it arrives carrying the authority of having been measured, and nothing in the output of either command announces the mismatch. The structural cost is that a wrong number in a USER VERIFY block manufactures a false fix verdict: the audit cycle earns its keep only while a mismatch means the save is wrong, and a drafter-side arithmetic error inverts that, spending a round on a correct file and teaching the reader to discount the next sentinel — the same erosion §12's blocker-classification note describes, arriving through a different door. The rule is that a one-line insertion asserts `wc -l` and the `git diff --stat` insertion count, both exact and unit-unambiguous, and that a character-level sentinel is measured with the identical command on both sides, `grep -o` piped to `wc -l` for the baseline as well as for the payload, or is not asserted at all. PB14 settled when the sentinel runs, against the drafted payload before handoff rather than only against the saved file after; this settles what it counts. It is also the mirror of PB15, PB16 and PB17, where in each case a passing outcome was mistaken for a passing check because nobody asked what the command had actually examined: here a failing comparison was nearly mistaken for a failing save, for precisely the same reason. The index bullet needs no amendment, because it already names the occurrence-counting form rather than the one that missed.

## PB-D61 — PB3.8n: the fee-routing hook's `governanceModule` is a one-shot binding that burns its own admin, so `AureumGovernance` is structurally disqualified as its target on every chain — a Stage-R gate, not a Sepolia artifact (2026-08-05; PB3.8n)

**(i) What the phase 5 execution established.** Seating the canonical Router required `setGovernanceModule` before `setTrustedRouter`, and the first of those is irreversible by construction. `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` L265-L273 assigns `governanceModule` and zeroes `_governanceAdmin` in the same call under the C-D11 two-flag lock, and either flag alone rejects every subsequent attempt: a non-zero `governanceModule` reverts `GovernanceModuleAlreadySet`, a zeroed `_governanceAdmin` reverts `NotGovernanceAdmin`. There is exactly one call, ever, per deployment of the hook. No rotation path exists anywhere in the system — PB2.6's `DissolutionGovernanceHandoff` rotates five authorities and this is not among them, and could not be, which PB-D11 already establishes as the shape one-shot seals take.

**(ii) `AureumGovernance` cannot reach either function it would own.** The hook holds exactly two `governanceModule`-gated entries, `setTrustedRouter` at L311-L314 and `routeGovernanceDeposit` at L660-L668. `AureumGovernance.execute()` at `src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol` L343-L374 dispatches over three proposal types and reaches three targets — `GAUGE_REGISTRY.revokeGauge`, the `SLOT_REGISTRY.replaceSlot` composition path, and `VAULT.setStaticSwapFeePercentage` — with no arbitrary-call path, no generic target field, and no calldata parameter anywhere in the proposal struct. Pointing the hook's module at that contract would therefore not delegate those two levers to governance; it would delete them, permanently, on a hook every Miliarium pool is bound to as its emissions recorder. The failure is total and silent at seat time: the transaction succeeds, emits `GovernanceModuleSet`, and the loss is observable only when someone later needs a router seated or a governance deposit routed and finds no caller on earth who can do it.

**(iii) The constraint is chain-independent, which is what makes it a Stage-R gate.** Nothing above turns on Sepolia. The two-flag lock, the two gated entries and `execute()`'s three targets are identical in the source that Stage R will deploy, so the mainnet hook likewise admits exactly one `setGovernanceModule` call and the same target is likewise disqualified. What makes it worth locking rather than leaving as deployment narrative is that no document currently states the rule prospectively. CLAUDE.md section 2's governance row speaks to the Vault's Authorizer, which does migrate to on-chain governance at Stage K by an explicit `setAuthorizer` CALL; the hook's `governanceModule` is a different slot with the opposite lifecycle and appears in no locked decision. A Stage-R author reading only the migration posture, and reasoning by analogy from a row that is correct about a different slot, would reach precisely the wrong target. PB-D12 (ii) attests the adjacent fact from the dissolution side, that the operational-wiring family reverts under `AureumGovernance` v1, but it does not name this slot or its irreversibility.

**(iv) Sepolia is seated correctly and needs no remediation.** `governanceModule` on the live hook at `0x954c972170eC131364570658D42F14e833A08588` is the deployer EOA `0xA851478dbee97375E784e9b98c0D7D599662bF85`, which is also `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` on this chain per PB-D23 (iv), sent at nonce 690 and recorded in deployment-record section 14. That is the runbook's own prescription and it is the correct one, for a reason stronger than the runbook gave: it is the only target that leaves both levers operable. The authority was proven before the send by running the exact transaction as an `eth_call` from the deployer rather than by deriving the private `_governanceAdmin` slot, which exercises every guard in the function rather than one of them.

**(v) The Stage-R obligation, and its register row.** Mainnet must seat the hook's `governanceModule` at the governance Safe multisig, once, as an explicit operator step with a pre-send simulation and a post-send state read, and must never seat it at `AureumGovernance` or at any contract lacking a call path to both gated entries. Recorded as RB-017 under a new Irreversible-authority binding hardening class, whose general form is the part worth carrying past this instance: a one-shot setter that burns its own admin converts a configuration mistake into a permanent capability loss, so its target belongs in a locked decision and its transaction belongs in a runbook with a simulation ahead of it, rather than being inferred at broadcast time from a document written before the alternatives existed. The candidate this rule excludes did not exist when the runbook's phase 5 text was drafted at PB3.5; it existed by the time that text was executed.

## PB-D62 — PB3.8q: F-21, the vacuous quorum denominator that lets a CompositionChallenge capture a Miliarium slot with zero votes and no possible opposition; severity High, disposition FIX (2026-08-05; PB3.8q)

**(i) What the PB3.8p PoC established, and what its framing understates.** The PoC drives a `CompositionChallenge` to slot capture with zero votes cast, against a same-fixture control in which a `GaugeChallenge` on identical state defeats. The arithmetic at `src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol` L310-L317 is that a zero snapshot denominator makes the quorum test `0 * 10_000 < 0 * 2_000`, which is false, so the guard does not fire and quorum passes vacuously; the branches then diverge only on strictness, `forVotes * 3 >= totalVotes * 2` yielding true at `0 >= 0` where `forVotes > againstVotes` yields false at `0 > 0`. The divergence is the symptom and not the defect. The defect is the vacuous denominator, and the Gauge and Fee branches are safe only accidentally: their safety rests on `VotingWeight` L24's invariant that `_totalQualifiedWeight` equals the sum of `_holderWeight[*]`, a property of a different contract, rather than on any check local to the tally.

**(ii) The window is unconditional rather than opportunistic, which is what separates this from F-01.** `VotingWeight._positionPower` L163-L172 short-circuits to zero unless the pool's TVL EMA has been seeding for at least `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS`, 432,000 blocks or 60 days. Before that boundary every holder in every pool reads zero power, so `poke` computes a `newWeight` equal to its `oldWeight` of zero, takes the no-op short-circuit at L143, and never pushes a checkpoint. `_totalQualifiedWeightHistory` therefore stays empty and `getPastTotalSupply` returns zero for every block in the window. Zero supply during the first 60 days after EMA seeding is not a state an operator can fall into or avoid; it is the launch condition of every chain the protocol is deployed to.
